Adversarial review of the provided diff only (truncated tail omitted per instructions).

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py` (~3232–3270, `_perform_with_drain2`)  
**ISSUE:** Ungated triggers call `switcher.switch_to` before any session-quiet check; activity is measured only afterward for labeling.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** For triggers not in `_gated_triggers()` (comment: at-limit, failover, proactive when `switchUnderLoad`), a full transcript tree can be skipped pre-switch. An active Claude Code session can still be mid-request when the OAuth slot changes → failed requests, wrong account, or corrupted in-flight work—exactly what the voluntary gate was meant to prevent, traded for latency.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Keep “swap first, scan later” only where policy explicitly opts in; for at-limit/failover, either retain a cheap pre-check (`stop_at=now - ε`) or document/enforce that `switchUnderLoad` is the only path that skips the gate and default it off.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py` (~1488–1496, `_pre_freshen`)  
**ISSUE:** Broad `except Exception` around `_freshen_target` and `_quarantine`; failures log at WARNING and the tick continues.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** If `_quarantine` fails (disk full, state lock, corrupt state file) after `identity-conflict` / `invalid_grant`, the bad slot stays in rotation. Pre-freshen was meant to catch that off the critical path; a silent quarantine miss surfaces again at switch time under time pressure.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Quarantine in its own `try/except`; on quarantine failure, emit a high-signal event (or re-raise) and exclude the slot from `pending` for this process lifetime.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py` (~1483–1484, `_pre_freshen`)  
**ISSUE:** `_prefreshened[num] = now` is set before `_freshen_target` completes.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A slow/hung refresh or repeated transient errors still consume the 120s throttle slot. Near expiry, pre-freshen may not retry until switch-time freshen—reintroducing the latency this feature targets when the network is flaky (not merely “unexpected” exceptions).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Set `_prefreshened[num]` only after a terminal freshen outcome (ok / identity-conflict / invalid_grant / transient), or use separate backoff for failures vs success.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py` (~1238–1239 engine state + ~3630 drain gate; `_latest_session_activity` implied in truncated hunk)  
**ISSUE:** Drain gate now uses `_latest_session_activity()` while gated switches use `_session_quiet(force=True)`; a 5s `SESSION_SCAN_CACHE` is introduced to collapse scans within a tick.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** If cache ignores `force` or is shared between “boolean since X” (`stop_at`) and “global latest mtime” callers, drain release / busy detection can reuse a snapshot from earlier in the tick. Activity starting after the cached scan but before drain completes can be invisible until cache expiry—extending forced-switch drain or releasing early relative to real traffic.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Cache key includes purpose (`stop_at` vs full max) and `force=True` bypasses cache; do not feed drain decisions from the same entry as below-threshold quiet checks unless semantics match.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py` (~1907–1930, prefer-mode home pin)  
**ISSUE:** When current headroom is unreadable (`active_headroom is None`), code may set `active_headroom = 100.0 - settings.threshold` from stale `last.last_good` vs `_home_return_margin`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Stale `last_good` (quota moved, weekly shift, or model pin changed) forces an “at threshold” escape while keychain is down; conversely, a last reading still above margin keeps blind pin until `IDLE_HOLD_MAX_S` with no fresh signal—wrong side of the wall if usage moved during keychain outage.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Bound staleness (timestamp on `last_good`), require margin breach by more than hysteresis, or treat prolonged `None` as “unknown → rotate” instead of injecting a synthetic threshold headroom.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py` (~2435–2445 + downstream switch loop)  
**ISSUE:** `truly_exhausted` requires `not unreadable`, but unreadable slots are excluded from `candidates` / `oauth_candidates`—they never enter the freshen-and-switch loop.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Overloaded keychain: every switchable slot unreadable, `truly_exhausted` is false (good), but `ordered` may be empty or all-zero while current sits at limit. Engine avoids the 600s sleep but may spin no-switch / at-limit handling without ever targeting the accounts that still have quota—stuck at limit until keychain recovers.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Explicit branch: if `unreadable` and at-limit, emit `NoSwitchEvent(reason="candidates-unreadable", …)` with short retry cadence instead of treating as normal empty ranking.

---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py` (~287–319, `latest_session_activity_ts`)  
**ISSUE:** Early exit on `stop_at` returns a `latest` that may be below the true tree maximum (documented as intentional).  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Safe for “any activity since X?” gates; unsafe if any caller uses the returned float as the actual last-activity timestamp in operator-facing `detail` (understates recency, confusing forensics—not a gate bypass).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Return a sentinel boolean for gate paths, or return `(found_since_stop_at, latest_seen)` so logs do not imply a global max.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3269
ISSUE: Ungated switch executes a blocking 350-second transcript scan inside `lock_state` for a completely unused local variable.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_perform_with_drain2`, for ungated switches (at-limit, failover), `quiet, _ = self._session_quiet(force=True)` is called while holding `with self.switcher.lock_state() as state:`. Because an at-limit switch occurs when sessions are stalled, the `stop_at` early exit never fires and forces a full walk of the entire transcript directory (which the author's own comments document taking 47 to 350 seconds on a loaded machine). The return value is assigned to local variable `quiet`, which is never written to `state`, never passed to `SwitchEvent`, and never logged. Running this 350-second blocking scan under the global lock freezes all other CLI commands and cron runs, and delays `state["lastSwitchAt"] = self.clock()` by several minutes after the switch actually landed, corrupting cooldown tracking.
SUGGESTED FIX: Remove the dead `quiet, _ = self._session_quiet(force=True)` call under `if not gated:` in `_perform_with_drain2`, or move it outside the lock if traffic state needs to be recorded.

---

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:320
ISSUE: `latest_session_activity_ts` early exit on `stop_at` causes cached scan to report false idle state to callers with narrower windows like `_drain_gate`.
ATTACK VECTOR: `latest_session_activity_ts` returns the mtime of the first file found with `mtime >= stop_at` instead of the true newest mtime. When cached in `_session_scan_cache` (valid for 5s) by a voluntary check using `stop_at = now - 300s`, an arbitrary older transcript (e.g. `now - 250s`) is cached even if another file has `mtime = now - 2s`. When `_drain_gate` runs within the same tick to check if transcripts were modified after `startedAt` (e.g. `now - 30s`), it inspects the cached `latest = now - 250s`, observes `latest < startedAt`, and prematurely concludes all sessions have drained. This causes the engine to drop the drain gate and switch accounts while a user is actively typing.
SUGGESTED FIX: Do not cache early-exit timestamps in `_session_scan_cache` for callers expecting exact activity time, or ensure `_drain_gate` bypasses the early-exit cache and evaluates activity strictly against `startedAt`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:2456
ISSUE: Inconsistent handling of `reserve_h is None` between `_rank_candidates` and `reserve_floor_blocked` drops reserve silently without explanation.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_rank_candidates` (line 2907), if `reserve_h` is `None` (which occurs when an account payload lacks weekly limits or usage parsing fails), the condition `if reserve_h is None or reserve_h < settings.reserve_min_life_pct:` evaluates to `True` and empties `qualifying = []`. In `_perform_switch` (line 2456), `reserve_floor_blocked` requires `reserve_h is not None`, evaluating to `False`. Because the reserve has positive 5-hour headroom, `truly_exhausted` is also `False`. Consequently, the only viable account is disqualified without qualifying as `reserve-protected` or `fleet-exhausted`, leaving the engine in an unhandled state with no candidate chosen and no explanation emitted.
SUGGESTED FIX: Reconcile both checks: in `_rank_candidates`, only disqualify the reserve if `reserve_h is not None and reserve_h < settings.reserve_min_life_pct`, or update `reserve_floor_blocked` to treat `reserve_h is None` consistently.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:1425
ISSUE: `_pre_freshen` marks slot as throttled before attempting token refresh, preventing retry for 120s on transient failure.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_pre_freshen`, `self._prefreshened[num] = now` is updated before calling `_freshen_target(num, email)`. If `account_email()` or `_freshen_target()` fails transiently (network timeout, DNS glitch, or momentary lock contention), the exception is caught and logged, but `_prefreshened[num]` remains stamped. The slot is then throttled for `PREFRESHEN_INTERVAL_S` (120 seconds / ~8 ticks). When the engine crosses the threshold 15 seconds later, this candidate's token is still near expiry, forcing the critical-path switch to pay the network latency under load that pre-freshening was designed to eliminate.
SUGGESTED FIX: Update `self._prefreshened[num] = now` only after `_freshen_target` completes successfully.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:1402
ISSUE: In-memory `_prefreshened` throttling mixes wall-clock `self.clock()` with monotonic timers.
ATTACK VECTOR: `_pre_freshen` checks `now - self._prefreshened.get(num, 0.0) < PREFRESHEN_INTERVAL_S` using `now = self.clock()` (UNIX epoch wall time), while the loop duration is tracked via `time.monotonic()`. Because `_prefreshened` is an in-memory dictionary that only persists during the running process, using wall time exposes the throttle to NTP step adjustments and system clock skew. A backward clock adjustment will freeze candidate pre-freshening indefinitely, while a forward adjustment will bypass the 120-second throttle.
SUGGESTED FIX: Use `time.monotonic()` for `now` when populating and checking `self._prefreshened`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:1932
ISSUE: Prefer-mode home escape triggers prematurely on transient unreadable readings when headroom is below 20%.
ATTACK VECTOR: Line 1932 checks `if last_h is not None and last_h < _home_return_margin(settings): active_headroom = 100.0 - settings.threshold`. `_home_return_margin` is defined as `max(20.0, 100.0 - threshold + hysteresis)`. If the home account is temporarily unreadable (e.g. Keychain unavailable for a single tick) and its last reading had 19% headroom (81% utilization), it is forced to `active_headroom = 3.0` (97% utilization). Even though the home account still had ample quota and was nowhere near the threshold (97%), a single transient read failure forces an immediate proactive switch away from home.
SUGGESTED FIX: Decouple the unreadable escape threshold from `_home_return_margin`; base the emergency escape check on the hysteresis band near the threshold (e.g., `last_h < (100.0 - settings.threshold + settings.hysteresis_pct)`), reserving the 20% bar strictly for returning to home.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:319
ISSUE: TypeError in latest_session_activity_ts when stop_at is set but all files are filtered
ATTACK VECTOR: On the first tick with stop_at set (e.g., a quiet check), if os.walk yields a directory with no readable .jsonl files, latest stays None. The new comparison latest >= stop_at then raises TypeError. This crashes the tick and leaves the engine in an inconsistent state.
SUGGESTED FIX: Check for latest is None before the comparison. If latest is None, return None immediately when stop_at is used.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3625
ISSUE: State leak in timing accounting due to unguarded early return
ATTACK VECTOR: When _perform_with_drain2 is called with trigger="at-limit" or similar early-exit triggers (e.g. "early" path returns on line 2624), the cached timing dictionary self._switch_timing is never cleared. Subsequent switches inherit and log this prior timing.
SUGGESTED FIX: Move timing reset immediately before any early return in _perform_with_drain2 (before the gated/ungated logic): self._switch_timing = None.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3007
ISSUE: Non-atomic re-check and state write creates race window
ATTACK VECTOR: The method re-queries session status twice and writes state between queries. In the presence of concurrent threads or async callbacks (e.g., Keychain updates), the switch decision could be based on stale state while the write happens after an unrelated change.
SUGGESTED FIX: Ensure the re-check to state-write path is executed under a single critical section, or defer state writes until the end after all checks complete atomically.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:2273
ISSUE: Silent removal of unreadable slots in exhaustion logic
ATTACK VECTOR: If the Keychain is temporarily overloaded, slots can vanish from the candidate list. The code treats this as "every candidate at 0" and sleeps for 600s. This masks a transient outage with a prolonged outage in production.
SUGGESTED FIX: Treat unreadable as unknown: do not enter exhaustion sleep until all candidates have been actively confirmed 0 headroom over multiple ticks.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:1496
ISSUE: Overly broad exception handler in best-effort freshen
ATTACK VECTOR: The pre-freshen optimization catches all Exceptions and logs a warning. Any unexpected error—such as corrupted local state, missing attributes, or a logic bug—becomes a warning instead of halting development and being surfaced.
SUGGESTED FIX: Catch specific expected exceptions (KeyError, ValueError) separately; re-raise or log critical for others during development.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:101
ISSUE: Inconsistent naming of cache timeout constant
ATTACK VECTOR: The cache timeout uses SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S (seconds) while related throttles use _MS (milliseconds). This increases the risk of unit-mismatch bugs when these constants are modified.
SUGGESTED FIX: Rename SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S to SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_MS and convert its value to milliseconds; update callers accordingly.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:2476
ISSUE: Use of float("inf") in sort key may be fragile if comparison rules change
ATTACK VECTOR: weekly_reset uses float("inf") for unknowns to sort last. If future changes use different comparison semantics (e.g., string comparison, NaN handling), the ordering will silently change and break assumptions.
SUGGESTED FIX: Use a sentinel large integer (e.g., a far future timestamp) instead of float("inf"), or explicitly handle unknown resets in sort keys.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py (`latest_session_activity_ts`, ~line 287; `_session_scan_cache`, ~line 1238)
ISSUE: The `stop_at` early exit returns a truncated "latest" value, and the 5 s scan cache is not keyed by `stop_at`.
ATTACK VECTOR: Caller A asks with `stop_at` = a 5-minute quiet cutoff and gets back the first transcript newer than that cutoff, which may be much older than the true newest. If the shared cache then serves that value to caller B, which checks a different window (for example the drain gate), B can read the session as quiet when a newer transcript exists. The returned value also feeds any "last activity N s ago" detail, so the reported age is overstated. The docstring's "safe direction" argument covers only "activity exists", not the age or the other window.
SUGGESTED FIX: Key the cache on `(stop_at)` or store `stop_at` alongside the result and bypass the cache when it differs. Or, for callers that need the age, do not pass `stop_at` when building the detail string.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py (prefer-home hold fallback, ~line 1914 in `_tick`/decision path)
ISSUE: The "unreadable home" fallback uses `entries[current].last_good` without any age check.
ATTACK VECTOR: `last_good` can be minutes or hours old. If the home was held at 91% and then reset while the reading was unavailable, the fallback still sets `active_headroom = 100 - threshold` and forces an escape off a home that has already recovered. The return logic then has to bring it back, which is the churn this change was meant to stop.
SUGGESTED FIX: Only apply the fallback when the `last_good` timestamp is within a short bound (for example one tick or `IDLE_HOLD_MAX_S`). Otherwise keep the existing hold.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py (reserve handling in `_try_switch` ~line 2302, and ranking ~line 2895)
ISSUE: `oauth.weekly_life(usage.get(reserve), ...)` receives the raw `decision_value()`, which can be a `str` or `None`. It skips the `isinstance(value, dict)` normalization that `_headroom_by_account` applies, and it drops `weekly_shift`, which every other headroom call passes.
ATTACK VECTOR: If `weekly_life` expects a dict, a reserve slot whose decision value is an error string can raise inside the switch path, or be read with a different weekly shift than the headroom used elsewhere. The reserve floor then gives a different answer from the rest of the decision for the same account.
SUGGESTED FIX: Normalize as the other call sites do (`v if isinstance(v, dict) else None`) and pass `weekly_shift=self.settings.weekly_shift`, or route the reserve value through a single helper.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py (`_perform_return_home` check, ~line 3537)
ISSUE: The return-home refusal dropped the explicit `100 - available >= threshold` ("used ≥ threshold") test. The remaining test is `available < _home_return_margin(settings)`, which is strict.
ATTACK VECTOR: With `hysteresis_pct = 0` and a threshold of 50 or 80, the margin is `max(20, 100 - threshold)`. A home at exactly the threshold (available = 50, or 20 at threshold 80) gives `available < margin == False`, so the engine returns to a home that is exactly at the limit. The old code refused it. Default hysteresis is not visible here, so this depends on config.
SUGGESTED FIX: Use `available <= 100.0 - settings.threshold` as an explicit guard alongside `available < _home_return_margin(settings)`, or make the margin strictly greater than the threshold band.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py (`truly_exhausted` / `unreadable`, ~line 2429)
ISSUE: `unreadable` is defined as "rotation accounts not in `candidates` and not quarantined". That treats every reason a slot is dropped from `candidates` as "unreadable".
ATTACK VECTOR: Any slot that is filtered out for a legitimate reason (wrong login family, held as the reserve, an inert home) is counted as unknown. `truly_exhausted` then stays False for as long as that slot exists, so a genuinely exhausted fleet never reaches the long sleep and keeps polling at the normal cadence.
SUGGESTED FIX: Count a slot as unreadable only when its usage entry is missing or unreadable (for example `usage.get(n) is None`), not whenever it is absent from `candidates`.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: low
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py (`_perform_with_drain2`, dry-run early return just before the `_switch_timing` clear, ~line 3206)
ISSUE: The comment says every early return leaves no stale timing, but the dry-run `return TickOutcome.SWITCHED` runs before `self._switch_timing` is cleared.
ATTACK VECTOR: A dry-run switch that sets `_switch_timing` in the freshen loop leaves it set. If a later perform call reaches the drain2 or forced path without setting timing, that switch logs the earlier freshen cost as its own.
SUGGESTED FIX: Move the `pre_timing = self._switch_timing or {}; self._switch_timing = None` pair above the dry-run return, or clear it there too.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: low
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py (constants block, `_home_return_margin` defined at ~line 150)
ISSUE: A function is defined between module constants, which breaks the grouping of the surrounding `# Sleep caps` and `# Drain gate` sections.
ATTACK VECTOR: No runtime failure. Future constant additions are likely to be inserted next to the function and become harder to find.
SUGGESTED FIX: Move `_home_return_margin` next to the other helpers, below the constants block.

Note: the diff was truncated at about 32 KB, so I reviewed only the portion shown. The caller of `latest_session_activity_ts` and the definitions of `weekly_life`, `_session_quiet`, and `_latest_session_activity` are not in the artifact, so the first two findings rest on how they are used here and should be checked against those.

Adversarial review of the provided diff only (production modules + settings/tests shown). Call-site behavior in `autoswitch` is inferred only where the diff explicitly documents it.

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/settings.py:136-137`  
**ISSUE:** Default `reserve_min_life_pct` changed from `80.0` to `20.0` without migration; same field name, opposite fleet behavior for anyone on defaults.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Upgrades pick up the new default silently. Reserve is eligible until ~80% of the weekly window is consumed instead of only when ≥80% life remained—owner/reserve account gets burned during exhaustion events that previously skipped it.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Config version bump + one-time migration (preserve explicit 80.0 for existing installs), or keep default `80.0` and require opt-in to `20.0`; log a one-shot warning when the default changes.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/oauth.py:505-518`  
**ISSUE:** `weekly_life()` does not mirror `account_headroom()`’s guard when scoped `models` omit expected weekly windows.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Partial/stale usage (missing per-model weekly window, API shape drift) yields a numeric “life” from whatever windows exist, while headroom is `None`. Reserve gating on `weekly_life` can treat the reserve as healthy and land on it when the fleet should treat usage as unknown/incomplete—repeat of “looked fine on one window” incidents.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Reuse the same `named - window_labels` check as `account_headroom`; return `None` when required windows are missing.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/settings.py:163-168`  
**ISSUE:** `weekly_shift` is zero whenever `weekly_threshold <= threshold`, with no validation or feedback.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Operator sets `weeklyThreshold` below `threshold` expecting earlier weekly switches; behavior is identical to `weekly_threshold=0`. Misconfiguration stays silent under load until weekly quota is stranded again.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Validate in settings load/CLI (`weekly_threshold == 0 or weekly_threshold > threshold`) or clamp with an explicit logged warning when ignored.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/macos_keychain.py:58-66`  
**ISSUE:** `_TIMEOUT` is fixed at import time from `CSWAP_KEYCHAIN_TIMEOUT_S`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Daemon/worker sets the env var after `macos_keychain` is already imported (common with lazy imports or test double loading). Process keeps 5s timeout → false “keychain unavailable” and blocked switches under load despite operator raising the env var.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Resolve timeout inside the subprocess wrapper per call, or expose a single lazy `_get_timeout()` used by all keychain ops.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/macos_keychain.py:58-64`  
**ISSUE:** `_timeout_from_env()` accepts non-finite and unbounded positive floats (`inf`, `1e9`).  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Typo or hostile env (`CSWAP_KEYCHAIN_TIMEOUT_S=inf`) removes effective deadline on `security` subprocess; credential path blocks indefinitely, stalling switches and pre-freshen ticks that depend on keychain.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Reject non-finite values; cap with a hard maximum (e.g. 120s) after `float()` parsing.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/oauth.py:549-556`  
**ISSUE:** Large `weekly_shift` (up to ~99.9 when `weekly_threshold` ≫ `threshold`) drives weekly window `pct` to `0` in headroom math while real utilization is unchanged.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Aggressive tuning makes weekly windows appear empty in `account_headroom` while 5h is fine; candidate ranking/pre-freshen (callers using headroom) favors accounts that are actually mid-week on quota, then switch-time hits real limits or wrong target order.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Cap shift so adjusted `pct` cannot fall more than a bounded margin below raw `pct`, or document/enforce max `(weekly_threshold - threshold)` in settings validation tied to intended switch delta.

---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/macos_keychain.py:59-61`  
**ISSUE:** Only `ValueError` is caught from `float()`; other parse failures propagate at import.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Rare env values that raise `OverflowError` (or similar) on import crash the CLI/daemon before it can fall back to file credentials—total outage vs degraded mode.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** `except (ValueError, OverflowError): return default` (optionally log once).

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/oauth.py:515
ISSUE: `weekly_life()` ignores unverified model windows, bypassing missing-model protections for reserve accounts.
ATTACK VECTOR: Unlike `account_headroom()`, which validates that every model in `models` exists in the reported windows (and returns `None` if missing to prevent selecting accounts with dead model access), `weekly_life()` accepts `models: Sequence[str] = ()` but never checks `named - {label.lower() for label, _, _ in windows}`. If an account omits the scoped window for a configured model (e.g. Fable access revoked or unverified), `weekly_life()` ignores the missing model and calculates life solely from the 7-day window. This allows the autoswitch engine to select an unusable account as a qualified reserve account.
SUGGESTED FIX: In `weekly_life()`, extract `named = {m.lower() for m in models} - {"all"}` and return `None` if `named - {label.lower() for label, _, _ in windows}` is non-empty.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/macos_keychain.py:67
ISSUE: `_TIMEOUT` evaluates `os.environ` once at module import time, freezing timeout values and accepting infinite floats.
ATTACK VECTOR: `_TIMEOUT = _timeout_from_env()` executes when the module is imported. The docstring notes that `CSWAP_KEYCHAIN_TIMEOUT_S` is intended for the long-running daemon; however, if the daemon configures or updates the environment variable during startup after imports have resolved, `_TIMEOUT` remains frozen at the 5.0s default. On loaded hosts, keychain calls continue timing out after 5s and refusing switches. Additionally, `float("inf") > 0` evaluates to `True`, so `CSWAP_KEYCHAIN_TIMEOUT_S=inf` eliminates subprocess timeouts entirely, risking indefinite process hangs.
SUGGESTED FIX: Call `_timeout_from_env()` dynamically at invocation sites (or via a helper function) rather than storing a module-level constant, and ensure `math.isfinite(value)` is checked.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/settings.py:165
ISSUE: `weekly_shift` silently disables `weekly_threshold` when configured lower than `threshold`.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `AutoSwitchSettings.weekly_shift`, the calculation is clamped using `max(0.0, self.weekly_threshold - self.threshold)`. If an operator specifies a lower threshold for weekly quota than 5-hour quota (e.g., `threshold = 95.0, weekly_threshold = 80.0` to conserve weekly quota by switching earlier), `weekly_shift` evaluates to `0.0`. `account_headroom()` then ignores the weekly threshold entirely and permits weekly utilization up to 95.0%, silently failing to enforce the user's configured setting.
SUGGESTED FIX: Either support negative shifts in `account_headroom()` for conservative weekly triggers, or add validation in `SettingSpec` rejecting `0 < weekly_threshold < threshold`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/oauth.py:551
ISSUE: `account_headroom()` creates a discontinuous cliff at 100% and distorts candidate selection ranking.
ATTACK VECTOR: `account_headroom()` discounts weekly `pct` by `weekly_shift` only while `pct < 100.0`. If `weekly_shift = 5.0`, an account at 99.9% usage reports 5.1% headroom, but the moment it hits 100.0%, discounting stops and headroom collapses from 5.1% to 0.0%. Furthermore, because the discount mutates general headroom rather than just threshold comparison, candidate sorting algorithms will rank an account with 98% weekly usage (2% actual life left) as having 6% headroom, incorrectly picking it over an account with 95% 5-hour usage (5% actual life left).
SUGGESTED FIX: Apply `weekly_shift` only at the threshold-evaluation decision site rather than mutating raw headroom returned by `account_headroom()`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_home_near_limit.py:102
ISSUE: At-limit switches execute before checking activity while falsely recording `event.gate = "quiet"`.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `TestUngatedSwitchScansAfterTheSwap`, `test_at_limit_switch_lands_before_the_transcript_walk` asserts that `switch_to` executes before `latest_session_activity_ts`. Moving credential switching ahead of the transcript walk leaves in-flight user or agent sessions unprotected against mid-turn credential swaps. Furthermore, asserting `event.gate == "quiet"` and `timing["quietScanMs"] == 0` generates inaccurate audit events claiming the switch was quiet-gated when the gate was in fact bypassed.
SUGGESTED FIX: When bypassing the pre-switch quiet scan, record `event.gate = "forced-at-limit"` or `"immediate"` rather than falsely emitting `"quiet"`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_expiring_quota_first.py:45
ISSUE: Accounts without reset timestamps are permanently deprioritized behind accounts with near-zero life.
ATTACK VECTOR: `test_unknown_resets_sort_after_known_ones` enforces that any account with a known reset timestamp beats an account with an unknown reset timestamp, regardless of remaining life. Account 3 with 50% life and a distant reset (`_R_LATEST`) beats Account 2 with 90% life and an unknown reset. If Anthropic rate-limit headers omit reset times or parsing fails for a subset of accounts, those accounts are starved even if virtually untouched.
SUGGESTED FIX: Only prioritize by reset time when the reset falls within an imminent window (e.g. < 24h), falling back to highest remaining quota when resets are distant or unknown.

ISSUE: `weekly_shift` moves weekly utilization in the wrong direction.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/settings.py
ISSUE: Division-by-zero risk in `weekly_shift` logic if `threshold` is zero
ATTACK VECTOR: If `threshold` is set to 0.0 (disabled 5h window), `settings.weekly_shift` calculates `self.weekly_threshold - 0`, but if `threshold` is never zero in practice, the math `pct - shift` in `account_headroom` assumes 0.0 is the 5h baseline. If `threshold` logic changes downstream, `pct - shift` could over-shave usage percentages below zero, masking exhaustion.
SUGGESTED FIX: Guard the shift calculation: `if self.threshold == 0: return 0.0`, or ensure `threshold` can never be zero in valid configurations.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/oauth.py
ISSUE: `weekly_life` ignores model-specific window constraints
ATTACK VECTOR: `relevant_windows` accepts a `models` parameter that scopes the windows. If the caller passes specific models to isolate quota for a particular model, `weekly_life` might still return headroom based on the *tightest* across all models rather than the specific model being evaluated, leading to incorrect quota calculations for isolated models.
SUGGESTED FIX: Explicitly filter `weekly` list by `models` names when computing `max(weekly)` or document that `weekly_life` always returns global headroom.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/macos_keychain.py

ATTACK VECTOR: `os.environ.get("CSWAP_KEYCHAIN_TIMEOUT_S", default)` parses float without max limit. If an operator sets `CSWAP_KEYCHAIN_TIMEOUT_S=1000000` during debugging, subsequent Keychain operations will block for hours, potentially hanging the main daemon thread and preventing switch logic from running.
SUGGESTED FIX: Clamp `value` in `_timeout_from_env()`: `min(value, 300.0)` (or appropriate upper limit) to prevent runaway timeouts.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/settings.py

ATTACK VECTOR: `threshold` is a setting field. If `threshold` changes (via config reload) after `AutoSwitchSettings` is constructed, `weekly_shift` dynamically reflects new value. If `account_headroom` cached `weekly_shift` value passed in as argument from old settings object, it will calculate headroom against stale `threshold` logic, causing headroom misalignment.
SUGGESTED FIX: Pass `threshold` and `weekly_threshold` explicitly to the calculation function, or cache `weekly_shift` only after ensuring settings are immutable during operation.

SEVERITY: INFO

FILE: src/claude_swap/oauth.py

ATTACK VECTOR: `weekly_life` and `account_headroom` return `float`. `relevant_windows` returns tuples. If downstream code expects `int` (e.g. `int(usage)`), or vice versa, comparing `usage` with integer thresholds (like `100`) can introduce floating-point rounding errors or equality checks failing (e.g., `99.99999 != 100`).
SUGGESTED FIX: Normalize all percentage calculations to `float` consistently or document expected precision throughout the usage chain.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:47
ISSUE: `weekly_shift` silently collapses to 0.0 when `weekly_threshold < threshold`, completely nullifying user configuration.
ATTACK VECTOR: If a user configures a stricter weekly threshold than 5-hour threshold (e.g., `autoswitch.weeklyThreshold = 90.0` with `threshold = 95.0` to protect scarce 7-day quotas), `AutoSwitchSettings.weekly_shift` evaluates via `max(0.0, self.weekly_threshold - self.threshold)` to `0.0`. `oauth.account_headroom` then applies zero shift, causing the weekly window to switch only at `threshold = 95.0`. The user's configuration is silently ignored without error or warning, and line 47 enshrines this silent failure as intended behavior.
SUGGESTED FIX: In `AutoSwitchSettings.weekly_shift` and `oauth.account_headroom`, support negative shift offsets, or validate in `SettingSpec` that `weekly_threshold >= threshold` (or 0) so invalid configurations fail fast.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:27
ISSUE: Candidate ranking under `strategy="best"` prioritizes accounts that have already exceeded their weekly threshold over healthy accounts.
ATTACK VECTOR: `account_headroom` discounts weekly usage by `weekly_shift` for all `pct < 100.0`. If `threshold = 95.0` and `weekly_threshold = 99.0` (`weekly_shift = 4.0`), an account at 99.5% weekly usage (already over the 99% weekly threshold with 0.5% left) reports `100 - (99.5 - 4.0) = 4.5%` headroom. If Candidate A has 5h usage at 96% and 7d at 10% (headroom = 4.0%), `strategy="best"` chooses Candidate B (4.5% headroom) over Candidate A. Once switched to Candidate B, the engine sees Candidate B is over threshold (95.5% >= 95.0%) on the very next tick, causing immediate re-switching and switch thrashing.
SUGGESTED FIX: In `oauth.account_headroom`, clamp headroom to 0.0 (or prevent discounting) for weekly windows where `pct >= (100.0 - (100.0 - threshold) + weekly_shift)` (i.e. `pct >= weekly_threshold`).

SEVERITY: WARNING

FILE: tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:59
ISSUE: Artificial test setup masks hysteresis deadlock when the active account switches at `weekly_threshold`.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_weekly_at_99_switches`, Candidate 2 is given `_usage7(0, 10)` (90% headroom). In production fleets where accounts age concurrently, when active Account 1 hits 99.0% weekly use (active headroom = 5.0%), other fleet accounts may sit at 97.0% weekly usage. Because crossing `threshold` enters a `proactive` (voluntary) trigger, line 2843 of `autoswitch.py` requires candidates to beat active headroom by `settings.hysteresis_pct` (default 3.0%). Candidate 2 has 7.0% headroom (`7.0 - 5.0 = 2.0% < 3.0%`). All candidates are rejected by hysteresis, resulting in `TickOutcome.BLOCKED` and trapping execution on Account 1 until it hits 100% hard exhaustion.
SUGGESTED FIX: In `autoswitch.py`, bypass the hysteresis requirement when switching due to weekly threshold breach, or scale hysteresis proportionally to the remaining weekly headroom band.

SUGGESTED FIX: Add a case with `_usage7(100.0, 57.0)` for the reserve and assert the expected outcome (`BLOCKED`, or a switch only if the normal 5h check is applied to the reserve). Then make the production code match that assertion.

**WARNING** · CONFIDENCE: low
FILE: tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:20-31 (`TestHeadroomShift`, `test_an_exhausted_weekly_window_stays_exhausted`)
ISSUE: The weekly shift is a constant added to headroom for every weekly value below 100, and only exactly `100` is clamped to zero.
ATTACK VECTOR: A weekly window at 99.9% used with `weekly_shift=4` reports `4.1` headroom while only 0.1% of the quota remains. A ranking or eligibility check that uses headroom can then prefer it over an account with a real 4% of headroom. The tests cover 97, 99 and exactly 100, but not the 99.x range where the value is inflated.
SUGGESTED FIX: Clamp the weekly contribution to zero once `seven_day` reaches the weekly threshold (or 100 minus the shift), not only at 100, and add a test at 99.9.

**INFO** · CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_reserve_account.py:~108-114 (`test_the_default_floor_lets_the_reserve_run_to_80pct_of_its_week`)
ISSUE: The name says "run to 80% of its week", but the only assertion is at 79% used. Nothing checks the edge.
ATTACK VECTOR: A regression that moves the default floor by a point, or that reverses the inclusive/exclusive comparison at the default, passes this test unnoticed. The only boundary tests use the explicit `reserved()` default, not `AutoSwitchSettings()`.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add one assertion at 80.1% used that expects `BLOCKED`, or at 80.0% that expects `SWITCHED`, using the settings default.

**INFO** · CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_reserve_account.py:~99-104 (docstring of `test_a_busy_5h_window_does_not_hold_the_reserve`)
ISSUE: The docstring cites the incident as "53% of its week left", but the fixture uses `_usage7(85.0, 57.0)`, which is 43% left.
ATTACK VECTOR: Someone trusting the test as a reproduction of the incident will believe it covers those exact numbers. It covers a similar case, not the recorded one.
SUGGESTED FIX: Use `_usage7(85.0, 47.0)` to match the 53% left, or change the docstring to describe the values actually used.

**INFO** · CONFIDENCE: low
FILE: tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:11
ISSUE: The new test module imports the private helper `_usage7` and the harness from `tests.test_autoswitch`, so it depends on another test module's internals.
ATTACK VECTOR: Renaming or moving the helper in `test_autoswitch` breaks this file, and the import error does not explain the coupling. This is a maintenance concern, not a runtime bug.
SUGGESTED FIX: Move `_usage7` and `EngineHarness` into a shared test helper module (for example `tests/_autoswitch_helpers.py`) and import from there in both files. Only do this if other test modules already share helpers that way.

###   PROVIDER: QWEN

**1. SEVERITY: CRITICAL — CONFIDENCE: high**
**FILE:** `tests/test_reserve_account.py:99-106` (new `test_a_busy_5h_window_does_not_hold_the_reserve`), replacing deleted `test_the_weekly_window_binds_the_life_check_too`
**ISSUE:** The floor fix over-corrected: it went from "headroom is the WORSE window" to "the floor reads the weekly window *only*", so a reserve whose 5h window is 85% consumed is now selected.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The deleted test's docstring states the old invariant explicitly ("headroom is the WORSE window"). The new test encodes `_usage7(85.0, 57.0) → SWITCHED`, i.e. the engine deliberately hands the session to a fallback account with ~15% of a 5h window left. That account crosses the 95% 5h threshold within ~15% of a working window and the session dies mid-stream — the exact class of outage the test claims to guard (2026-09-29 22:49Z), just relocated from "reserve held" to "reserve burnt on arrival." The value 85.0 is chosen to sit *below* the 95 normal threshold, which shows the 5h path is not covered by any other check either. Nothing in the artifact prevents a reserve at 5h=94% weekly=0% from being promoted.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Keep the floor weekly-only (per the owner's rule) but add a hard selection precondition that the candidate's binding window also clears the normal threshold, or floor on `min(5h left, weekly left)` for *selection* while using weekly-only for *protection*. Add the mirrored test: `_usage7(96.0, 20.0)` must NOT switch.

**2. SEVERITY: WARNING — CONFIDENCE: high**
**FILE:** `tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:28-40`
**ISSUE:** `account_headroom()` no longer returns headroom — it returns a threshold-adjusted score — and the shape of the tests proves it.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `test_an_exhausted_weekly_window_stays_exhausted` exists only because the function needed a special case to stop `+weekly_shift` from resurrecting a 100%-used window. So the returned number is now "100 − pct + shift", which is not comparable to "percent remaining" for any other consumer: a UI/status line, a ranking tiebreak, or an alert that prints `account_headroom(...)` will overstate real weekly availability by `weekly_shift`, silently, in production. The metric also becomes non-monotone against reality at the cliff: weekly 99.9% → score 4.9, weekly 100% → score 0.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Leave `account_headroom` as true remaining headroom and move the per-window threshold into the switch rule (`pct >= window_threshold(window)`), passing the threshold instead of the derived gap. If that's too large a change now, rename the function (`switch_score`) so callers can't mistake it for availability, and stop using it where the raw number is displayed.

**3. SEVERITY: WARNING — CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE:** `tests/test_reserve_account.py:99-115` + `tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:38-40`
**ISSUE:** No test distinguishes whether the reserve floor reads the *shifted* or *unshifted* weekly headroom — and the fixtures look chosen so both interpretations pass.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `weekly_shift` defaults to `0.0` and `test_no_shift_is_the_old_behaviour` pins that as correct, meaning threading it into a call site is opt-in per site. The default-floor test uses weekly=79.0 with floor≈20: shifted remaining = 25, raw = 21, both ≥ 20 → SWITCHED either way. The value that would separate the two is weekly=81 (raw 19 → BLOCKED, shifted 23 → SWITCHED). The suite never runs it. So a 4-percentage-point semantic — precisely the quota the whole change exists to reclaim — is unpinned for the floor path. The one place the unshifted metric *is* pinned, the `"40% of its week"` string assertion, runs in a harness where `weekly_threshold` is almost certainly unset (shift 0), so it proves nothing.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add `test_the_floor_ignores_the_weekly_shift`: build the harness with `weekly_threshold=99.0` and assert the floor blocks at weekly=81 with `reserve_min_life_pct=20.0`, so the floor's metric is defined, not inherited by accident.

**4. SEVERITY: WARNING — CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE:** `tests/test_reserve_account.py:99` and `:108-113`
**ISSUE:** The fixture's default floor (80% life left) is not the production default, and the two tests have to work around it.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The tests that keep the fixture default assert BLOCKED for weekly-used 20.1/25/50/60 and print "80% reserve floor"; for `test_the_default_floor_lets_the_reserve_run_to_80pct_of_its_week` to pass with weekly=79, `AutoSwitchSettings().reserve_min_life_pct` must be ≤ 21. That is only consistent if `reserved()` hardcodes a floor that production never uses — which is why both new tests pass `reserve_min_life_pct` explicitly and the second one has to reach into `settings` for the real value. Consequence: the entire "reserve-protected" suite validates a 4x-more-conservative config than ships; a regression that loosens or breaks the floor default is invisible, and the misleading test name ("the default floor") advertises coverage that the fixture actually withholds.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Make `reserved()` default `reserve_min_life_pct` to `AutoSwitchSettings().reserve_min_life_pct` and pin the numeric default in one explicit assertion (`assert AutoSwitchSettings().reserve_min_life_pct == 20.0`), then re-tune the 80%-based tests to pass their floor explicitly as a named scenario.

**5. SEVERITY: WARNING — CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE:** `tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:43-51`
**ISSUE:** `weekly_threshold` is only round-tripped through config and only *lower*-clamped; there is no upper bound, no range validation, and the coupling to `threshold` is untested.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `weekly_shift == max(0, weekly_threshold − threshold)` means an operator typo (`autoswitch.weeklyThreshold = 150`, or a stray edit of `threshold` to 80) inflates weekly headroom by an arbitrary amount instead of erroring — an account at weekly 90% consumed would score 60% headroom and be treated as near-full fleet-wide, defeating switching entirely. Conversely setting `weeklyThreshold` *below* `threshold` is silently coerced to 0 (`test_shift_is_the_gap_...` line 47 pins the clamp), so the misconfiguration produces no signal at all; the operator believes weekly windows switch at 90 while they still switch at 95. The load test only exercises the happy path `"99"`.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Validate `weekly_threshold` at the settings boundary (reject non-numeric and anything outside `0 < x <= 100`, and warn when `weekly_threshold < threshold` rather than clamping to silence), cap `weekly_shift` at a sane maximum, and add a `load_settings` test for a bad value asserting the typed error/behaviour.

**6. SEVERITY: WARNING — CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE:** `tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:29-36`
**ISSUE:** `account_headroom` is fed only hand-built dicts with both windows present and integral percentages; the real response shapes that the shift logic has to survive are unpinned.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Two production cases the guard does not cover. (a) A weekly percentage over 100 (APIs round up, and `pct` is often a float like `100.02` after recalculation): the exhaustion special case is only proven at exactly `100.0`, so `100 − 100.4 + 4 = 3.6` presents an over-quota account as having headroom and the engine switches *to* a dead window. (b) A payload with no `seven_day` entry (plan tiers that don't get a weekly cap) — every test passes both keys, so the missing-key path (KeyError, or a `None` comparison that throws) is never taken. Also no test covers a partially-populated response (`{"seven_day": {}}`, missing `pct`) which is what a rate-limited/degraded upstream actually returns.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add cases for `_usage7(0, 100.4)` → 0.0 and for an absent/None/`{}`-`seven_day` payload, asserting the function falls back to the 5h window. Clamp before adding: `max(0.0, 100 - pct) + shift` is wrong; clamp the *result* after, and treat `pct >= 100` as exhausted rather than `== 100`.

**7. SEVERITY: INFO — CONFIDENCE: high**
**FILE:** `tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:13, 30, 32, 36, 40`
**ISSUE:** Exact `==` on computed floats, and cross-module import of private helpers.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `== 7.0 / 4.0 / 3.0 / 0.0` only holds because every fixture percentage is an integer. Real percentages arrive via `float(...)` from config and JSON; the moment the implementation reorders the arithmetic (`(100 - pct) + shift` vs `100 - (pct - shift)`) these assertions break for reasons unrelated to behavior, and the suite starts failing at random — the classic signal that gets a test deleted rather than fixed. Relatedly, `test_weekly_at_99_switches` places the boundary at exactly `99.0`, so inclusive-vs-exclusive weekly boundary semantics is pinned only by an integral value that any real quota reading will miss. `from tests.test_autoswitch import EngineHarness, _usage7` also couples an unrelated suite to another module's underscore-private helper and depends on `tests` being importable (rootdir/`__init__.py`), so a refactor of `test_autoswitch` breaks `test_weekly_threshold` and `test_reserve_account`.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use `pytest.approx` for the headroom assertions, add one non-integral boundary pair (e.g. `98.999999` no-switch vs `99.000001` switch), and hoist `EngineHarness`/`_usage7` into `tests/conftest.py` (or a public helper module) instead of importing them across test modules.

Adversarial review of the provided diff (chunk 2/3), focused on production failures and bypasses—not style.

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (`stored_path` / `switch`)  
**ISSUE:** Stored auth paths are built from unsanitized `email` (`f"{email.lower()}.auth.json"`), so `/`, `..`, or other path segments in CLI `switch` input (or a crafted JWT `email` in auth JSON) can resolve outside `store_dir`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `cswap codex switch '../../other'` or a malicious `email` claim in auth data can read/write auth files outside the intended backup tree (token theft or clobbering unrelated files).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Reject emails containing path separators or `..`; normalize with `.resolve()` and assert `path.is_relative_to(store_dir)` (or equivalent) before any read/write.

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (`login`, post-login live check)  
**ISSUE:** `if read_auth(live_auth_path()) is None: write_private(live_auth_path(), auth)` treats any unreadable live auth (missing file, permissions, corrupt JSON) the same as “no login” and installs the new account as live.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Transient I/O failure, partial write, or corrupt `auth.json` during `cswap codex login` silently replaces the working live Codex session with the newly logged-in account—matching the failure mode the feature is meant to avoid (revoked/overwritten logins).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Only seed live when the path is definitively absent (e.g. `not live_auth_path().exists()`), or distinguish `read_auth` outcomes; on parse/OS errors, fail with `CodexError` instead of overwriting live.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/cli.py` (`_codex_command`, `auto` branch)  
**ISSUE:** The inner `except Exception` around `auto_tick` also catches `KeyboardInterrupt`, `CodexError`, and other base exceptions, converts them to JSON, and continues the loop.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Ctrl+C during `auto` may not terminate with 130; a failed `switch()` surfaced as `CodexError` elsewhere becomes a log line and the daemon keeps running—masking hard failures operators expect to be fatal.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Catch a narrow set (e.g. `OSError`, `urllib` errors if any bubble up); re-raise `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit`; either re-raise `CodexError` or map it to `sys.exit(1)` after one emitted event.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (`run_after_switch`)  
**ISSUE:** Hook runs as `subprocess.Popen(command, shell=True, …)` with `command` from settings (`codex_after_switch`).  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Anyone who can change settings under the backup root (synced folder, backup restore, shared machine) gets arbitrary shell execution on every successful switch—full RCE as the cswap user.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Run without `shell=True` using `shlex.split` + argv exec, or restrict to a fixed allowlisted script path; document that the backup root must be user-only trusted.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (`switch`, `auto_tick`, `sync_live`)  
**ISSUE:** No cross-process locking around sync-live → write-stored → write-live and credits-state updates.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Two `cswap codex auto` loops or a manual `switch` during `auto_tick` can interleave: live `auth.json` from account A while stored copies reflect B, duplicate/conflicting refresh-token writes, or double `after_switch` hooks.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use an exclusive lock file (e.g. `fcntl`/`portalocker`) for the whole `sync_live` + `switch` + credits-state critical section.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (`fetch_usage` / `auto_tick`)  
**ISSUE:** Usage for the active account uses the current access token with no refresh; expired access yields `Usage(error=…)` and `auto_tick` returns `active-usage-unknown` without attempting rotation.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Live session still works via Codex’s own refresh, but `auto` stays stuck below/above threshold logic incorrectly for hours/days until someone manually switches—quota exhaustion on the “wrong” account while rotation appears “healthy” in the loop.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** On 401 “token expired” for the live account only, trigger a lightweight refresh (delegate to `codex` CLI if available) or treat as “needs sync” and retry once after re-reading live auth post-refresh.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (`login`)  
**ISSUE:** `subprocess.call(["codex", "login", *args], env=env)` has no timeout.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Hung device-auth or network stall blocks the CLI indefinitely; automation/scripts waiting on `cswap codex login` never complete and temp `CODEX_HOME` cleanup only runs after return.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Pass a configurable timeout to `subprocess.run(..., timeout=…)` and raise `CodexError` on expiry, ensuring `finally` still removes the temp directory.

###   PROVIDER: AGY

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:284
ISSUE: Missing OAuth token refresh mechanism causes permanent daemon freeze on expired access tokens
ATTACK VECTOR: Inactive accounts in `backup_root/codex` have OAuth access tokens that expire after ~1 hour. `fetch_usage()` probes OpenAI using `tokens.get('access_token')` and receives HTTP 401 (`error="token expired — refreshes on the next switch to it"`). However, `switch()` merely copies JSON files; it performs no token refresh exchange. When `auto_tick` switches to an account with an expired access token, the subsequent tick gets a 401 on `current`, causing `auto_tick` to hit `if live_usage.error is not None: return {"event": "codex-no-switch", "reason": "active-usage-unknown"}`. The daemon permanently halts rotation. Furthermore, expired tokens disqualify accounts from credit mode because `on_credits_ok()` checks `self.error is None`.
SUGGESTED FIX: In `fetch_usage()` or prior to switching, perform an OAuth token refresh using `refresh_token` when an access token is expired, or do not treat expired access tokens on the live account as an unrecoverable `active-usage-unknown` error.

---

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:317
ISSUE: `rank()` retains error and revoked accounts, trapping `auto_tick` on dead logins
ATTACK VECTOR: `rank()` filters out quota-exhausted accounts (`life is not None and life <= 0`), but retains accounts where `life is None` (accounts returning errors, including `token_revoked` and connection timeouts). When all usable accounts are past their weekly threshold, `rank()` returns the broken account instead of an empty list, bypassing the credits fallback (`if ordered:` evaluates to `True`). Once switched to the broken account, `auto_tick` aborts on `live_usage.error is not None` on every tick, locking the switcher onto an unauthenticated or revoked account forever.
SUGGESTED FIX: In `rank()`, ignore accounts with errors (`if usage.error is not None: continue`), and allow `auto_tick` to switch away from `current` if `live_usage.error` indicates a permanently revoked token.

---

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:355
ISSUE: `switch()` permanently destroys unstored active Codex logins by overwriting `auth.json` without backup
ATTACK VECTOR: When a user runs `cswap codex switch <email>`, `switch()` calls `sync_live()`. If the active login was not previously added via `cswap codex add`, `stored_path(backup_root, email).exists()` is `False`, so `sync_live()` skips saving it. `switch()` then immediately overwrites `~/.codex/auth.json` with the target account's credentials, permanently obliterating the active session's refresh tokens from disk. While `auto_tick` safeguards against this (`reason: live-login-not-stored`), `switch()` does not.
SUGGESTED FIX: In `switch()`, verify that `previous` is backed up before calling `write_private(live_auth_path(), target)`, or auto-store the live login if unstored (or raise `CodexError`).

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:343
ISSUE: Accounts with errors rank ahead of a healthy reserve account in rotation order
ATTACK VECTOR: The sort key tuple in `rank()` places `email == reserve` as index 0 and `life is None` as index 1. Because `False < True` in Python tuple comparison, any non-reserve account returning an error or unreadable state (`email == reserve` is `False`, `life is None` is `True`) ranks ahead of a healthy reserve account with quota remaining (`email == reserve` is `True`, `life is None` is `False`). When an account fails usage retrieval, rotation selects the broken account instead of falling back to the reserve.
SUGGESTED FIX: Place `life is None` before `email == reserve` in the sort tuple: `((life is None, email == reserve, ...), email)`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/cli.py:968
ISSUE: Manual CLI `switch` command fails to trigger `codex_after_switch` hook
ATTACK VECTOR: Running Codex processes and the background language server daemon cache authentication in memory on startup. When `cswap codex switch <email>` is executed manually, it only calls `codex.switch()` and omits `run_after_switch()`. Existing Codex sessions continue running under the replaced account and burning its quota/credits, defeating the purpose of the switch.
SUGGESTED FIX: In `_codex_command`'s `switch` handler, load `settings.codex_after_switch` and invoke `codex.run_after_switch(settings.codex_after_switch, previous, args.email)`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/cli.py:978
ISSUE: `cswap codex --json` fails with an unrecognized argument error
ATTACK VECTOR: The `--json` argument is attached only to the `list` subparser (`p_list.add_argument("--json")`), but not to the parent `parser`. The CLI falls through to `else: # list (default)` when no subcommand is specified, but executing `cswap codex --json` causes `argparse` to abort with `unrecognized arguments: --json` before reaching the dispatch handler.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add `--json` to the parent `parser`, or define `p_list` options on the root parser.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:497
ISSUE: Unreaped subprocesses in `run_after_switch` leak zombie processes in long-running auto loop
ATTACK VECTOR: `run_after_switch()` spawns commands using `subprocess.Popen(..., start_new_session=True)` without retaining the process handle, calling `wait()`/`poll()`, or registering a `SIGCHLD` handler. In the persistent `cswap codex auto` loop, every switch hook invocation leaves a defunct zombie process in the process table until the daemon terminates.
SUGGESTED FIX: Explicitly ignore `SIGCHLD` via `signal.signal(signal.SIGCHLD, signal.SIG_IGN)` or double-fork the hook process so it is adopted by `init`.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:140  
ISSUE: `sync_live()` can overwrite a stored login with an older live refresh token.  
ATTACK VECTOR: A running Codex process rotates the token while `sync_live()` reads `auth.json`, then writes the older snapshot after the process updates the live file. A later switch restores the stale token.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Coordinate reads and writes with the process that updates `auth.json`, or verify the live file has not changed before replacing the stored copy.

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:374  
ISSUE: Usage failures are ranked as switch candidates.  
ATTACK VECTOR: When another account’s usage request fails, `rank()` includes it after known usable accounts. If all known accounts are exhausted, `auto_tick()` switches to the unreadable account, potentially selecting a revoked login.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Exclude accounts with `usage.error` from automatic switching.

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:319  
ISSUE: A manual switch accepts a stored file whose account identity does not match the requested email.  
ATTACK VECTOR: A mislabeled or corrupted stored file passes `read_auth()` and becomes the live login. The command reports that it switched to the requested account while installing another account’s credentials.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Check `email_of(target) == email` before writing the live file.

SEVERITY: WARNING

FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:426  
ISSUE: Credits state is changed before the login switch succeeds.  
ATTACK VECTOR: If writing the live auth file fails, credits mode may already be pinned to the target or cleared while the original login remains active. Later ticks make decisions from inconsistent state.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Update credits state only after `switch()` succeeds.

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:458  
ISSUE: The after-switch hook reports success when its process merely starts.  
ATTACK VECTOR: A shell command can exit immediately with an error, while the event says `"started"` and the loop continues. Running sessions then remain on the old login without an actionable failure signal.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Capture the hook’s exit status or write its output and status to a monitored log.


###############################################################
###   PROVIDER: CLAUDE
###############################################################

**Adversarial review: chunk 2 (cswap codex CLI wiring, `codex.py` login/switch/credits/after-switch)**

**1. WARNING · high confidence**
FILE: `src/claude_swap/cli.py` (`_codex_command`, text `list` branch)
ISSUE: Unknown weekly usage is printed as `week: 0%`.
ATTACK VECTOR: `u.weekly_pct if u.weekly_pct is not None else 0` runs when `u.error` is None but no weekly window was parsed (payload without a weekly window, or the window misclassified, see #2). The row shows a fresh week at 0% used, while `rank()` treats the same account as "unknown life". The operator sees a full quota that may not exist.
SUGGESTED FIX: Print `week: unknown` (or `?`) when `weekly_pct is None`, and keep the `0` fallback out of the display.

**2. WARNING · medium confidence**
FILE: `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (`parse_usage`)
ISSUE: Window classification by `limit_window_seconds` is lossy. A missing or zero length counts as weekly, and a second weekly-length window silently overwrites the first.
ATTACK VECTOR: A 5h window that lacks `limit_window_seconds` lands in `weekly`. Its `used_percent` and `reset_at` then drive the weekly threshold and the "soonest weekly reset" ranking. The real weekly quota is ignored, so the switch fires late, or on the wrong window, and running sessions hit the limit.
SUGGESTED FIX: Treat a window with no length as ambiguous (skip it or keep it separate), and do not overwrite an already-assigned slot. Log or surface the ambiguity instead of guessing.

**3. WARNING · medium confidence**
FILE: `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (`auto_tick`, the early `below-threshold` return)
ISSUE: `credits-mode.json` is only cleared on the quota-switch path. When the pinned credits account drops back under threshold (for example after its weekly reset), the pin is never cleared.
ATTACK VECTOR: `cswap codex list` keeps tagging that account `(on credits)`. Later, when it goes over threshold again with no quota candidates, `pinned == current` stays true, so the loop stays on credits without a fresh decision.
SUGGESTED FIX: In the below-threshold return (when not `dry_run`), call `_set_credits_account(backup_root, None)` if the pin equals `current`.

**4. WARNING · low confidence**
FILE: `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (`switch`, `sync_live` then `write_private(live_auth_path(), target)`)
ISSUE: Between the live copy-back and the overwrite, a running Codex process can refresh the live token.
ATTACK VECTOR: Refresh tokens rotate. The refreshed token written in that window is discarded, so the previous account's stored copy holds an already-rotated refresh token. The next switch back gets `token_revoked`. The window is small, so this is unlikely but not impossible while the daemon is active.
SUGGESTED FIX: Hold a lock (for example `fcntl.flock` on the store dir) across sync and write, or re-run `sync_live` immediately before the replace and accept the small residual risk. Document it if left as is.

**5. INFO · low confidence**
FILE: `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (`write_private`)
ISSUE: `os.chmod(path.parent, 0o700)` also runs when the target is the live file, so cswap changes the mode of the user's real `~/.codex` directory.
ATTACK VECTOR: Writing the live login silently changes Codex's own directory permissions, which the user did not ask for.
SUGGESTED FIX: Only chmod directories cswap creates (`store_dir`), and check the mode is already 0700 before changing it.

**6. INFO · low confidence**
FILE: `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (`email_of` → `stored_path`)
ISSUE: The email from the id_token JWT is used unvalidated as a filename component.
ATTACK VECTOR: A crafted claim containing `/` or `..` could write outside `store_dir`. The token normally comes from OpenAI, so the risk is low, but the code trusts the claim.
SUGGESTED FIX: Validate the email against a conservative regex (for example `^[a-z0-9._+-]+@[a-z0-9.-]+$`) in `email_of` and return None on mismatch.

**7. INFO · high confidence**
FILE: `src/claude_swap/cli.py` (top-level `epilog`, `%(prog)s codex {add,list,switch,auto}`)
ISSUE: The help text omits the `login` action, which the sub-parser defines (`metavar` lists `login` first).
ATTACK VECTOR: Users read `--help`, never discover `cswap codex login` (the safe way to add a second account), and fall back to a bare `codex login`, which the module docstring says revokes the live login.
SUGGESTED FIX: Change the epilog line to `{login,add,list,switch,auto}` and mention `login` in the description.

Adversarial review of **`tests/test_codex.py`** only (chunk 2/2). Findings are ordered by severity, then confidence.

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py:~234–238, ~272  
**ISSUE:** `subprocess.Popen` mocks return `None` (via `list.append` or explicit `lambda …: None`), so hook lifecycle is never exercised.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Production code that calls `.poll()`, `.wait()`, or checks the process object assumes a real `Popen`; hook failures, zombies, or fire-and-forget mistakes never fail CI.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Return a minimal fake process (`poll()` → `None`, `returncode` → `0`) or `unittest.mock.MagicMock`; assert whatever the module actually uses on the return value.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py:~193–211  
**ISSUE:** `TestStore` asserts stored auth is `0o600`, but `TestLogin` never checks mode (or ownership) for accounts added via `login()`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A `login()` path that writes `auth.json` with default umask (often group/world readable) ships undetected while `add()` looks secure in tests.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** After `codex.login(env)`, assert `stat.S_IMODE(os.stat(codex.stored_path(env, "new@x.com")).st_mode) == 0o600` (same as `test_add_stores_a_private_copy_keyed_by_email`).

**ATTACK VECTOR:** Switch/store keyed by email but API calls keyed by `account_id` can overwrite the wrong blob, attach usage to the wrong identity, or merge accounts; tests always look consistent.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Parameterize `account_id` per email in `_auth()` and add one test where two stored accounts differ in `account_id` and switch preserves the target’s id token fields.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:105-116
ISSUE: Test enforces ranking unreadable/error accounts as valid switch candidates, cementing a fatal rotation deadlock in production.
ATTACK VECTOR: `test_soonest_reset_with_real_room_first_then_low_then_unknown` asserts that `blind@x.com` (`error="http 500"`) is returned in `rank()`. When `auto_tick` executes, it will switch to `blind@x.com`. On the subsequent tick, `blind@x.com` is active, and `test_unknown_active_usage_never_switches` (lines 156-159) dictates that any error on the active account halts switching (`reason: active-usage-unknown`). If an account has revoked tokens (401) or a network failure, the daemon switches into it, gets permanently wedged on `active-usage-unknown`, and never switches back to available quota or falls back to credits mode.
SUGGESTED FIX: Update `test_soonest_reset_with_real_room_first_then_low_then_unknown` to assert that accounts with errors (`usage.error is not None` / `life is None`) are excluded from candidate ranking rather than queued for switching.

---

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:248-257
ISSUE: Mock expects `subprocess.Popen` with `shell=True` to raise `FileNotFoundError`, testing an impossible failure path while hiding silent production failures.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_a_hook_that_cannot_start_is_reported_not_raised`, `Popen` is mocked to raise `FileNotFoundError("sh")`. In production, `run_after_switch` executes `subprocess.Popen(command, shell=True, ...)` which invokes `/bin/sh -c <command>`. On Unix systems, `/bin/sh` always exists, so `Popen` never raises `FileNotFoundError` even when the target script in `command` does not exist or is not executable. The subshell fails asynchronously in the background (exiting 127 into `DEVNULL`), while `run_after_switch` falsely returns `"started"`. The test validates an impossible exception path and masks the fact that hook failures fail completely silently.
SUGGESTED FIX: In the production code and tests, execute commands without `shell=True` (e.g. `shlex.split`) or resolve the binary via `shutil.which` before spawning so `FileNotFoundError` can actually be raised and handled when the command is missing.

ISSUE: `TestLogin` omits testing re-login/repair of the active account, hiding a bug where subsequent sync overwrites the new tokens with stale live data.
ATTACK VECTOR: `test_login_runs_in_a_throwaway_home_and_leaves_the_live_login` only tests logging in a distinct new account (`new@x.com` while live is `live@x.com`). In production, a common reason to run `cswap codex login` is repairing an expired or revoked live login. If `codex.login` only writes to the stored directory and leaves `live_auth_path()` untouched, the next command calling `sync_live()` will immediately copy the expired live tokens back over the fresh stored file, silently wiping out the login repair.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add a test verifying that when `codex.login` is performed for an account whose email matches the currently active live login, `live_auth_path()` is updated alongside the stored copy.

SEVERITY: WARNING

ISSUE: Test asserts that an unstored live login is not adopted, enabling irrevocable token destruction on switch.
ATTACK VECTOR: `test_an_unstored_live_login_is_not_adopted_silently` asserts that a live account not in `stored_accounts` is ignored by `sync_live`. When `codex.switch()` is called (or triggered by `auto_tick`), `_switch` calls `_sync_live(backup_root)` (which discards the unstored tokens) and immediately overwrites `live_auth_path()` with the target account. The only copy of `stray@x.com`'s OAuth tokens in `~/.codex/auth.json` is permanently destroyed—reproducing the exact data-loss scenario the project was created to prevent.

SEVERITY: WARNING

In production, ChatGPT/Codex plans enforce short windows (e.g. 5-hour limits) as well as weekly limits. Because `short_pct` is never populated across any `auto_tick` test, regressions where short-window usage fails to trigger a switch or is compared against the wrong threshold will pass CI completely unnoticed.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add a test in `TestAutoTick` where `weekly_pct` is low (e.g. 20%) but `short_pct` exceeds `threshold` (e.g. 96% >= 95.0%), asserting that `auto_tick` executes a switch.

ISSUE: Test mocks use raw dictionary indexing `by_email[codex.email_of(auth)]`, which crashes on unreadable or malformed auth payloads.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_at_the_weekly_threshold_switches` (and `_three` at line 271), `fetch_usage` is mocked as `lambda auth, timeout=20.0: by_email[codex.email_of(auth)]`. If `auth` contains invalid claims, corrupted tokens, or non-ChatGPT logins, `codex.email_of(auth)` returns `None`, crashing the mock with `KeyError: None`. This prevents tests from properly verifying how `auto_tick` and `usages_for` handle degraded, missing, or corrupted stored account files in production.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:120-129
ISSUE: Synthetic small integer `reset_at` values in tests mask timestamp handling and clock-skew edge cases.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_weekly_and_short_windows_are_told_apart_by_length`, `reset_at` values are `7` and `3`, and in `TestRank` they are `2000` and `5000`. In production, the `/wham/usage` endpoint returns absolute Unix epoch timestamps. Testing only small relative integers obscures bugs when sorting windows where an account's reset timestamp has already passed (`reset_at < time.time()`) vs future reset timestamps.
SUGGESTED FIX: Use realistic epoch timestamps (`time.time() + offset`) in tests and assert ranking behavior when one candidate has an expired reset timestamp in the past.

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py:31  
**ISSUE:** `_jwt` builds a JWT without the required Base64 padding (`=`) characters. Many JWT libraries (including the one used by *codex*) call `base64.urlsafe_b64decode` on the three parts and expect correctly‑padded strings; stripping the padding can raise `binascii.Error: Incorrect padding` for claim payloads whose length isn’t a multiple of 4.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In production the helper would generate a token that cannot be decoded, causing the login flow to abort or, worse, to fall back to a silent‑failure path that leaves stale credentials on disk.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Preserve padding when encoding, or re‑add it before decoding, e.g.:

```python
def _jwt(claims: dict) -> str:
    body = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(json.dumps(claims).encode()).decode()
    # keep padding (or add it back when decoding)
    return f"h.{body}.s"
```

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py:71  
**ISSUE:** `TestStore.test_add_stores_a_private_copy_keyed_by_email` feeds an email with mixed case (`"A@x.com"`) but asserts that the stored key is lower‑cased (`"a@x.com"`). The test never verifies that the lower‑casing actually happens inside `codex.add`; it merely trusts the implementation. If the real code later stops normalising case, the test will still pass, hiding a potential case‑sensitivity bug.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A user could create two distinct accounts `"A@x.com"` and `"a@x.com"` that the system treats as different, leading to duplicate credential files and possible credential leakage.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Explicitly assert the normalisation step, e.g.:

```python
email, plan = codex.add(env)
assert email == "a@x.com"          # ensure the function returns the normalised email
assert codex.stored_path(env, email).exists()
```

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py (multiple places – `TestRank`, `TestReserve`)  
**ISSUE:** The tests build a plain `dict` (`usages = { … }`) and then compare the exact order of the list returned by `codex.rank`. This assumes that `codex.rank` preserves the insertion order of the input dict or sorts deterministically. If the implementation ever switches to a set, a `dict.values()` iteration, or a different sorting key, the test will become flaky even though the logical ordering is correct.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In production, a change in the internal ranking algorithm that no longer respects dict order could go unnoticed because the test would start failing intermittently, masking a real regression.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Make the test order‑independent, e.g.:

```python
expected = {"soon@x.com", "late@x.com", "thin@x.com", "blind@x.com"}
assert set(codex.rank(usages, "active@x.com", 95.0, 99.0)) == expected
```

or explicitly sort the result before asserting.

**SEVERITY:** INFO

**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py:165‑176 (`TestLogin.test_login_runs_in_a_throwaway_home_and_leaves_the_live_login`)  
**ISSUE:** The fake subprocess (`fake_call`) writes `auth.json` directly into `env["CODEX_HOME"]` without first ensuring that the directory exists. The real `codex.login` creates a temporary directory via `tempfile.mkdtemp` (or similar). If the production code changes to a stricter creation path, this test will not catch a failure to create the directory, giving a false sense of correctness.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In production a missing temporary directory would raise an unhandled `FileNotFoundError`, aborting the login flow and leaving the live credentials unchanged.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Mirror the real creation logic in the fake call:

```python
def fake_call(argv, env):
    seen["argv"], seen["home"] = argv, env["CODEX_HOME"]
    os.makedirs(seen["home"], exist_ok=True)
    with open(os.path.join(seen["home"], "auth.json"), "w") as fh:
        json.dump(_auth("new@x.com"), fh)
    return 0
```

**SEVERITY:** INFO
