**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py:191‑196 (`TestLogin.test_an_aborted_login_stores_nothing`)  
**ISSUE:** The test checks that `codex.stored_accounts(env) == []` after a failed login, but it never verifies that the temporary `CODEX_HOME` directory created for the login attempt is removed. A leak of that directory would consume disk space over many runs.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In long‑running CI pipelines or production scripts that repeatedly invoke `codex.login`, orphaned temporary directories could accumulate, eventually exhausting disk space.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** After the `with pytest.raises` block, assert that the temporary directory does not exist:

```python
with pytest.raises(codex.CodexError):
    codex.login(env)
assert not any(p.is_dir() for p in Path(os.getenv("CODEX_HOME")).parent.iterdir()
           if p.name.startswith("tmp"))
```

or expose the temporary path from `codex.login` for cleanup verification.

**SEVERITY:** WARNING

**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py:260‑267 (`TestCredits.test_a_spend_cap_takes_an_account_out_of_the_credits_pool`)  
**ISSUE:** The helper `_cr` sets `credits_usable` to `True` by default, but the test expects the account with `usable=False` to be excluded. The test does not explicitly verify that the `usable` flag is honoured by `codex.auto_tick`; it merely relies on the outcome. If the implementation later ignores `credits_usable`, the test would still pass because the “biggest balance” happens to be the same account.

```python
event = codex.auto_tick(env, 95.0, 99.0)

```

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py:285‑291 (`TestCredits.test_a_week_that_resets_brings_it_back_off_credits`)  
**ISSUE:** The test mutates the `by` dict after the first `auto_tick` call to simulate a week reset, but it does not reset the mock (`fetch_usage`) to reflect the changed data. Because `monkeypatch.setattr` binds the lambda to the original `by` dict, the mutation is visible, but this relies on the mutable capture semantics of closures. If the test were refactored to use a new dict or a function that copies the mapping, the mutation would be lost, making the test brittle.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Future refactors could unintentionally break the simulation of a week reset, allowing a regression where the system never falls back from credits.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use a mutable container that the mock reads each time, or re‑patch after mutation:

```python
by = {"a@x.com": _cr(100, 30000), ...}
def usage_lookup(auth):
    return by[codex.email_of(auth)]

monkeypatch.setattr(codex, "fetch_usage", usage_lookup)

# after first tick
by["a@x.com"] = _cr(0, 30000)   # mutate in‑place – still works
# or re‑patch if a new dict is created
```

###   PROVIDER: CLAUDE

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:52 (`test_the_live_login_overrides_its_stored_copy`) and :88 (`test_switch_saves_the_rotated_live_token_before_replacing_it`)
ISSUE: The `0o600` check runs only after `add()`. The tests that rewrite the stored copy (`sync_live`, `switch`) never re-check its mode.
ATTACK VECTOR: If `sync_live` or `switch` rewrites the stored token file with plain `write_text`, the file gets the default umask (usually 0644). The rotated refresh token then becomes readable by other local users. The suite stays green because no test checks permissions on those paths.
SUGGESTED FIX: After `sync_live` and `switch` in those two tests, assert `stat.S_IMODE(os.stat(path).st_mode) == 0o600`. In the code, write through `os.open(..., 0o600)` or call `chmod` on every write.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:253 (`TestAfterSwitch`, `started.append((cmd, ...))`)
ISSUE: The hook is always a single token (`"restart-codex"`), and the test asserts only the `CSWAP_CODEX_*` env keys. It never checks argv-vs-shell handling or that the rest of the environment is inherited.
ATTACK VECTOR: `after_switch` is a command string. If it is passed to `Popen` without `shell=True` (or with `env=` replacing `os.environ`), a real hook like `systemctl restart codex-daemon` fails with `FileNotFoundError`, or the child loses `PATH`/`HOME`. The auto-switch still happens, but the restart silently never does, and every test passes.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add a case with a multi-word hook (for example `"sh -c 'echo $CSWAP_CODEX_TO'"`) that asserts the command ran. Assert that `PATH` is present in `kw["env"]`.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:278 (`test_login_runs_in_a_throwaway_home...`) and :298 (`test_an_aborted_login_stores_nothing`)
ISSUE: Abort is covered only as a non-zero return code. The temp-home cleanup is asserted only on the success path, and there is no test for an exception raised from the subprocess.
ATTACK VECTOR: A Ctrl-C during `codex login --device-auth` raises `KeyboardInterrupt`, not a return code of 1. If cleanup happens only after a normal return, the throwaway `CODEX_HOME` with fresh tokens stays on disk. The aborted-login test also never checks that the live login is untouched.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add a test where the fake `call` raises `KeyboardInterrupt`. Assert the temp dir is gone and `live_auth_path()` is unchanged. Put the cleanup in a `try/finally` or a context manager.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:157 (`test_below_the_weekly_threshold_stays`) and :163 (`test_at_the_weekly_threshold_switches`)
ISSUE: `auto_tick(env, 95.0, 99.0)` takes two unnamed floats, and the test names don't match the values. A usage of 97 is above 95 but is named "below the weekly threshold". Only the 99 threshold is what the name refers to.
ATTACK VECTOR: A reader or future maintainer can't tell which float is the switch threshold and which is the candidate-room threshold. Swapping them, or changing a default, can make a test pass for the wrong reason.
SUGGESTED FIX: Use keyword arguments in the tests (`switch_at=99.0, room_below=95.0`) or rename the tests to state the threshold they mean (for example "under the 99% switch threshold").

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:46 (`test_an_unstored_live_login_is_not_adopted_silently`)
ISSUE: `sync_live` returns the stray email even though it does not adopt the login. The return value reads as "synced", while the stored list stays empty.
ATTACK VECTOR: A caller that displays or logs the return value of `sync_live` reports an account as synced when nothing was stored. A later `switch` to that email then fails, and the cause is not obvious.
SUGGESTED FIX: Return `None` (or a tuple that says "unadopted") when the login is not stored, and assert that in this test.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: low
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:172 (`test_unknown_active_usage_never_switches`) and :387 (`test_an_empty_credits_account_hands_over...`)
ISSUE: The unknown-usage test checks only the reason string and not that the live login stayed on `a@x.com`. The empty-credits test uses `10.0` with no boundary cases, so the "empty" threshold is undefined.
ATTACK VECTOR: If the reason is reported correctly but a switch still happens, the test passes. A change to the empty floor (for example from 10 to 1000) goes unnoticed.
SUGGESTED FIX: Assert `codex.email_of(codex.read_auth(codex.live_auth_path())) == "a@x.com"` in the unknown-usage test. Add a boundary pair (just above and just below the floor) to the empty-credits test.

SEVERITY: WARNING

FILE: tests/test_codex.py
ISSUE: `Popen` doubles return `None`, hiding unreaped process handles
ATTACK VECTOR: `TestAfterSwitch` and `TestCredits._three` stub `Popen` with lambdas returning `None` (`started.append(...)` returns `None`). Code that never calls `wait()`/`poll()`, never closes pipes, or leaks a zombie passes. In a ticking daemon this grows fds/zombies unboundedly.
SUGGESTED FIX: Return a dummy handle with `wait/poll` and assert it is reaped, or spawn a real short-lived process.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_codex.py
ISSUE: `0o600` check is post-hoc `stat`, cannot detect insecure creation or symlink swap
ATTACK VECTOR: `stat.S_IMODE(os.stat(path).st_mode) == 0o600` follows symlinks and runs after the fact. A write-then-`chmod` window (file briefly `0o644` under umask `022`) or a symlink at the stored path swapping in an attacker file both pass. Refresh tokens are briefly world-readable or written to the wrong path.
SUGGESTED FIX: Create with `os.open(O_CREAT|O_EXCL, 0o600)`, `lstat` instead of `stat`, and test under umask `022` with a pre-planted symlink expecting failure.

FILE: tests/test_codex.py

ATTACK VECTOR: All fixtures use `_login(_auth(...))` with valid JSON, valid `id_token`, present `email` claim. One corrupt stored copy can crash `add/sync_live/switch/rank` with an unhandled exception instead of `CodexError`, blocking rotation when it is most needed.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add tests for corrupt live JSON, missing `id_token`, and email-less JWT, expecting `CodexError`.

SEVERITY: WARNING

SUGGESTED FIX: Add a case where `fetch_usage` raises for one non-active account and assert the tick degrades to error-`Usage` handling rather than propagating.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_codex.py
ISSUE: Rank/reset tests use toy 1970-epoch timestamps with no frozen clock
ATTACK VECTOR: Resets are `100/2000/5000` and `7/3` — all far in the past versus a 2026 wall clock. If `rank`/`life` compute `reset_at - now`, every fixture is expired, yet ordering still passes. Expired-vs-future handling, ms-vs-seconds confusion, and clock skew are invisible; production future resets may rank differently.
SUGGESTED FIX: Use realistic future `reset_at` values with a frozen `time.time()` plus one explicitly expired reset.

Adversarial review of the provided artifact only (chunk 3/3). Findings sorted by severity, then confidence.

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** scripts/deploy-hosts.sh (REMOTE block, move then `uv tool install`)  
**ISSUE:** Old install tree is removed before `uv tool install` succeeds; there is no rollback.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `mv` succeeds and `uv tool install` fails (disk full, network, bad package). The host loses the previous `~/.local/src/cswap` tree, the script exits before `systemctl restart`, and long‑running daemons keep old in‑memory behavior while disk state is already on the new (possibly broken) tree—manual recovery with no automatic revert.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Install/verify first (e.g. unpack to `cswap.new`, run `uv tool install` against that path or a symlink swap), then atomically replace `cswap` and restart; or keep the previous directory as `cswap.old` until install + health check pass.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** launchd/com.greglas.cswap-hub-status.plist:11–12 + scripts/hub-status-push.py:59–61, 91–93  
**ISSUE:** LaunchAgent fires every 60s while `collect()` allows up to 90s per target (four parallel SSH/local runs)—runs can overlap with no guard.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Two hub-status processes run concurrently → doubled SSH/`sudo -u gha` load on CI hosts, parallel `cswap list` during switches, duplicated hub PUTs, and stdout/stderr interleaved into one log file; under load this can look like flaky hosts or amplify keychain/SSH rate limits.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Set `ThrottleInterval` / use a lock file (e.g. `flock`) in the script, or set `StartInterval` ≥ worst-case runtime (e.g. 120–180s), or skip if previous PID still running.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/switcher.py (json_output branch, `except Exception: pass`)  
**ISSUE:** JSON `order` / `note` enrichment swallows all exceptions and returns a payload as if enrichment succeeded.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Transient settings/load/resolve failures (or a future bug in `_in_switch_order`) produce hub/CLI JSON without `order`/`note` and no signal; the live status UI can show slot order that disagrees with auto-switch while consumers assume new fields exist.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Log at warning with `exc_info=True`; optionally set a top-level `"order_meta": {"error": "..."}` or re-raise for `--json` when strictness is desired.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** scripts/hub-status-push.py:37–45  
**ISSUE:** `parse()` takes the first `{` in combined shell output and `json.loads` the suffix—fragile boundary parsing.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** MOTD, shell profile, or `cswap`/wrapper stderr merged into stdout (or a line containing `{` before the real JSON) yields wrong object, partial parse failure, or wrong account snapshot pushed to the hub while SSH exit code is still 0.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Require marker lines (as with `@@CODEX@@`), read only the last JSON object, or fail unless `returncode == 0` and output matches a strict prefix/schema.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** scripts/cswap-ci:38–41  
**ISSUE:** Existing rotation is discovered via `cswap list` with `2>/dev/null`, so SSH/sudo/cswap failures look like an empty rotation.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Host unreachable or `as_gha` broken → `have` empty → `add-claude` attempts full interactive login for every email, wasting operator time and risking duplicate/conflicting sessions on a host that already had accounts.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Check `as_gha` exit status; if non-zero, abort with a clear error instead of treating stderr as “no accounts”.

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** scripts/hub-status-push.py:59–71  
**ISSUE:** `collect()` ignores `subprocess.run` return code and does not treat non-zero `cswap` as failure if stdout still parses as JSON.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Partial failure (old JSON cached in wrapper, or mixed success stderr) uploads misleading quota/order to the hub; operators trust the page while CI/Mac state is wrong.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** If `p.returncode != 0`, set `errors["host"]` and avoid treating parsed JSON as authoritative unless return code is 0 (or validate schema/version field).

**SEVERITY:** WARNING

**FILE:** src/claude_swap/switcher.py:503–510 vs 7050–7056  
**ISSUE:** `_read_credentials_settled` retries only on empty string `""`, not on `None`; switch rollback snapshot uses this settled read without covering the None case the same way.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** If the store returns `None` transiently (same class of Keychain/store glitches as `""`), switch aborts immediately with `CredentialReadError` instead of retrying—production still sees the “empty read” failure mode the comment describes, but on a different return shape.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Retry when `creds is None or creds == ""` (with a cap), or document and enforce a single sentinel for “unsettled read” from `_read_credentials()`.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5452-5467](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/switcher.py#L5452-L5467)
ISSUE: Hardcoded weekly reset ranking contradicts default `"best"` switching strategy, showing wrong next accounts.
ATTACK VECTOR: The auto-switch daemon defaults to the `"best"` strategy (prioritizing maximum remaining headroom, `-life`), whereas `consume-first` ranks by earliest weekly reset (`_seven_day_reset_ts`). In `_in_switch_order`, candidate sorting unconditionally uses `(first, life < MIN_USEFUL_LIFE_PCT, reset, -life)`. A freshly seeded account with 100% headroom has no weekly reset recorded (`reset = None`), so it is assigned `reset = float("inf")` and ranked behind nearly-exhausted accounts nearing weekly reset. Consequently, `cswap list` and JSON `order` tag an expiring account as `(next)`, but when threshold limits are triggered, the daemon activates the 100% headroom account instead.
SUGGESTED FIX: Respect `settings.strategy`: sort by `(first, -life)` when `strategy == "best"` and `home` prefer-mode is inactive, using the reset tuple only when `by_reset` is active.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5438-5450](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/switcher.py#L5438-L5450)
ISSUE: Reserve account check shadows `life <= 0`, assigning `(reserve — last resort)` to an exhausted reserve account.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_in_switch_order`, `elif num == reserve:` is evaluated before `elif life <= 0:`. If the reserve account's short-term (5-hour) headroom is completely exhausted (`life <= 0`) while its 7-day quota still clears `reserve_min_life_pct`, it enters the reserve branch instead of being routed to `unusable`. It is added to `reserve_rows` with `(reserve — last resort)` rather than `(exhausted)`. In addition, if all standard accounts are exhausted and `ranked` is empty, the reserve account is never marked as `(next)`. In production, operators and automation see an exhausted reserve advertised as an available fallback.
SUGGESTED FIX: Check `elif life <= 0:` before checking `elif num == reserve:`, or ensure `life > 0` before appending to `reserve_rows`.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5283-5295](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/switcher.py#L5283-L5295)
ISSUE: Sequential `order` indices assigned to permanently unusable, dead, and disabled accounts.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `json_output`, `rank` maps `{str(row[0]): i for i, row in enumerate(ordered)}`, where `ordered` concatenates `active + [ranked] + reserve_rows + unusable`. Disabled accounts, dead credentials, and exhausted accounts in `unusable` are assigned valid integer `order` ranks (e.g., 3, 4, 5). Any external dashboard or API consumer reading `acc["order"]` (such as the Cloudflare status hub) cannot differentiate viable rotational candidates from disabled or broken accounts, presenting dead accounts as upcoming in the rotation sequence.
SUGGESTED FIX: Only map `order` for accounts in `[row for _, row in ranked]` and `reserve_rows`; leave `acc["order"] = None` for rows in `unusable`.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [scripts/deploy-hosts.sh:34,41-43](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/scripts/deploy-hosts.sh#L34-L43)
ISSUE: `ctl()` executes `systemctl --user` in a non-login subshell without `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, failing on remote user hosts.
ATTACK VECTOR: When `deploy_host` connects to `ryzen-dev` via non-interactive SSH, the executing shell is not a login shell. While `run()` wraps commands in `bash -lc`, `ctl()` is defined directly as `systemctl --user "$@"`. Without an active login session or explicit `export XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/user/$(id -u)`, `systemctl --user cat` fails to connect to the user D-Bus daemon (`Failed to connect to bus`). As a result, `units` remains empty and deployment fails unconditionally with `FAIL no cswap units here`.
SUGGESTED FIX: Define `ctl()` for user mode to ensure `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` is exported, e.g.:
```bash
ctl() { XDG_RUNTIME_DIR="/run/user/$(id -u)" systemctl --user "$@"; }
```

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [scripts/hub-status-push.py:25,66-68](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/scripts/hub-status-push.py#L25-L68)
ISSUE: `2>/dev/null` in `BOTH` strips stderr, making `doc["errors"]["claude"]` unable to report failure causes.
ATTACK VECTOR: `BOTH` discards stderr (`cswap list --json 2>/dev/null`). If `cswap` fails due to Python import errors, lock contention, or syntax exceptions, all traceback data is discarded. In `collect()`, `doc["errors"]["claude"] = (p.stderr.strip().splitlines() or ["no output from cswap list --json"])[-1]` will always see an empty `p.stderr` and fall back to the generic string. The dashboard is completely blinded to the root cause of failures across the fleet.
SUGGESTED FIX: Remove `2>/dev/null` from `BOTH` and instead capture stderr cleanly in `subprocess.run(capture_output=True)`.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [scripts/hub-status-push.py:73-75,98](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/scripts/hub-status-push.py#L73-L98)
ISSUE: Unhandled `subprocess.CalledProcessError` in `owner_password()` crashes the service after lengthy network collection.
ATTACK VECTOR: `token = owner_password()` runs after all remote hosts have been queried (taking up to 90 seconds). `subprocess.run(["security", ...], check=True)` raises `CalledProcessError` if the keychain is locked or the secret is missing. Because the call is unhandled in `main()`, any keychain retrieval issue crashes the LaunchAgent after wasting up to 90 seconds of network I/O, writing uncaught tracebacks to `/Users/greglas/Library/Logs/cswap-hub-status.log` every 60 seconds.
SUGGESTED FIX: Query `owner_password()` before querying hosts, and catch `CalledProcessError`/`Exception` early to log a clean error and exit.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: [src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5291-5293](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/switcher.py#L5291-L5293)
ISSUE: Bare `except Exception: pass` silently suppresses ordering calculation failures in JSON output.

The resulting JSON payload is returned with missing `order` and `note` fields without any debug logging or diagnostic trace, concealing regressions from test harnesses and status push scripts.

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** scripts/hub-status-push.py:45  
**ISSUE:** `owner_password()` calls `subprocess.run(..., check=True)` and lets `CalledProcessError` propagate. If the macOS keychain entry is missing or the `security` command fails, the whole script aborts, stopping the LaunchAgent and leaving the hub without updates.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A missing or expired keychain item (common after OS upgrades or user password changes) silently kills the periodic status push, causing stale data on the public hub.

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** scripts/deploy-hosts.sh:30  
**ISSUE:** The remote deployment script obtains the install timestamp with `installed=$(stat -c %Y "$H/.local/share/uv/tools/claude-swap/uv-receipt.toml")` but does not verify that `stat` succeeded. If the receipt file is absent, `installed` is empty, causing the later numeric test `[ "$started" -ge "$installed" ]` to fail with a “integer expression expected” error and mark the host as FAILED.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A fresh host (or one where the UV tool was never installed) will always be reported as a failed deployment, preventing legitimate roll‑outs.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Check the exit status of `stat` and abort with a clear message if the file is missing, or set `installed=0` and adjust the comparison logic accordingly.

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/switcher.py:520  
**ISSUE:** `_read_credentials_settled()` treats an empty string (`""`) as “retry‑able” but returns it after the retry loop. Downstream code interprets any non‑`None` return as a credential blob, so an empty string can be passed to the credential store as if it were valid, leading to authentication failures that are hard to trace.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** On macOS where the Keychain temporarily returns an empty string, the switcher may think it has read a credential, attempt to use it, and repeatedly fail to switch accounts.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** After the retry loop, return `None` when the final value is empty (`if creds == "": return None`). Adjust callers to treat `None` as “no credential”.

---

**SEVERITY:** HIGH  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** scripts/cswap-ci:15  
**ISSUE:** `as_gha()` builds the remote command with `ssh … "sudo -u gha -H bash -lc $(printf %q "$2")"`. The `%q` quoting is performed **locally**, then the result is inserted into a double‑quoted remote string. If `$2` contains single quotes, newlines, or other shell‑special characters, the remote command can be malformed or, in the worst case, allow command injection.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A malicious email address or malformed argument supplied to `cswap-ci add-claude` could cause the remote host to execute unintended commands as `gha`.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Pass the command to `ssh` via `bash -lc` with proper quoting on the remote side, e.g.:

```bash
ssh "$host" "sudo -u gha -H bash -lc $(printf '%q' "$2")"
```

or better, avoid local expansion entirely:

```bash
ssh "$host" sudo -u gha -H bash -lc "$2"
```

and rely on `ssh`’s argument handling.

---

**SEVERITY:** HIGH  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** scripts/hub-status-push.py:70  
**ISSUE:** `collect()` runs the combined `cswap` commands and only captures `stdout`. Errors from `cswap` (including JSON printed to `stderr` or a non‑zero exit status) are ignored, so a failure can be silently interpreted as “no data” and later masked by generic error messages.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** If `cswap` crashes or outputs malformed JSON to `stderr`, the script will store `None` for that host without a clear indication, leading to stale or missing status entries on the hub.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Capture both `stdout` and `stderr`; if `p.returncode != 0` or `stderr` is non‑empty, record a distinct error and skip parsing. Example:

```python
p = subprocess.run(argv, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=90)
if p.returncode != 0:
    doc["errors"]["host"] = f"cswap exited {p.returncode}: {p.stderr.strip()}"
    return doc
```

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** scripts/deploy-hosts.sh:120  
**ISSUE:** The remote deployment output is piped through `tail -1`, discarding all but the last line. If the remote script prints multiple informative lines (e.g., error diagnostics), they are lost, making troubleshooting difficult and potentially causing the local script to mis‑interpret a multi‑line success as a failure.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** During a partial rollout, a host may emit an error followed by a “OK” line; `tail -1` would keep the “OK”, hiding the earlier failure.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Capture the full output, store it in a variable, and use the last line only for the summary while preserving the full log for debugging, e.g.:

```bash
output=$(ssh …)
summary=$(printf '%s\n' "$output" | tail -1)
echo "$summary"
# optionally log "$output" to a debug file
```

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5400  
**ISSUE:** `_in_switch_order()` wraps the entire ranking logic in a broad `try/except Exception: return accounts_info, {}`. Any unexpected exception (e.g., a typo, a new field in `settings`, or a failure in `oauth.account_headroom`) is silently ignored, yielding an empty `notes` dict and potentially an incorrectly ordered list without any indication of the underlying problem.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Silent loss of ranking information can mislead operators about which account will be used next, especially after a code change that introduces a new exception path.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Narrow the exception handling to only anticipated errors (e.g., missing settings) and log unexpected exceptions. For example:

```python
except (KeyError, ValueError) as e:
    self._logger.debug("Ranking fallback: %s", e)
    return accounts_info, {}
except Exception as e:
    self._logger.error("Unexpected error in _in_switch_order", exc_info=True)
    raise
```

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: scripts/deploy-hosts.sh
ISSUE: Remote install deletes live code before the replacement is known-good
ATTACK VECTOR: `rm -rf ~/.local/src/cswap && mv ~/.local/src/cswap.new/cswap ...` runs as two steps; if the `mv` fails (corrupt/truncated tarball over ssh, disk full, interrupted connection) the host is left with no `cswap` tree, and the subsequent `uv tool install` and daemon restart then fail on an already-bricked host.
SUGGESTED FIX: Rename atomically: move live aside (`mv cswap cswap.old`), move new into place, only `rm -rf cswap.old` after the move succeeds; abort without touching live on unpack/move failure.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: scripts/hub-status-push.py
ISSUE: Single keychain read aborts all machines' uploads despite per-machine fault isolation
ATTACK VECTOR: `token = owner_password()` runs after collection with `check=True` and no `try`; a transient keychain lock (screen lock/sleep, exactly when a 1-minute LaunchAgent fires) raises `CalledProcessError` and discards successfully collected docs for all four hosts, contradicting the "one machine's failure never stops the others" handling in the send loop.
SUGGESTED FIX: Wrap the `owner_password()` call in `try/except subprocess.CalledProcessError`, log to stderr, and `return 1` without traceback.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/switcher.py
ISSUE: Bare `except Exception: pass` around JSON `order`/`note` augmentation silently drops fields
ATTACK VECTOR: If `_in_switch_order`, `load_settings`, or the private `autoswitch` import fails in production (renamed engine constant, corrupt settings, resolver error), the JSON payload returns without `order`/`note` while the live status page keeps rendering stale ordering with no error signal; failures are undebuggable because nothing is logged.
SUGGESTED FIX: Replace `pass` with `self._logger.debug(..., exc_info=True)` so the payload still returns but the failure is recorded.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: scripts/cswap-ci
ISSUE: `have` computation swallows ssh failure and final verification never sets `rc`
ATTACK VECTOR: `have=$(as_gha ... 2>/dev/null | ...)` ignores ssh exit status, so a down/unreachable host yields empty `have` and every email is treated as "not in rotation", prompting interactive `claude auth login` logins that churn tokens; additionally the trailing `cswap list | grep ...` check does not affect `rc`, so a failed verification still exits 0.
SUGGESTED FIX: Check `as_gha` exit status before trusting empty `have` (skip host with `rc=1`), and `|| rc=1` the final list pipe.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: scripts/hub-status-push.py
ISSUE: `parse()` takes the first `{` in stdout, so a shell banner containing `{` poisons JSON parsing
ATTACK VECTOR: The code comment admits login shells may print banners; `text.find("{")` then `json.loads(text[i:])` fails with `ValueError` when the banner (MOTD, fortune, shell init) contains a brace, marking a healthy machine as "no output", keeping a stale hub document and misreporting the error as a `cswap` failure.
SUGGESTED FIX: Try each `{` offset in turn (or search for the known payload start) and accept the first offset that `json.loads` succeeds on.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: launchd/com.greglas.cswap-hub-status.plist
ISSUE: 60s `StartInterval` with no overlap guard races 90s collect + 30s PUT timeouts
ATTACK VECTOR: `StartInterval=60` does not wait for the prior run; a slow round (hung ssh within the 90s `subprocess` timeout) overlaps the next run, and since the hub keeps last-writer-wins with no timestamp comparison, the older run's delayed `PUT` can overwrite the newer run's fresh document, regressing the page to stale data.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add `ThrottleInterval` and a local lock (`flock -n` around the script, exit quietly if held) or skip a round when the previous instance is still alive.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py:31
ISSUE: `latest_session_activity_ts(stop_at=...)` returns an arbitrary file's timestamp instead of the newest, causing premature account swaps.
ATTACK VECTOR: When multiple transcript files are active within the quiet window, directory traversal order (filesystem-dependent) can cause early exit on an older file (e.g. touched 55s ago) rather than the most recent one (e.g. touched 1s ago). When `_session_quiet()` computes remaining wait time or caches this timestamp, it falsely calculates that activity ended 55s ago. After just 5s, the engine concludes the quiet window has elapsed and switches accounts while Claude is actively writing to a session.
SUGGESTED FIX: If early exit is purely an activity check, return a boolean flag. If returning a timestamp, do not treat an early-exit timestamp as the true latest activity timestamp for wait calculations or caching in `_session_quiet`.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py:49
ISSUE: Optimization failure: Full 9,500+ file scan still executes on every valid account switch.
ATTACK VECTOR: The module docstring targets eliminating a 12.5s bottleneck (`quietScanMs=12546` across 9,530 files). However, `test_all_old_transcripts_need_the_full_scan_and_report_the_newest` mandates that when all files are older than `stop_at`, the function must scan every file to find the newest. Because a switch *requires* the system to be quiet, every actual switch path only contains old files, forcing a full 9,530-file scan. Coupled with `test_force_bypasses_the_cache` showing `_perform` passes `force=True`, the entire 12.5s disk scan still runs synchronously on every swap.
SUGGESTED FIX: When `stop_at` is provided and no file exceeds the cutoff, return `None` (or early-exit with `< stop_at`) rather than scanning the entire historical directory tree.

FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py
ISSUE: `test_one_scan_serves_the_whole_tick` locks in negative (`None`) caching for the whole tick
ATTACK VECTOR: First scan returns `None` (no activity), cached; a transcript written immediately after is invisible to the next two non-force `_latest_session_activity()` calls in the same tick. SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py
ISSUE: Fixture is a flat directory; production scan is recursive over projects dir with symlinks/nesting
ATTACK VECTOR: Docstring states the slow path stats every `*.jsonl` under the projects dir (9530 files / 10.7 GB). Flat-dir fixtures don't exercise recursion, broken symlinks, symlink loops, permission-denied subdirs, or `stat` vs `lstat` handling, so a production-only crash/slowdown or double-count stays untested.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add one test with a nested subdir + symlink (including a broken symlink) asserting same no-raise and correct newest-timestamp behavior.


###############################################################
###   PROVIDER: QWEN
###############################################################

Reviewing the test artifact only. Findings below are ordered by severity, then confidence.

---

**SEVERITY: WARNING | CONFIDENCE: high**
**FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py:31-39** (and `:49-54`)
**ISSUE:** The early-exit test cannot fail for a full-scan implementation — the performance regression that motivated the entire change is unguarded.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `transcripts()` writes `a=10s, b=5s, c=99999s` and the only assertion is `got >= stop_at`. A production implementation that scans all 9530 files / 10.7 GB and returns the true newest (`b`) satisfies this assertion identically to one that stops at the first in-window hit. `test_all_old_transcripts_need_the_full_scan_and_report_the_newest` likewise only inspects the return value, never the work done. Someone reverts the cutoff (or reorders traversal so the newest file is always visited last) and CI stays green while every tick goes back to 12.5 s — exactly the failure the docstring says was measured in production.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Assert on work, not just result. Wrap the traversal primitive the module uses (`os.scandir`/`Path.glob`/`os.stat`) in `claude_swap.autoswitch` with a counting shim and assert `visits <= N` for a fixture with dozens of entries; or cheaper: make the *last* visited entry a `boom.jsonl` whose `stat()` raises (broken symlink) and assert the call returns — that only passes if traversal stopped before it.

**SEVERITY: WARNING | CONFIDENCE: high**
**FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py:59-64**
**ISSUE:** `test_an_unreadable_entry_never_raises` does not create an unreadable entry; it creates a file the scanner is expected to filter out, and its assertion can't detect a filter regression either.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `not-a-transcript.txt` is a *non-matching* path, not an error path. The cases that actually raise in production — `EACCES` from a `chmod 000` project dir, a dangling `*.jsonl` symlink (session rotated out mid-write), a directory named `x.jsonl`, and a file unlinked between `scandir()` and `stat()` — are all untested, yet "never raises" is precisely the contract for a scanner walking a live `~/.claude/projects` tree. Second-order bug: the `.txt` file is written *after* `a` was backdated, so it is the newest entry; if extension filtering ever breaks, `got` becomes the `.txt` mtime and `assert got is not None` still passes.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add a broken symlink and a `<name>.jsonl` directory to the fixture; assert with `pytest.approx` that the returned ts is `a`'s mtime (so a filter leak fails loudly); add a `chmod 000` case guarded by `pytest.skipif(os.geteuid() == 0)`, and cover the *existing-but-empty* dir separately (`:56-57` only covers a missing dir, a different code path).

**SEVERITY: WARNING | CONFIDENCE: high**
**FILE: tests/test_switcher.py:8458-8466**
**ISSUE:** "carries switch order without resorting" is unverifiable as written — switch order, insertion order, and numeric order are all identical in the fixture.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `_seed(s, 1, ...)` then `_seed(s, 2, ...)` makes the expected sequence `1, 2` reachable by three different (one correct, two buggy) code paths: preserve switch order, preserve insertion order, or `sorted(by="number")`. A regression that re-sorts the JSON payload numerically — which is the exact behavior the docstring says breaks the live status page, since the page renders the text list's order — passes. The docstring also claims the payload carries "order **and notes**"; nothing asserts `notes`, so dropped/blank notes in `json_output=True` go undetected.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Seed out of numeric order (`_seed(s, 2, ...)` before `_seed(s, 1, ...)`) or seed then mutate the stored switch order so `numbers == [2, 1]`, and assert the exact list. Add an `assert "notes" in payload["accounts"][0]` / expected-note check, or retitle the docstring to the single claim tested.

**SEVERITY: WARNING | CONFIDENCE: high**
**FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py:9-19, :31-39 vs :70-90**
**ISSUE:** `latest_session_activity_ts(stop_at=...)` has two contradictory contracts under one "latest" name, and the test canonizes the ambiguity.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The comment at `:36-37` states outright that with a cutoff the return value is "whatever it returns ... inside the window" — i.e. an arbitrary entry ≥ cutoff, traversal-order-dependent, not the maximum. But `_session_quiet()` (`:92-105`) consumes that value to build a `detail` string containing "waits", i.e. it is rendered as a *duration estimate* for a human. With early exit, `detail` will report the wait implied by whichever file happened to be scanned first, and the number will shift run-to-run on the same directory state while the boolean verdict stays right. The sibling test at `:41-47` promises the opposite semantics (true newest) for `stop_at=None`, so the function's meaning flips on an optional argument.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Split the intent: either return a tri-state (`None` / `bool in_window`) plus a separate `latest_session_activity_ts()` for reporting, or keep one function and have `_session_quiet` not derive the "waits" figure from an early-exited value. At minimum, document the `stop_at` contract on the function and assert `got <= true_max` plus a fixed traversal in the test.

**SEVERITY: WARNING | CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py:84-85**
**ISSUE:** `assert calls[0] is not None` validates only that *some* cutoff was passed, and the fixture mixes a fake engine clock with wall-clock mtimes.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Any truthy value passes — `0.0`, a cutoff an hour in the future, or one computed from the wrong constant. A future-dated cutoff makes every scan early-exit with "no activity", which reads as permanently quiet, which is how you swap accounts while a session is mid-write; the test still goes green. The clock sources compound this: the TTL assertion at `:87` advances `h.clock`, while `transcripts()` backdates mtimes with `time.time()` and the pure-scan tests use `time.time()`. So the production comparison (wall-clock mtimes vs a `h.clock`-derived cutoff) is never exercised under the fake clock at all, and after `h.clock.advance(...)` the two clocks diverge for the rest of the test.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Assert the value: `assert calls[0] == pytest.approx(h.clock.now - QUIET_WINDOW_S, abs=1.0)`. Have `transcripts()` take `now` from the same clock the engine uses (or assert the engine passes a cutoff derived from its own injected clock, not `time.time()`).

**SEVERITY: WARNING | CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py:70-90**
**ISSUE:** Cache tests exercise only the cheapest possible scan result and only one directory, so two plausible cache defects pass.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The stub returns `None`, so "cached" means a falsy sentinel. If the implementation stores the result in an attribute/dict and tests validity with truthiness (`if cached: return cached`) or `dict.get(key)`, `None` never counts as cached — but here `len(calls) == 1` is checked against a stub whose own return is what's cached, so the sentinel handling that matters for real `None`/`0.0` mtimes is not pinned down. Separately, every call uses one directory: a cache keyed on the clock only (not on the scanned path) would serve project A's scan for project B, silently reporting another directory's activity as the quiet verdict.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add `h.engine._latest_session_activity(root=dirA)` then `... (root=dirB)` and assert two scans (if the API takes a path); and re-run `test_one_scan_serves_the_whole_tick` with the stub returning a real timestamp so a truthy-vs-sentinel cache bug is distinguishable.

**SEVERITY: INFO | CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py:78-80, 96-99, 120-131**
**ISSUE:** Three copies of manual module-global patch + `try/finally` restore, with a hand-rolled stub signature that no test pins.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Assigning `mod.latest_session_activity_ts` mutates a process-global for the whole `claude_swap` module; if the autoswitch engine runs a timer/watch thread (the class name and `_latest_session_activity()`/tick framing imply one), that thread sees the stub for the duration of the `try` and can consume a cache slot or append to `calls`, making `len(calls)` non-deterministic under load or `-p xdist` timing. The stubs also encode the call convention implicitly (`def counting(_dir, *, stop_at=None)`) — a production switch to a positional or extra keyword arg raises `TypeError` rather than a clear assertion, and the same convention is asserted nowhere.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use pytest's `monkeypatch.setattr(mod, "latest_session_activity_ts", counting)` (auto-restored, no `try/finally`), and add one assertion on the call signature (e.g. record `(args, kwargs)` instead of only `stop_at`).

Adversarial review of **part auto1** only (`src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py`). Findings are limited to what the diff shows; session-cache **consumption** may live in a later hunk—not scored here.

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~1935`  
**ISSUE:** Prefer-mode “blind home” trust treats `last.age_s is None` as always fresh, bypassing `TRUST_MAX_AGE_S`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** After a weekly reset (or long idle), `last_good` can still describe the **previous** window while Keychain/usage is unreadable. With `age_s is None`, `last_h` stays valid, `last_h < _home_return_margin(settings)` fires, and `active_headroom` is forced to `100.0 - threshold`—escape/switch on stale quota, or the opposite mis-hold if margin logic misfires on old highs. The inline comment requires a young reading; `None` negates that.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Treat unknown age as untrusted: require `last.age_s is not None and last.age_s <= TRUST_MAX_AGE_S` (or treat `None` like “too old” for this path only).

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~1465`  
**ISSUE:** Pre-freshen exception fallback `switchable = set(headroom)` when `switchable_account_numbers()` fails re-admits every headroom key, including slots explicitly excluded when switchable is readable (disabled / non-rotation).  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Keychain/store blips during high utilization (exactly when pre-freshen runs) trigger refresh attempts on slots the switch will never use—extra OAuth traffic, refresh-token rotation, and `invalid_grant` / quarantine on accounts that should have been skipped.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** On failure, skip pre-freshen entirely (`return`) or fall back to a last-known switchable set cached from a successful tick—not `set(headroom)`.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~2449`  
**ISSUE:** `truly_exhausted` now calls `rotation_account_numbers()` with no local error handling to build `unreadable`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Under Keychain overload (the scenario that drops slots from `candidates`), this extra enumeration can raise and abort the whole tick instead of the safer “not exhausted / keep polling” behavior `unreadable` was meant to enforce—fleet stuck at limit with no sleep/recovery path for that tick.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Wrap in the same degrade pattern as pre-freshen’s switchable read: on failure, treat “unreadable present” as true (or reuse candidate-building’s slot list from the same tick snapshot).

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~1488`  
**ISSUE:** `_prefreshened[num] = now` is set before `_freshen_target` / `_quarantine`; failures still consume the 120s throttle.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Quarantine throws (called out in the comment) or transient freshen fails after the stamp—slot gets no pre-freshen for two minutes while switch-time freshen may not run if the tick stays below switch threshold; candidate arrives at swap with token still in the pre-band → slow path or failed switch under burn rate.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Record throttle only after successful freshen (`status == "ok"`) or after successful quarantine; on failure, do not update `_prefreshened[num]`.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~1431` (engine lifecycle)  
**ISSUE:** `_pre_freshen` performs network refresh + quarantine with no mention of the state-file lock used around `_perform_with_drain2`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Loop daemon and `cron --once` both above `pre_freshen_threshold` can pre-freshen the same slot concurrently—parallel refresh grants, last-write-wins on stored tokens, intermittent identity-conflict/quarantine or wrong active credential.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Hold the same state lock for pre-freshen (short critical section), or a per-slot file lock shared with `_freshen_target` / store writes.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~1478`  
**ISSUE:** `PREFRESHEN_BUDGET_S` only caps how many refreshes **start**; one in-flight refresh can run until its own network timeout.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Below-threshold ticks that emit `NoSwitchEvent` can still block for tens of seconds on a single slow refresh, missing the stated “must never stall the tick” goal and burning threshold margin while session traffic continues.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Check remaining budget before calling `_freshen_target`, or run pre-freshen in a thread with a hard wall-clock cap that does not block the tick (accept abandoning in-flight only if refresh is safely non-committing—which must match `_freshen_target` semantics).

---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~2895`  
**ISSUE:** Reserve gating switched from binding `headroom` to `oauth.weekly_life` while ranking/tiers still use binding headroom `h` (including `MIN_USEFUL_LIFE_PCT`).  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Reserve can hold high **5h** headroom but low **weekly** life (or the reverse): fleet may refuse “only reserve left” (`reserve-protected`) while prefer-mode ordering still promotes/demotes other slots on binding headroom—operator sees “53% week left” blocked reserve messages while rotation order optimizes weekly reset, not the window actually exhausted. May be intentional per comments; still a production confusion / wrong-target switch if 5h is the binding limit.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** If reserve floor is weekly-only, document and align rank keys for reserve comparison; or gate reserve on the same binding metric as `account_headroom` when `_models` is empty.

---

**Not reported (weak or out of scope for this hunk):** `_switch_timing` / `SwitchEvent.timing` wiring looks intentionally cleared in `_perform_with_drain2`; `stop_at` early exit matches documented safety; `weekly_shift` threading is consistent within the diff. Session scan **cache use** is not in this artifact—no finding on cache staleness here.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:2265
ISSUE: Keychain outage causing empty `switchable_account_numbers()` triggers a 10-minute fallback sleep instead of retrying.
ATTACK VECTOR: When the macOS Keychain is locked or experiencing high contention/overload during an at-limit or failover tick, `switcher.switchable_account_numbers()` returns `[]` because stored credentials cannot be read. Lines 2265–2283 catch `not oauth_candidates and not api_key_candidates`, emit `no-candidates`, set `_blocked_wait_long = True`, and sleep for 600s (`NO_RESET_FALLBACK_S`). The unreadable slot guard (`unreadable = [n for n in self.switcher.rotation_account_numbers() if ...]`) was placed only downstream at line 2450 (`truly_exhausted`). If all candidate slots fail Keychain reads simultaneously, the downstream check is bypassed, falsely declaring the fleet empty and starving the active session at its limit for 10 minutes when rotation slots exist.
SUGGESTED FIX: At line 2265, check if `self.switcher.rotation_account_numbers()` contains eligible non-current, non-quarantined slots before setting `_blocked_wait_long = True`; if so, treat candidates as unreadable and retry at the normal polling interval.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3281
ISSUE: Ungated switch executes full transcript scan inside global state lock while emitting 0 ms in `SwitchEvent.timing["quietScanMs"]`.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_perform_with_drain2`, for ungated switches (at-limit, failover, proactive under `switchUnderLoad`), `quiet, _ = self._session_quiet()` is invoked inside `with self._state_lock():`. Because sessions are stalled at the limit, `stop_at` never exits early, forcing a full transcript tree walk that can take up to 350s on loaded hosts. Holding `_state_lock` for minutes blocks any concurrent CLI operations or cron ticks (`cswap cron --once`). Furthermore, because `quiet_s` is only measured inside `if gated:`, `quietScanMs` is recorded as 0 in `SwitchEvent.timing`, completely obscuring the multi-second delay in logs and burn reports.
SUGGESTED FIX: Move `_session_quiet()` outside `with self._state_lock():` (re-acquiring the lock briefly only if gate label updates), and record its elapsed time in `quiet_s` so `timing["quietScanMs"]` reflects reality.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:1480
ISSUE: `_pre_freshen` marks candidate as throttled before `_freshen_target` completes, suppressing retries on transient failures.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_pre_freshen`, `self._prefreshened[num] = now` is updated prior to calling `_freshen_target(num, email)`. If a transient network glitch, DNS timeout, or temporary credential lock contention occurs, `_prefreshened[num]` remains stamped. The candidate is throttled for `PREFRESHEN_INTERVAL_S` (120s). Because the pre-band (e.g. 92% to 97% at ~5.8 pts/min) is only ~50s wide, a single transient failure suppresses all pre-freshening for the remainder of the pre-band. When the switch fires at threshold, the token is still near expiry and the switch pays the 25–88s latency pre-freshening was designed to avoid.
SUGGESTED FIX: Set `self._prefreshened[num] = now` only when `_freshen_target` returns `"ok"` (or terminal status), leaving transient errors unthrottled or backed off on a shorter interval.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:1945
ISSUE: Prefer-mode home escape triggers prematurely on transient unreadable readings when headroom is below 20%.
ATTACK VECTOR: Line 1945 checks `if last_h is not None and last_h < _home_return_margin(settings): active_headroom = 100.0 - settings.threshold`, where `_home_return_margin` is `max(20.0, 100.0 - settings.threshold + settings.hysteresis_pct)`. If home account usage is temporarily unreadable (e.g. single Keychain blip) and its last reading had 19% headroom (81% utilization), it is forced to `active_headroom = 3.0%` (97% utilization). Even though the home account still had 19% headroom and was far from the threshold (97%), a single transient read failure immediately triggers a premature proactive switch away from home.
SUGGESTED FIX: Decouple the unreadable escape threshold from `_home_return_margin`; base the emergency escape check on the hysteresis band near the threshold (`last_h < 100.0 - settings.threshold + settings.hysteresis_pct`), reserving the 20% bar strictly for returning home.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:1937
ISSUE: Blind-home age check accepts entries with `age_s is None`, risking decisions based on days-old data.
ATTACK VECTOR: Line 1937 evaluates `(last.age_s is None or last.age_s <= TRUST_MAX_AGE_S)`. In `UsageEntry`, `age_s` is `None` whenever `fetchedAt` is missing or invalid in the stored row. If a row exists without a valid timestamp, `last.age_s is None` evaluates to `True`, bypassing the `TRUST_MAX_AGE_S` cap. A days-old reading from before a weekly reset can force an escape off a healthy home slot on the first Keychain blip, directly violating the comment's intent.
SUGGESTED FIX: Change condition to `last.age_s is not None and last.age_s <= TRUST_MAX_AGE_S`, matching the ceiling guard in `usage_store.py`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/oauth.py:515
ISSUE: `weekly_life` ignores missing named models, allowing unverified model-restricted accounts to pass reserve floor.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `account_headroom`, if `models` contains named models (e.g. `("fable",)`), it returns `None` if the account does not report that scoped window, preventing switches to accounts with unverified model access. However, `oauth.weekly_life` does not verify that named models are present in `relevant_windows`. If a reserve account's weekly usage only reports `seven_day` and lacks the configured model window, `weekly_life` calculates life solely from `seven_day` and clears `reserve_min_life_pct`. When activated, model-pinned work crashes or stalls because the model quota was never verified.
SUGGESTED FIX: In `weekly_life`, check `if named - {label.lower() for label, _, _ in windows}: return None` matching `account_headroom`.

---

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:2894
ISSUE: Prefer-mode candidate ranking orders resets exclusively by `seven_day`, ignoring model-scoped weekly resets.
ATTACK VECTOR: In prefer-mode candidate ranking, `weekly_reset` is computed via `_seven_day_reset_ts(usage.get(num), now)`. If a fleet is running work pinned to a named model (e.g. `models=("fable",)`), the binding weekly reset may belong to the model's scoped window, not the account-wide 7-day window. If Account A has a 7-day reset in 1 hour but its Fable window resets in 5 days, and Account B has a Fable reset in 2 hours, Account A sorts first despite Account B having the more perishable model quota.
SUGGESTED FIX: Expand `_seven_day_reset_ts` to consider the earliest weekly reset among all relevant windows including configured model scopes.

**1**  
**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:2407‑2456  
**ISSUE:** The loop that freshens candidate tokens ignores the `"transient"` status returned by `_freshen_target`. When a network‑related transient error occurs, the code falls through as if the freshen succeeded, potentially leaving the switch to use an expired or stale token.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In production, intermittent network failures (e.g., DNS hiccup, temporary auth service outage) will cause the engine to switch onto accounts with invalid tokens, leading to failed logins, quota loss, or lock‑outs.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** After calling `_freshen_target`, handle the `"transient"` case explicitly (e.g., set `transient_failure = True` and break/continue to a safe path). Ensure the engine either retries later or aborts the switch, and propagate the failure to the caller.

---

**2**  
**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:2407‑2445, 2987‑2995  
**ISSUE:** The `self._switch_timing` dictionary is populated with freshen timing data, but it is never attached to the `SwitchEvent` that is emitted after a successful switch. Moreover, early‑return paths clear the field, causing the timing information to be lost entirely.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Operators cannot see where time is spent during a switch (freshen vs traffic scan vs write), making performance regressions invisible and hindering root‑cause analysis of long‑running swaps.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** When constructing the `SwitchEvent` (in the code path after a successful `_perform_with_drain2`), pass `timing=self._switch_timing`. Also, only clear the field after the event has been emitted, not before.

**3**  
**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:1179‑1185, 1199‑1205  
**ISSUE:** The attribute `_session_scan_cache` is created (intended to cache the result of a session‑activity scan for `SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S` seconds) but is never read or written anywhere else in the file. Consequently, every tick performs a full recursive `os.walk` over all transcript files.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** On large installations (thousands of transcript files), each tick incurs a costly filesystem scan, potentially causing the tick to exceed its time budget and delaying token refreshes, leading to missed expiries.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Implement cache logic: before calling `latest_session_activity_ts`, check if a cached result exists and is younger than `SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S`; otherwise, store the new result with the current monotonic timestamp.

**4**  
**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:1249‑1255, 1270‑1276  
**ISSUE:** Broad `except Exception` blocks in `_pre_freshen` (around `self.switcher.switchable_account_numbers()` and the freshen loop) swallow all errors and only log at debug/warning level. Failures such as permission errors, corrupted state, or unexpected exceptions are hidden, leaving the engine unaware that it could not obtain a candidate list or freshen a token.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In production, a transient I/O error could cause the engine to skip the entire pre‑freshen phase silently, increasing the chance that the subsequent switch will need to freshen on‑the‑fly and exceed its time budget.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Narrow the caught exceptions to expected ones (e.g., `OSError`, `KeyError`). Re‑raise or record unexpected exceptions so they surface in logs and metrics.

**5**  
**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:1430‑1438  
**ISSUE:** `_freshen_target` computes `now_ms = self.clock() * 1000` and compares it to `expires_at` (which is presumably an epoch‑based timestamp). If `self.clock` is implemented with `time.monotonic()` (a monotonic clock), the comparison will always be false, preventing any near‑expiry detection.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Tokens would never be refreshed proactively, leading to unexpected expiries during a switch.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Ensure `self.clock` returns wall‑clock time (`time.time()`) for timestamp calculations, or convert `expires_at` to a monotonic duration before comparison.

---

**6**  
**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:2256‑2270  
**ISSUE:** The exhaustion check sets `truly_exhausted = not unreadable and all(h is not None and h <= 0 for h in candidate_headrooms)`. If any rotation slot is unreadable (e.g., Keychain failure), `truly_exhausted` becomes `False` even when all readable candidates have zero headroom. This can cause the engine to think work remains and skip the intended back‑off sleep, leading to rapid, useless polling.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In a degraded environment where some slots cannot be read, the engine may spin aggressively, consuming CPU and possibly hitting rate limits.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Treat unreadable slots as exhausted unless they can be verified as having usable headroom. Adjust the condition to `truly_exhausted = all(h is not None and h <= 0 for h in candidate_headrooms) or not candidate_headrooms` and handle unreadable slots separately.

---

**7**  
**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:1279‑1295  
**ISSUE:** In `_pre_freshen`, the result of `_freshen_target` is examined only for `"identity-conflict"` and `"invalid_grant"` statuses. The `"transient"` status is ignored, and the loop proceeds as if the freshen succeeded. This mirrors the bug in the main freshen loop but is confined to the pre‑freshen path.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Transient network failures during pre‑freshen will silently leave tokens stale, causing the later switch‑time freshen to take longer or fail, re‑introducing the performance regression the pre‑freshen was meant to avoid.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add handling for `"transient"` similar to the main loop (e.g., set a flag, log, and possibly retry later).

###   PROVIDER: CLAUDE
###############################################################

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:2455
ISSUE: A permanently unreadable slot disables the "fleet exhausted" check, so the engine never takes the long exhausted-poll path.
ATTACK VECTOR: `unreadable` is built from `rotation_account_numbers()`, which includes enabled slots whose stored backup is gone or corrupt. `switchable_account_numbers()` drops those slots, so they land in `unreadable` on every tick. `truly_exhausted = not unreadable and ...` is then False for as long as that slot exists. The comment says the usual cause is a transient Keychain stall, but a missing backup never heals. If every real candidate is at 0%, the engine keeps the normal cadence instead of the exhausted sleep, so it polls the usage endpoint and Keychain far more often than before, for as long as the bad slot remains.
SUGGESTED FIX: Exclude slots whose backup is verifiably missing, not just unreadable. For example, treat a slot as unreadable only if it was switchable on the previous tick, or give the "unreadable" set its own time bound (e.g. a last-seen timestamp older than `IDLE_HOLD_MAX_S` stops blocking exhaustion).

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py (`_rank_candidates`, prefer-mode key, `h < MIN_USEFUL_LIFE_PCT`; constant near line 147)
ISSUE: `MIN_USEFUL_LIFE_PCT = 10` is a headroom cliff that sits far above the switch threshold, so a candidate that will re-trigger within minutes can outrank a healthy one.
ATTACK VECTOR: Threshold is 97, so the switch fires at 3% headroom. A candidate at 10.5% headroom with its weekly reset tomorrow passes the `h < 10` test and sorts ahead of a candidate at 95% headroom resetting in six days, because the earlier reset comes first in the key. At the measured burn of about 5.8 pts/min, 7.5 points is roughly 80 seconds, so the engine switches again almost immediately. The constant's comment says landings that "switch again within minutes" should sort last, and this case does not.
SUGGESTED FIX: Derive the floor from the switch threshold and burn rate, e.g. `h < (100 - settings.threshold) + MIN_USEFUL_LIFE_PCT`, or compare against the expected minutes-to-threshold. At minimum, document that the 10-point figure is a deliberate trade-off, so the next reader does not take it for a tuned value.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: low
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:2224-2226 (hold path that sets `active_headroom = 100.0 - settings.threshold`)
ISSUE: When the home reading is held blind, `active_headroom` is overwritten with a synthetic value that looks like a real measurement.
ATTACK VECTOR: The substitute `100 - threshold` (3.0 at threshold 97) is indistinguishable from a real reading in later code. Any log line, event or comparison that reads `active_headroom` after this point reports a made-up headroom, which makes the burn report and post-incident analysis misleading. I could not confirm from this part of the diff whether any event reads it after the substitution, so confidence is low.
SUGGESTED FIX: Keep the escape decision as a separate boolean (e.g. `home_spent_blind = True`) and leave `active_headroom` as `None` for logging, or emit the substitute in the event's detail text only.

Checked and not reported: the session-scan cache (`autoswitch.py:4783–4800`) pins a result that hits the early exit to `clock()`, so the cached value cannot age into a false "quiet"; `weekly_life` and `_seven_day_reset_ts` both handle the sentinel `str` via `isinstance` guards inside `relevant_windows`, so the unguarded `usage.get(...)` calls do not crash; and `rotation_account_numbers()` excludes disabled slots, so disabled accounts do not trip the `unreadable` check.

## Adversarial Review — autoswitch.py (part auto1, a21eed1..2abeb2a)

---

**1. SEVERITY: CRITICAL — CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~317 (`latest_session_activity_ts`) + ~1241 (`_session_scan_cache`)**
**ISSUE:** The early-exit scan returns a *lower bound* on the newest mtime, but the docstring's claim that "older than the true newest is the safe direction" only holds when the same `stop_at` is reused. The new 5-second cache reuses that truncated value for later asks whose `stop_at` has advanced, turning "activity exists" into false "quiet".
**ATTACK VECTOR:** With the owner's 9,530-transcript tree, the walk order is arbitrary, so the first file ≥ `now-300` may be a transcript from 299 s ago even though a session wrote 10 s ago. The gate asks at t0, early-exits with R≈t0-300. The drain gate / pre-swap re-measure hits the cache with stop_at = t1-300 > R, so the engine concludes "quiet" — the exact inverse of what the truncation guarantees. A voluntary swap then steals a live session, reintroducing the failure the quiet window exists to prevent. Related: any caller that consumes the *returned timestamp* as the actual last-activity time (e.g., drain-episode staleness aging) will treat a young busy observation as older than it is and expire the episode mid-session.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Only serve the cache when the asked `stop_at` is ≤ the cached scan's `stop_at` **and** the cached result is a complete max (no early exit) or ≥ the new `stop_at`; re-scan with the new threshold otherwise. Never let a truncated result age into a "quiet" verdict, and never use a truncated result as a timestamp.

---

**2. SEVERITY: WARNING — CONFIDENCE: high**
**FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~2885 (`_seven_day_reset_ts(usage.get(num), now)`) and ~2464/~2905 (`oauth.weekly_life(usage.get(reserve), ...)`)**
**ISSUE:** `usage` is typed `dict[str, dict | str | None]` and every *existing* consumer (`_headroom_by_account`, the `active_headroom` call sites) explicitly guards `isinstance(value, dict)` before passing it to an oauth helper. The three new call sites pass the raw value, so error-marker strings now flow into `_seven_day_reset_ts` / `weekly_life` with no guard — convention drift *and* a potential crash path.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A slot whose usage fetch recorded a string error state (which the type and the neighboring guards prove can occur) reaches `_rank_candidates` or the reserve-floor check; depending on the helper's internals this raises inside the ranking loop (killing the tick — the pass is not wrapped best-effort like `_pre_freshen`) or returns a nonsense reset time that misorders the whole fleet.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Apply the same `value if isinstance(value, dict) else None` guard at the three new call sites, matching `_headroom_by_account`.

---

**3. SEVERITY: WARNING — CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~2445 (`unreadable = [n for n in self.switcher.rotation_account_numbers() ...]`)**
**ISSUE:** "Unreadable" is inferred as set difference between `rotation_account_numbers()` and `candidates`, but the codebase itself proves other reasons a slot is absent from `candidates` — the same diff had to add a separate `switchable_account_numbers()` call precisely because *disabled* slots must be excluded from switch-relevant lists.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A single disabled (or otherwise non-switchable-but-rotating-listed) account makes `unreadable` permanently non-empty, so `truly_exhausted` is never true. The engine never enters the exhausted/long-sleep cadence and never emits the honest "fleet at limit" outcome — it polls the whole fleet at full cadence forever, multiplying usage-API load and rate-limit exposure, and the operator sees an engine that "still believes" there is quota because a slot they intentionally disabled is counted as unknown-but-promising.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Compute `unreadable` from `switchable_account_numbers()` (or subtract disabled slots) instead of `rotation_account_numbers()`, so the set means literally "switchable slot missing from this tick's candidates".

---

**4. SEVERITY: WARNING — CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~2900 (reserve floor now `oauth.weekly_life` only)**
**ISSUE:** The reserve floor dropped the binding-window check ("The 5h window is left to the normal threshold gate above"), but the threshold gate evaluates the *current* account's utilization, never the landing candidate's. Nothing in the switch path checks the reserve's own 5h headroom before activating it.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Fleet at 5h limit, reserve has 5h window empty but 40% of its week left: floor passes, engine switches onto the reserve, next tick reads the *new* active account at 100% and fires the at-limit escape. Each swap costs the measured 25–88 s of stolen wall time with the last points burning at ~5.8 pts/min — the exact pathology the whole pre-freshen/timing work exists to fix — and the fleet can ping-pong through the reserve's 5h reset window. Previously `headroom.get(reserve)` (the binding worse-of-both) prevented the landing.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** In the sole-candidate branch, require *both* `weekly_life ≥ floor` and the reserve's binding headroom `> 0` before allowing the landing; keep the weekly-only floor as an additional constraint, not a replacement.

---

**5. SEVERITY: INFO — CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~3219–3225 (`pre_timing = self._switch_timing or {}`)**
**ISSUE:** The timing is taken *after* the first early `return TickOutcome.SWITCHED` in `_perform_with_drain2`, so that path returns without clearing `_switch_timing`; the comment's own invariant ("the NEXT switch would report this one's freshen as its own") is violated for precisely the path above the clear.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Freshen loop sets `_switch_timing` for candidate X; `_perform_with_drain2` short-circuits (e.g., X turns out to be the already-active slot). Next real switch picks up the stale dict and logs X's previous freshen cost against the wrong swap — corrupting exactly the "where did the seconds go" telemetry this field was added to produce, and the burn report can't distinguish it.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Move `pre_timing = self._switch_timing or {}; self._switch_timing = None` above the early `TickOutcome.SWITCHED` return, or clear in a `finally`-equivalent at all exits.

---

**6. SEVERITY: INFO — CONFIDENCE: low**
**FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~1935 (blind-home rule: `last.age_s is None or last.age_s <= TRUST_MAX_AGE_S`)**
**ISSUE:** An *unknown* age is treated as young, so a legacy/never-timestamped `last_good` reading — the very days-old pre-reset reading the comment says must not force a healthy home off its pin — passes the freshness check. The throttle in `_pre_freshen` (`now = self.clock()`, wall-clock epoch, vs. budget on `time.monotonic()`) has the same clock-trust assumption: an NTP forward jump makes every slot look throttle-expired at once.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Keychain blip + an ageless stale reading → engine injects `active_headroom = 100 - threshold` from a reading describing a previous quota week, forcing an escape switch the owner never needed; clock jump → pre-freshen re-attempts refreshes fleet-wide for one tick, spending the network budget on an "optimisation" that is supposed to never stall the tick.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Treat `age_s is None` as *not* trusted (skip the blind-escape), and key `_prefreshened` on `time.monotonic()` like the budget already is.

Adversarial review of **only** the provided diff (no repo on disk). Prior-listed fixes are excluded.

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~3258–3275`  
**ISSUE:** Gated triggers run `_session_quiet(force=True)` once, then call `switch_to()` with no second forced quiet check in between.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A transcript write (or mtime update) after the scan finishes but before `switch_to` completes still switches. The gate is meant to block voluntary swaps under live session traffic; this is a classic check-then-act gap on the only path that is supposed to block.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Hold whatever lock/ordering `test_perform_rechecks_quiet_under_lock` expects across scan + switch, or call `_session_quiet(force=True)` again immediately before `switch_to` (and abort if not quiet).

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~3310–3314`, `~4768–4805`, `~3652–3655`  
**ISSUE:** One `_session_scan_cache` is shared; ungated post-switch labeling and `_drain_gate` call `_session_quiet()` / `_latest_session_activity()` with `force=False`, so they can reuse a pre-switch (or pre-write) scan.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Activity during or right after the swap is invisible until `SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S` expires. Ungated path can set `state["lastSwitchGate"] = "quiet"` and persist it while sessions are active; drain logic can treat the tree as quieter than it is and release or defer drain incorrectly.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** After a successful switch, call `_latest_session_activity(force=True)` for labeling (or invalidate cache at switch boundary). Use `force=True` in `_drain_gate` when the decision must reflect current traffic, or use separate cache keys/purposes for “label” vs “gate”.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~4793–4800`  
**ISSUE:** On early exit, `latest` is replaced with `max(latest, self.clock())` and that value is cached as “newest activity.”  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** That timestamp is scan-end time, not the real last write time. `_drain_gate` (and any logic using cached `latest` for “how long since last activity”) sees artificially recent activity for the whole cache TTL—drain/return-home timing skews even when no new writes occur after the scan.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Cache two values: a boolean/verdict for quiet gating and the real `mtime` for age math; or only pin to `clock()` inside `_session_quiet` for the verdict string, not in the cached `latest` used by drain.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~3258–3270`, `~3320–3330`  
**ISSUE:** On gated success, `SwitchEvent.quiet` (and `detail` from the pre-switch call) is never updated after `switch_to`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Operators or downstream automation trust the event as ground truth; switches that land under load are logged as quiet. Mis-attribution hides regressions and makes “sessions-active” gate effectiveness impossible to measure in production.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** After a gated switch succeeds, optionally re-measure with `force=True` for event fields only, or emit `quietPreSwitch` vs `quietAtSwitch` so telemetry matches behavior.

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~3310–3314`  
**ISSUE:** Ungated labeling runs `_session_quiet()` (potentially a full tree walk) synchronously on the tick path after state and drain markers are already persisted.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** On large project dirs (comments cite tens of seconds to hundreds of seconds), the engine stays in a post-commit phase with stale in-memory assumptions while the filesystem scan runs—overlapping ticks, delayed failover/proactive logic, and timer skew. Switch is irreversible; only labeling/drain metadata lags.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Cap work (budget ms + partial scan), defer labeling to a background step with “provisional” gate, or persist `lastSwitchGate=pending` until a forced scan completes.

**SEVERITY:** INFO

**ISSUE:** `timing["quietScanMs"]` is only populated for gated pre-switch; ungated post-switch scan cost is omitted while comments describe it as the expensive path.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Production profiles show fast “ungated” switches while CPU is spent in an unmeasured post-switch walk—wrong optimization targets and silent SLO breaches.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Record a second metric (e.g. `quietLabelScanMs`) around the ungated `_session_quiet()` block.

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~3312–3314`  
**ISSUE:** Second `atomic_write_json(self.state_path, state)` for `lastSwitchGate` uses the in-memory `state` from the start of the tick, not a read-merge-write.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** If another writer updates the state file between the first switch write and this one, the second write can drop fields (lost `lastSwitchGate` elsewhere or clobber concurrent updates)—depends on multi-process use not shown in the artifact.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Merge from disk before updating `lastSwitchGate`, or use a single atomic write including gate when known, or file-level locking consistent with the store lock mentioned in prior fixes.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3050
ISSUE: `pre_timing` is referenced without definition, raising `NameError` on every switch
ATTACK VECTOR: At line 3608 in `_maybe_return_home`, freshen timing is saved to `self._switch_timing = {"freshenMs": freshen_ms, "freshenAttempts": 1}`. However, inside `_perform_with_drain2` at line 3050, the code attempts `timing = dict(pre_timing)`. `pre_timing` is neither a parameter of `_perform_with_drain2` nor defined in local/enclosing scope. Every switch invocation will crash with `NameError: name 'pre_timing' is not defined` right before `SwitchEvent` emission. Even if `pre_timing` were passed as `None`, `dict(None)` raises `TypeError`.
SUGGESTED FIX: Read and consume the instance attribute with a fallback, e.g.:
```python
timing = dict(getattr(self, "_switch_timing", None) or {})
self._switch_timing = None
```
