SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3378
ISSUE: `_drain_gate` uses `_latest_session_activity()`, causing artificial timestamp inflation that stalls drains
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_drain_gate`, `latest_session_activity_ts(...)` was replaced with `self._latest_session_activity()`. The docstring of `_latest_session_activity` explicitly assumes: *"Both callers ask 'was anything written inside QUIET_WINDOW_S'"*. This assumption is false: `_drain_gate` checks if any transcript write occurred *since the drain started* (`latest > startedAt`). If a transcript was written before the drain started but within `QUIET_WINDOW_S`, `_latest_session_activity` pins `latest = max(latest, self.clock())` (line 4799). When `_drain_gate` compares `latest > started`, it sees the synthesized `now` timestamp, falsely concludes active sessions wrote during the drain, and aborts or permanently stalls the drain.
SUGGESTED FIX: Do not route `_drain_gate` through `_latest_session_activity()`. Keep `_drain_gate` calling `latest_session_activity_ts(self.claude_projects_dir)` directly without `stop_at` or clock-pinning.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3046
ISSUE: State/engine lock is held during the uncached 350-second transcript walk for ungated switches
ATTACK VECTOR: The diff attempts to move the transcript scan after writing `lastSwitchAt` because *"at the limit the walk never exits early — tens of seconds, 350 s measured on a loaded host"*. However, `quiet, _ = self._session_quiet()` at line 3046 remains indented at 16 spaces inside the 12-space critical section (`with lock:` block). For an ungated switch, no scan was executed prior to the swap, meaning `_session_scan_cache` has no warm entry. The entire 350-second filesystem traversal executes while holding the state lock, blocking concurrent CLI commands (`cswap`) and background engine tasks for minutes.
SUGGESTED FIX: Move `quiet, _ = self._session_quiet()` and its state-update write outside the lock block (at 8 spaces), or run the label scan asynchronously/after lock release.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3608
ISSUE: `self._switch_timing` is never cleared on early exits or after completion, leaking stale timing to subsequent switches
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_maybe_return_home`, `self._switch_timing` is assigned right before calling `_perform_with_drain2`. If `_perform_with_drain2` aborts early (e.g. `not quiet` on line 3018, or `switch_to` returns False on line 3026), it returns `TickOutcome.NO_ACTION` without clearing `self._switch_timing`. Furthermore, even upon successful switch, nothing clears `self._switch_timing`. On subsequent ticks, completely unrelated switches (such as failovers or at-limit triggers) will report the stale `freshenMs` and `freshenAttempts: 1` belonging to the old return-home attempt.
SUGGESTED FIX: Pop or reset `self._switch_timing` in `_perform_with_drain2` (or in a `try...finally` block within `_maybe_return_home`).

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:4793
ISSUE: Cache creation timestamp is sampled before the slow scan, causing immediate cache expiration
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_latest_session_activity`, `now = self.clock()` is recorded before `latest_session_activity_ts(...)` begins. If the transcript scan takes 45 to 350 seconds on a loaded host, the method stores `self._session_scan_cache = (now, latest)` using the start timestamp. When the next caller checks `0 <= now - cached[0] < SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S`, `now - cached[0]` is already greater than the scan duration (e.g. 45s > 5s cache TTL). The cache is already expired the moment it is saved, causing back-to-back calls to repeat the full filesystem scan.
SUGGESTED FIX: Store the completion timestamp in the cache tuple:
```python
self._session_scan_cache = (self.clock(), latest)
```

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3048
ISSUE: `state["lastSwitchGate"]` is not cleared when an ungated switch occurs under active traffic
ATTACK VECTOR: For ungated switches, the code only updates `state["lastSwitchGate"] = "quiet"` if `quiet` evaluates to `True`. If active sessions are running (`quiet is False`), the block is skipped. Because `state` is mutated in place from the existing state on disk, if a previous switch had set `lastSwitchGate = "quiet"`, the field is left untouched. The on-disk state file and memory state retain the stale `"quiet"` label, falsely indicating that the current ungated switch occurred during quiet traffic.
SUGGESTED FIX: Explicitly update `state["lastSwitchGate"]` for both branches:
```python
state["lastSwitchGate"] = "quiet" if quiet else "active"
atomic_write_json(self.state_path, state)
```

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3051
ISSUE: `timing["quietScanMs"]` reports 0ms for all ungated switches
ATTACK VECTOR: `quiet_s` is initialized to `0.0`. While gated switches time `_session_quiet(force=True)` into `quiet_s`, the ungated branch at line 3046 executes `quiet, _ = self._session_quiet()` without updating `quiet_s`. Consequently, `timing["quietScanMs"]` is always emitted as `0` for ungated triggers, masking multi-second transcript scan latencies in event telemetry.
SUGGESTED FIX: Measure monotonic elapsed time around `_session_quiet()` on the ungated path and assign it to `quiet_s`.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3309
ISSUE: Stale cache used to set `lastSwitchGate` after non-gated switch
ATTACK VECTOR: In non-gated flows (at-limit/failover), the code checks `quiet` after the switch but calls `_session_quiet()` without `force=True`. The `_latest_session_activity` cache might return `latest` from before the switch operation. If user activity occurred during the switch (e.g., ~0), the stale cache reports `quiet=True`, leading to a wrong `lastSwitchGate:quiet"` state write.
SUGGESTED FIX: Pass `force=True` to `self._session_quiet()` in the `if not gated:` block at line 3309 to ensure fresh measurement before writing state.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3309
ISSUE: Missing state write error handling for post-switch gate update
ATTACK VECTOR: In the `if not gated:` block, the code writes `state` with `lastSwitchGate` via `atomic_write_json`. If this write fails (OSError), the error is not logged or handled. `lastSwitchGate` is lost, potentially causing future logic to assume the switch occurred during active session traffic or fail consistency checks.
SUGGESTED FIX: Wrap the `if not gated:` `atomic_write_json` call in a try/except block (similar to previous writes) and log errors.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3553
ISSUE: Race condition on `state` dict in `return_home` path
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_return_home`, `self._switch_timing` is assigned only on the success path. If `_freshen_target` returns early (e.g., `identity-conflict`), `_switch_timing` remains stale from a previous operation. Consumers of this flag might read outdated timing data.
SUGGESTED FIX: Initialize `self._switch_timing` to `None` at the start of `_return_home` or delete it on early returns.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3567
ISSUE: Invariant violation on `latest` timestamp calculation
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_latest_session_activity`, the code sets `latest = max(latest, self.clock())` if `latest >= stop_at`. If `self.clock()` is significantly larger than file mtime due to clock skew, `latest` gets pinned to system time. Future comparisons `now - latest` return ~0, incorrectly marking old files as "fresh".
SUGGESTED FIX: Do not clamp `latest` to `self.clock()` unless explicitly modeling file system monotonicity guarantees.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3327
ISSUE: Unnecessary state mutation in `not gated` path
ATTACK VECTOR: The `not gated` path writes `state["lastSwitchGate"]` via `atomic_write_json` even though `SwitchEvent` already includes `timing` info. If the switch logic is changed to not write state here, the atomic write is wasted. More critically, if this state write is not part of a transactional write with `lastSwitchAt`, disk state may diverge from reported metrics.
SUGGESTED FIX: Remove the redundant `atomic_write_json` in the `not gated` block or consolidate into single write with `lastSwitchAt`.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: low
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3547
ISSUE: Cache expiry condition assumes monotonic clock
ATTACK VECTOR: Cache check `0 <= now - cached[0] < SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S` assumes `self.clock()` is monotonic. If system clock jumps backward (NTP correction), `now - cached[0]` becomes negative. While `0 <=` guards against this, cache expiration logic becomes inconsistent relative to actual elapsed wall-clock time.
SUGGESTED FIX: Use `time.monotonic()` consistently for elapsed time calculations to handle clock skew.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: low
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3555
ISSUE: Inconsistent exception type handling
ATTACK VECTOR: `_return_home` returns `None` or calls `quarantine`, but `switch_to` uses explicit `if not result` checks. The control flow in `_return_home` returns `None` after `quarantine`, relying on caller logic. If caller expects `False` or exception for failure, this inconsistency leads to silent failures or logic drift.
SUGGESTED FIX: Standardize failure return values across `_return_home` and `_perform`.


###############################################################
###   PROVIDER: OPENROUTER-4
###############################################################

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3110‑3119  
**ISSUE:** After a successful switch the code re‑uses the cached result from `_session_quiet()` to decide whether to label the switch as “quiet”. The cache is populated *before* the switch (forced scan) and is **not** refreshed after the switch, so any session activity that occurs during the switch window can be missed and the switch will be incorrectly labeled as quiet.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In production a burst of user traffic that starts just after the gated quiet check but before the switch completes will be silently ignored, leading to inaccurate metrics and potentially to downstream logic that assumes the system is idle (e.g., throttling, billing).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** After the switch, call `_session_quiet(force=True)` (or invalidate the cache) before labeling, e.g.:

```python
if not gated:
    quiet, _ = self._session_quiet(force=True)
    if quiet:
        state["lastSwitchGate"] = "quiet"
        atomic_write_json(self.state_path, state)
```


---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3225‑3232  
**ISSUE:** The cache timestamp (`self._session_scan_cache[0]`) and the expiration check both use `self.clock()` (wall‑clock time). If the system clock is adjusted (NTP jump forward or backward) the cache may be considered valid for longer or shorter than `SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A backward clock adjustment could cause the cache to be reused indefinitely, preventing detection of new session activity; a forward jump could cause premature cache invalidation, leading to unnecessary full scans and performance degradation.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use `time.monotonic()` for the cache age calculation while keeping the scanned timestamps in wall‑clock time, e.g.:

```python
cached_time, cached_latest = self._session_scan_cache
if not force and cached_time is not None:
    if time.monotonic() - cached_time < SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S:
        return cached_latest
self._session_scan_cache = (time.monotonic(), latest)
```


---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3225‑3245  
**ISSUE:** `_session_scan_cache` is a plain instance attribute accessed and mutated without any synchronization. If `AutoSwitchEngine` is used from multiple threads (or async tasks) concurrent calls to `_session_quiet()` can race, corrupting the cache tuple or returning mismatched timestamps.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A race could cause the engine to incorrectly deem the system “quiet” or “active”, leading to missed switches or unnecessary throttling.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Guard cache reads/writes with a lightweight lock, e.g.:

```python
self._cache_lock = threading.Lock()
...
with self._cache_lock:
    # read or write self._session_scan_cache
```


---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3335‑3340  
**ISSUE:** `self._switch_timing` is only populated on the freshen‑home path. Subsequent switches that do not freshen leave the previous timing data in place, so the emitted `SwitchEvent` may contain stale `freshenMs`/`freshenAttempts` values.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Monitoring/alerting systems that rely on these metrics could be misled into thinking a freshen took longer (or happened) when it did not, obscuring real performance problems.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Reset or clear `self._switch_timing` at the start of each `_perform` call, or only include the field in the event when a freshen actually occurred.

```python
self._switch_timing = {}
...
if freshen_performed:
    self._switch_timing = {"freshenMs": freshen_ms, "freshenAttempts": 1}
```


---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3229‑3235  
**ISSUE:** `_latest_session_activity` forwards `stop_at` to `latest_session_activity_ts`, but the underlying helper may ignore this argument (its original signature only accepted a path). If ignored, the intended early‑exit optimisation is lost and every call performs a full directory walk.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Unnecessary I/O on large transcript trees can increase latency, especially under load, potentially causing timeouts.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Verify that `latest_session_activity_ts` accepts `stop_at` and, if not, extend it to support early termination or implement the stop logic directly in `_latest_session_activity`.


---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3379‑3384  
**ISSUE:** `_drain_gate` calls `self._latest_session_activity()` without `force=True`, so it may rely on a cached result that is up to `SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S` seconds old. Recent session activity could be missed, allowing the drain gate to be taken when it should be blocked.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In a high‑traffic scenario a new session could start just after the cache was populated, bypassing the drain‑gate check and leading to resource contention or policy violations.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Force a fresh scan when evaluating the drain gate:

```python
latest = self._latest_session_activity(force=True)
```


---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:4492‑4499 (within `_session_quiet`)  
**ISSUE:** The method mixes `self.clock()` (wall‑clock) for timestamp arithmetic with `time.monotonic()` for duration measurement elsewhere. If the system clock jumps backwards, `age = self.clock() - latest` can become negative, causing the function to treat the system as active even when it is truly quiet.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Sudden NTP adjustments could cause spurious “sessions‑active” events, preventing legitimate switches and degrading user experience.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Guard against negative ages:

```python
age = max(0.0, self.clock() - latest)
```  

or use a monotonic source for both the cache age and the quiet‑window comparison.

###   PROVIDER: CLAUDE
###############################################################

**Finding 1**
SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~3322 (`timing = dict(pre_timing)` / `quietScanMs`)
ISSUE: For ungated switches, `quietScanMs` is reported as `0`, but the scan that actually runs (the post-switch label scan) is never timed.
ATTACK VECTOR: The diff exists to measure the scan cost, which the comments say reached 350 s at the limit. `quiet_s` is set only on the gated branch, so every ungated switch logs `quietScanMs: 0` no matter how long the label walk took. The telemetry then shows the scan as free for exactly the trigger class (at-limit, failover) where it is expensive, and the next person tuning this reads a false result.
SUGGESTED FIX: Time the label call (`t_label = time.monotonic()` around `self._session_quiet()`) and assign `quiet_s` from it in the `if not gated:` block, so `quietScanMs` covers whichever scan ran.

**Finding 2**
SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~3309-3316 (`if not gated:` label block after `atomic_write_json`)
ISSUE: The ungated label scan runs after the switch, cooldown and state are committed, with no guard, and the `SwitchEvent` is emitted only after it returns.
ATTACK VECTOR: At the limit the walk is a full scan, which the comment puts at tens of seconds to 350 s on a loaded host, so the tick stalls that long after the swap. If `latest_session_activity_ts` or `_session_quiet()` raises (for example, a transcript deleted mid-walk, or an OSError the early exit does not catch), the exception escapes after the switch is already done. The switch happens with no `SwitchEvent` in the log, and the operator sees no record of it. The change moves the expensive scan to where it can fail with side effects already committed.
SUGGESTED FIX: Wrap the label call in `try/except Exception` that logs at debug and leaves `quiet=False`, so the event is always emitted. Also consider emitting the `SwitchEvent` before the label scan and labelling it with the pre-switch cached value.

**Finding 3**
SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~4790-4800 (`_latest_session_activity`, the `latest = max(latest, self.clock())` pin)
ISSUE: The pin to scan-end time is aged by the cache lifetime, so the "never flips to quiet" guarantee depends on `SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S <= QUIET_WINDOW_S`, which the artifact does not show.
ATTACK VECTOR: A cache entry `(now, E)` is returned for up to `SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S`. `_session_quiet` computes `age = clock() - E`, so once `QUIET_WINDOW_S` has passed since `E`, a session that wrote a second before `E` reads as quiet. The docstring says this flip is what the pin prevents, but the pin only moves the reference point. If the TTL is larger than the quiet window (a plausible tuning change), a gated switch can see "quiet" from a stale cache while a session is active, and the pre-swap gate is the one that is supposed to block it.
SUGGESTED FIX: Enforce the ordering at the definition site, e.g. `assert SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S <= QUIET_WINDOW_S` at module load, or store the cache's valid-until time and return "active" (`clock()`) for any cached value older than the quiet window.

**Finding 4**
SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~3589-3593 (`self._switch_timing = {...}`)
ISSUE: `_switch_timing` is written on the return-home path and then read by whatever performs the switch. Nothing in the diff clears it after it is read, and `freshenAttempts` is hardcoded to `1`.
ATTACK VECTOR: The comment says no exit can leave a stale timing behind, but that holds only if `_perform_with_drain2` clears the attribute. If it does not, a later switch that takes a path without a freshen (dry-run return-home, or any other trigger) reports the old `freshenMs`, which is wrong telemetry. Separately, if `_freshen_target` retries internally, `freshenAttempts: 1` is false.
SUGGESTED FIX: Pop the attribute in the consumer (`self._switch_timing = None` after use) and set `freshenAttempts` from the value `_freshen_target` reports, or drop the field.

**Finding 5**
SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: low
FILE: src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:~3313-3317 (`state["lastSwitchGate"] = "quiet"` only under `if quiet`)
ISSUE: The ungated branch writes `lastSwitchGate` only when the answer is quiet and never clears it when the answer is active.
ATTACK VECTOR: If an earlier switch set `lastSwitchGate` to `"quiet"`, a later ungated switch that lands while sessions are active leaves the old value in state. Whatever reads that field (status output, drain logic) sees a quiet landing that did not happen.
SUGGESTED FIX: Write the value on both outcomes, e.g. `state["lastSwitchGate"] = "quiet" if quiet else "active"`, and save state in both cases.

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `scripts/deploy-hosts.sh:39` (REMOTE block, `rm -rf` + `mv` chain)  
**ISSUE:** Install replaces `~/.local/src/cswap` with a single shell chain: delete live tree, then move from `.new` — no rollback if `mv`/`rmdir`/`COMMIT` write fails after `rm -rf`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Disk full, permission glitch, or interrupted SSH mid-command leaves **no** `cswap` tree while systemd still restarts units; CI/Mac hosts run broken or missing installs until manual recovery.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Move current tree aside (`mv cswap cswap.old`), promote `.new`, then remove `.old` only after `uv tool install` succeeds; or use `rsync` into place and swap a symlink atomically.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `scripts/hub-status-push.py:38-45`  
**ISSUE:** `parse()` anchors on the first `{` and `json.loads(text[i:])` on the remainder — no validation that the blob is solely `cswap … --json` output.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** MOTD, shell profile noise, or a line containing `{` before the real payload yields wrong object, decode error → `None`, or accidental success on the wrong JSON; hub gets stale merge or error strings while operators assume live rotation state.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Require a stable delimiter (e.g. marker line before JSON), or scan for the last complete JSON object; treat non-zero exit / empty stdout as hard failure before parse.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `scripts/hub-status-push.py:68-74,124-128`  
**ISSUE:** After stale backfill from `prev`, `errors["claude"]` / `errors["codex"]` are not cleared when the half was restored from `old`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Hub document shows **both** recovered list data and error text; dashboards/alerts treat the machine as broken, or hide “stale” semantics because errors dominate the UI contract.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** When copying `old[half]` into `d[half]`, delete the matching `errors[half]` key (or set an explicit `stale`/`recoveredFrom` field and drop the parse error).

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `scripts/hub-status-push.py:84-93,121-128`  
**ISSUE:** `previous_docs()` swallows all failures and returns `{}`; merge only runs when prev fetch succeeded.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Transient hub outage or auth glitch during **read**, while **collect** partially fails on a host, PUT still sends `null` halves and **overwrites** last-good hub rows (opposite of “keeps last document” intent for that round).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** If `previous_docs` fails, skip PUT for any doc where a half is `None` (same as the all-null branch), or retry prev fetch once before merge.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `scripts/deploy-hosts.sh:48-51`  
**ISSUE:** Staleness check uses `ps -o etimes= -p "$pid"` without guarding empty/failed `ps` (crash/restart between `show` and `ps`). With `set -u`, empty substitution can abort the remote deploy script mid-verification.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Race during restart reports FAIL or aborts before `OK`/`FAIL` line; operator sees `FAIL no answer` or partial rollout marked failed while state is unknown; repeat deploys under stress.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** If `ps` fails or etimes empty, record `$u(stale|unknown)` and continue; wrap arithmetic in a test `[ -n "$etime" ]`.

**SEVERITY:** WARNING

**ATTACK VECTOR:** List headers, aliases, or error lines that contain `@` skip a real add; or a typo’d address not in grep’s character class duplicates work / leaves rotation inconsistent with intent.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use `cswap list --json` (or equivalent) and compare normalized `email` fields only.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: scripts/deploy-hosts.sh:38
ISSUE: Missing `-f -` in `tar -x` causes extraction failure on Linux GNU tar
ATTACK VECTOR: On Linux (GNU tar on ryzen-dev, ryzen-tf, waw-tf), running `tar -x -C ~/.local/src/cswap.new` without `-f` does not read from stdin. GNU tar falls back to the default archive device (typically `/dev/rmt0`), failing immediately with `tar: /dev/rmt0: Cannot open: No such file or directory`. Line 38 aborts with `FAIL unpack`, preventing deployment to all remote Linux hosts.
SUGGESTED FIX: Change `tar -x -C ~/.local/src/cswap.new` to `tar -xf - -C ~/.local/src/cswap.new`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: scripts/hub-status-push.py:131
ISSUE: Half-failure staleness timestamp is overwritten with the previous failed round's timestamp, masking cumulative age
ATTACK VECTOR: When a half (`claude` or `codex`) fails across consecutive runs, line 131 sets `d.setdefault("stale", {})[half] = old.get("at")`. Because `old["at"]` is the timestamp of the immediately preceding push (which also updated its top-level `at`), `stale[half]` resets to `now - 60s` on every subsequent failure instead of retaining the original collection timestamp. The web hub will display stale data as fresh (never older than 60s) even if an endpoint has been down for hours or days.
SUGGESTED FIX: Preserve the existing stale timestamp if already set: `d.setdefault("stale", {})[half] = (old.get("stale") or {}).get(half, old.get("at"))`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/cli.py:987-1040
ISSUE: `cswap codex auto` loop caches settings once at startup and ignores `autoswitch.intervalSeconds`
ATTACK VECTOR: Unlike `cswap auto` (which loads settings dynamically per tick or listens for configuration updates), `cswap codex auto` reads `load_settings()` once before entering `while True:`. Dynamic updates made via `cswap config set autoswitch.threshold` or `codexReserveAccount` during the daemon's long-running operation are never picked up without a manual process restart. Furthermore, loop sleep defaults to `args.interval` (60s) and never references `settings.interval_seconds`, ignoring user-configured intervals.
SUGGESTED FIX: Move `settings = load_settings(root)` inside the `while True:` loop and fall back to `settings.interval_seconds` if `--interval` is not explicitly set.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: scripts/hub-status-push.py:25
ISSUE: Stderr redirection to `/dev/null` in `BOTH` pipeline discards CLI error diagnostics from `doc["errors"]`
ATTACK VECTOR: `BOTH` is defined as `"cswap list --json 2>/dev/null; echo '@@CODEX@@'; cswap codex list --json 2>/dev/null"`. Line 69 attempts to extract error messages from stderr via `(p.stderr.strip().splitlines() or ["no output from cswap list --json"])[-1][:300]`. Because stderr from `cswap` was redirected to `/dev/null`, `p.stderr` is always empty for CLI errors (e.g., auth failures, syntax errors, missing dependencies), causing `doc["errors"]` to always display the generic fallback and blinding operators to the root cause.
SUGGESTED FIX: Remove `2>/dev/null` from the `cswap` commands in `BOTH` so remote error output is retained in `p.stderr`.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: scripts/deploy-hosts.sh:51-52
ISSUE: Clock sampling skew between `date +%s` and `ps etimes` causes spurious `u(stale)` deployment failures
ATTACK VECTOR: Line 51 evaluates `date +%s` first, followed by a separate subshell executing `ps -o etimes=`. If the clock second ticks over between the two calls, `date - etimes` evaluates to 1 second before actual process start. If `installed` falls in that same second, `[ "$started" -ge "$installed" ]` evaluates to false, causing `deploy-hosts.sh` to report a false-positive failure `FAIL ... u(stale)`. Furthermore, if `uv-receipt.toml` is absent or unreadable, an empty `installed` string causes a `[: -ge: unary operator expected` syntax error.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add a tolerance margin and default fallback: `[ "$((started + 2))" -ge "${installed:-0}" ]`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: scripts/deploy-hosts.sh:34
ISSUE: `systemctl --user` invoked over non-interactive SSH on `ryzen-dev` fails without `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`
ATTACK VECTOR: For target `ryzen-dev`, `REMOTE` defines `ctl() { systemctl --user "$@"; }`. Unlike `run()`, which wraps commands in `bash -lc "$1"`, `ctl` executes directly in the environment of the non-interactive SSH command. Non-interactive SSH sessions on systemd systems generally do not export `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` or `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS`, causing `systemctl --user` to fail with `Failed to connect to bus: No medium found` or `No such file or directory`.
SUGGESTED FIX: Wrap `ctl` in the user's login shell or explicitly supply the bus address: `ctl() { XDG_RUNTIME_DIR="/run/user/$(id -u)" systemctl --user "$@"; }`.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/cli.py:979-985
ISSUE: `cswap codex auto` rejects `--json` parameter despite unconditionally emitting JSON output
ATTACK VECTOR: Launchd plists (e.g. `com.greglas.claude-account-switcher.plist`) and standard wrapper daemons invoke `cswap auto` with `--json`. However, `p_auto` in `_codex_command` does not declare `--json`, even though line 1036 unconditionally prints JSON output. Passing `--json` to `cswap codex auto` causes `argparse` to exit with code 2 (`unrecognized arguments: --json`), breaking CLI parity and causing wrapper configurations to fail.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add `p_auto.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help=argparse.SUPPRESS)` so the flag is accepted as a no-op.

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: scripts/deploy-hosts.sh:69  
ISSUE: A failed remote deploy can be reported as successful.  
ATTACK VECTOR: `deploy_host` pipes SSH output through `tail -1` without `pipefail`. A remote command can print `OK` and then exit unsuccessfully; the host is marked deployed because only the final output line is checked.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Preserve and check the SSH exit status as well as the remote verification result.

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: scripts/deploy-hosts.sh:35  
ISSUE: Deployment removes the running installation before the replacement is ready.  
ATTACK VECTOR: After the existing source tree is deleted, `uv tool install`, restart, or verification can fail. The host is left with a partial rollout and no rollback path.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Install and verify the replacement in a staging location, then switch it into place; retain the prior installation until restart succeeds.

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: scripts/hub-status-push.py:103  
ISSUE: A failed previous-document read can erase the last good half of a status document.  
ATTACK VECTOR: `previous_docs()` returns `{}` on any read failure. If one collector then succeeds and the other fails, `main()` sends the successful half with `null` for the failed half, overwriting its last good value.  
SUGGESTED FIX: When the previous-document read fails, skip writes for documents with a missing half.

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: scripts/hub-status-push.py:129  
ISSUE: Repeated partial failures reset the displayed age of stale data.  
ATTACK VECTOR: A preserved half is copied from an older document, but its `stale` timestamp is set to that document’s fresh `at` value on every round. The page can therefore show persistently old account data as only one minute old.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Carry forward `old["stale"][half]` when present; otherwise use `old["at"]`.

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: scripts/hub-status-push.py:62  
ISSUE: A successful collection can be accepted from a command sequence that failed.  
ATTACK VECTOR: The shell command separates the two list calls with `;` and redirects their errors away. If a login shell or command emits parseable JSON before failing, `collect()` accepts it without checking the subprocess exit status.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Run each list command separately and require a successful exit status before accepting its JSON.

###   PROVIDER: MUSE
###############################################################

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: scripts/hub-status-push.py
ISSUE: Failed `previous_docs()` fetch causes good hub data to be overwritten with null
ATTACK VECTOR: `previous_docs()` returns `{}` on any exception. The merge loop then sees `old={}`, so `old.get(half)` is None and the failed half is not restored — but if the other half parsed, the code still `put()`s a document with one half as `None`, permanently replacing the last good half on the hub with null. A transient read failure thus causes permanent data loss.
SUGGESTED FIX: If `prev` is empty/failed, skip PUTs for any doc with a `None` half (same as the both-None path) instead of sending partial nulls.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: launchd/com.greglas.cswap-hub-status.plist
ISSUE: Overlapping rounds race on hub writes (interval 60s < per-host timeout 90s)
ATTACK VECTOR: `StartInterval 60` with `subprocess.run(timeout=90)` plus 30s hub timeouts means round N can still be collecting while round N+1 starts. Two concurrent rounds read different `prev` snapshots and PUT out of order, so an older round can overwrite a newer round's fresh half with a stale-restored half.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add a lock (`flock -n`) around one round, or lower collect timeout well under 60s and set `ThrottleInterval`.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: scripts/deploy-hosts.sh
ISSUE: Reports DEPLOY-OK while Mac and hosts run different code
ATTACK VECTOR: Dirty `src/` only warns; hosts get `HEAD` via `git archive` while the Mac runs the editable working tree with local changes. Success still prints `deploy-hosts: DEPLOY-OK`, re-creating the exact Mac/host ranking skew the header comment cites as the reason for this script.
SUGGESTED FIX: Exit non-zero (or refuse DEPLOY-OK) when `git diff --quiet HEAD -- src` fails, or record the dirty state and verify it.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: scripts/cswap-ci
ISSUE: Suppressed SSH errors defeat duplicate-skip and status check
ATTACK VECTOR: `have=$(as_gha ... 2>/dev/null | grep ...)` hides SSH/sudo failures as empty `have`, so every email looks new and triggers interactive `claude auth login` prompts. The final `as_gha ... | grep ...` lacks `pipefail`, so the verdict is grep's exit, not ssh's — root cause is hidden in both paths.
SUGGESTED FIX: Keep ssh stderr, check exit status before parsing (`set -o pipefail` or `${PIPESTATUS[0]}`), abort the host on connection failure.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: src/claude_swap/cli.py
ISSUE: Private `_credits_account()` called per-row in text list, cached once in JSON list
ATTACK VECTOR: Text branch re-reads the credits store/keychain for every account (N I/O operations, slow/flaky path hit N times) and can show an inconsistent view if it changes mid-list; JSON branch caches once. Use of the underscore-private accessor from the CLI is also an abstraction leak.
SUGGESTED FIX: Hoist to one call before the loop (or add a public accessor) and reuse it in both branches.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: scripts/hub-status-push.py
ISSUE: Remote errors discarded, return code ignored
ATTACK VECTOR: `BOTH` contains `2>/dev/null`, so remote `cswap` errors never reach `collect()`; it then reads `p.stderr` (SSH stderr only) and ignores `p.returncode`. A half-failing remote that still emits partial stdout is treated as success with no error recorded, misdirecting debugging.
SUGGESTED FIX: Drop `2>/dev/null` from the remote command (capture remote stderr separately) and record an error when `p.returncode != 0` even if JSON parses.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: scripts/hub-status-push.py
ISSUE: Stale half advertised with fresh timestamp
ATTACK VECTOR: On half-restore, `d["at"]` stays at the new collection time while `stale[half] = old.get("at")` copies the parent document time, not that half's actual collection time (and chains lose the original time after two stale rounds). Consumers reading `at` alone see stale data as fresh.
SUGGESTED FIX: Preserve per-half timestamps from the old body instead of the parent `at`, and don't advance `d["at"]` for a restored half.

**FINDINGS**

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** `scripts/hub-status-push.py:80-91` (`previous_docs`) + `:118-133` (`main`)
**ISSUE:** A failed read of the previous documents degrades to `{}`, and the round then PUTs anyway — overwriting the hub's last-good half with `null`, exactly the data loss `previous_docs` exists to prevent.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `urlopen` on `/api/db?since=0` is the most fragile call in the round (Cloudflare 403/5xx, 30 s timeout, one `KeyError` on a doc missing `"id"`/`"body"` — all funnel into `except Exception: return {}`). With `prev == {}`, `old = prev.get(...) or {}` is empty, so `old.get(half) is not None` is false for every machine and no stale value is carried. One transient read failure plus one flaky `cswap list` on the same tick blanks that machine's good half on the live page; the whole round still exits 0, so launchd logs a single stderr line and nothing ever alerts. The inline comment admits the consequence ("without it a failed half is sent as null") but the code takes that path silently rather than skipping.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Make the failure distinguishable — return `None` from `previous_docs` on exception, and in `main` skip any doc that has a `None` half when `prev is None` (plus `rc = 1`). Never PUT a doc containing a `None` half if the previous state was unreadable.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** `scripts/hub-status-push.py:127-129` (`d.setdefault("stale", {})[half] = old.get("at")`)
**ISSUE:** The age of a carried-over half is re-stamped from the *previous round's* top-level `at`, so indefinitely stale data creeps forward and always looks ~1 minute old.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Round N publishes `{at: tN, claude: Cn, codex: X0, stale: {codex: t0}}`. In round N+1 the codex half fails again, `old.get("codex")` is the *carried* `X0`, and `old.get("at")` is `tN` — not `t0`. Every subsequent round moves `stale.codex` forward by one interval. After an hour of `cswap codex list` failing (older cswap on a CI host, locked store, missing `codex` subcommand), the hub page reports the frozen Codex panel as 60 s old. The stated design contract ("the page shows how old each one is") is broken precisely in the outage case it was built for, and readers will act on hours-old rotation data as current.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Preserve the original stamp: `d.setdefault("stale", {})[half] = (old.get("stale") or {}).get(half) or old.get("at")`.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/cli.py:1039-1043` (list branch, before the `if getattr(args, "json", False)`)
**ISSUE:** `cswap codex list --json` prints human prose (and nothing else) when no accounts are stored, violating its own machine-readable contract.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The empty-store early `return` is shared by both output modes, so `--json` emits `No Codex accounts stored — store the current login with...`. `hub-status-push.parse()` finds no `{`, returns `None`, records `errors.codex`, and then `main()` *carries the previous Codex doc forever* — so a CI host whose Codex accounts were all revoked/removed keeps showing a full healthy panel on the public hub (compounded by the stale-age bug above). Any other JSON consumer (jq scripts, the page itself) gets a parse error instead of an empty account list, and "zero accounts" is indistinguishable from "collection failed".
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Move the `--json` check above the `if not emails:` guard and emit the normal envelope (`{"schemaVersion": 1, ..., "accounts": []}`); keep the prose hint for the text path only.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** `scripts/hub-status-push.py:25` (`BOTH`) vs `:68-71`
**ISSUE:** The collected command redirects the CLIs' stderr to `/dev/null`, but the error surface for the page is built from `p.stderr` — so the real reason for a failure is thrown away and the report is always the generic string.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `cswap list --json 2>/dev/null` inside `BOTH` means `p.stderr` only ever carries ssh/bash noise. When a host's store lock times out, `get_backup_root` points somewhere else, or the installed cswap predates the `codex` subcommand (`error: unknown command 'codex'`), `doc["errors"]["claude"]` reports "no output from cswap list --json" and `doc["errors"]["codex"]` reports the canned "older cswap" guess. Diagnosing a red panel on the hub requires re-running the collection by hand on each host; the failure is silently unattributable, and the guess actively misleads (a genuine lock/permission error is reported as "no Codex accounts").
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Drop `2>/dev/null` from both inner commands (stderr is already captured separately), or tag them: `cswap list --json 2>&1` is wrong — instead capture per-command stderr with distinct markers so `parse()` can fail with the real text.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/cli.py:1069-1073` (text branch) vs `:1053` (JSON branch)
**ISSUE:** The same "reserve held back" predicate is implemented twice, differently — `codex.reserve_spent(u, reserve_min_life)` for JSON and an inline `u.weekly_life() < reserve_min_life` for the terminal.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Two sources of truth for one rule means the operator's `cswap codex list` and the hub's `--json` can disagree for the same account at the same instant. Any condition `reserve_spent` carries that the inline form lacks (unknown weekly life → treat as spent/not-spent, `u.error`, `limit_reached`, credits fallback) makes the CLI say "(reserve — last resort)" while JSON says `held: true`, so the daemon appears to skip an account the page shows as usable — the exact confusion the reserve rule exists to avoid. The inline path also does `f"{week:.0f}% ... {reserve_min_life:.0f}%"`, which raises `TypeError` if `codex_reserve_min_life_pct` is unset/`None` (the JSON path hands the same value to a helper that may guard it), and `TypeError` is not caught by `except codex.CodexError` — traceback instead of a list.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use `held = email == reserve and codex.reserve_spent(u, reserve_min_life)` in the text branch too, and print the tag off that flag; make the `:.0f` formatting conditional on `reserve_min_life` being a number.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/cli.py:994` and `:1014` (`.lower()`) vs `:1039` (`current = codex.sync_live(root)`)
**ISSUE:** Every other identity in the command is case-normalised (`reserve`, `args.email`) but `current` is compared case-sensitively against the stored keys.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** ChatGPT emails routinely contain uppercase letters, and `cswap codex switch` proves store keys/`previous` are treated as lowercase. If `sync_live` returns the credential file's raw email, then `current in emails` is false, `email == current` never matches, and: the live account is dropped from the top of the list (no `(active)` row, no `(on credits)`), it lands in `rest` → `"exhausted": true` on the hub for the account *actually serving traffic*, `rank(usages, current, …)` can't exclude it, and the CLI prints the misleading "not stored — run `cswap codex add`" warning that invites the operator to re-add (and re-login, which revokes the live token). Real ChatGPT accounts with `Name.Surname@gmail.com` hit this deterministically.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Normalise once at the boundary: `current = (codex.sync_live(root) or "").lower()` (and assert `stored_accounts` returns lowercase), rather than sprinkling `.lower()` at call sites.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** `scripts/hub-status-push.py:34` (`DROP`) + `:48-51` (`scrub`) + `:113-115`
**ISSUE:** The payload forwarded to a public host is filtered by a two-key denylist over a schema the script does not own.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `scrub` passes through everything `cswap list --json` / `cswap codex list --json` ever add. This repo stores long-lived credentials (`add-token`, `attach-token` "year-long setup-token"), so the same store the list serialises contains token material; any future field (`tokenHint`, `slots`, `uuid`, `refreshExp`, full name) is published to `tgm-mockups.pages.dev` — a mockups CDN — automatically, every 60 s, from four machines, with no diff review of the egress. A denylist fails *open*: the safe default is the opposite of this. Emails themselves are already an account-privacy exposure on a page that shows per-account quota and credit balances (i.e. who is near-limit, useful for targeting).
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Whitelist the fields the page actually reads (the keys emitted by `--json` in `cli.py`) in `scrub`, and pin the expected key set so an unexpected extra key is dropped rather than sent.

# Adversarial code review (chunk 2/6)

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (~228–232, `login`)  
**ISSUE:** Repair path always writes tmp `auth.json` over live when JWT email matches, even if live tokens were refreshed during the long-running `codex login` subprocess.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** User runs `login` for the same account as live (or live becomes that account mid-flow). Codex CLI/daemon refreshes `~/.codex/auth.json` while login uses an isolated `CODEX_HOME`. On success, fresh live refresh is replaced by tmp login tokens → other rotation invalidates one side, 401/revoke, or “lost Pro login” again.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** After subprocess, under `store_lock`, re-read live; write live from tmp only if live is missing/unparseable, or JWT indicates revoked/expired, or explicit `--force-live`; never blind overwrite when live already has valid tokens for that email.

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (~579–581, `run_after_switch`)  
**ISSUE:** `subprocess.Popen(..., shell=True)` with caller-supplied `command`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Config/env hook like `after_switch: "restart; curl attacker"` (metacharacters, subshells) runs as the daemon user with inherited env (paths, secrets).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Drop `shell=True`; use `shlex.split` + argv list, or a fixed allowlist wrapper; reject commands containing shell metacharacters at config load.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (~451–453, ~486–487, `_auto_tick` / `usages_for`)  
**ISSUE:** `store_lock` (exclusive) is held for the whole tick, including `fetch_usage` (up to 20s × number of accounts).  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Slow ChatGPT API or many stored accounts blocks `switch`, `add`, `sync_live`, and other login paths for minutes; autoswitch stalls, manual recovery fails under load, looks like deadlock.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Under lock: sync, read paths, decide target only; release lock; fetch usage outside lock; re-acquire for validate + `_switch` (re-sync live before write).

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (~348–354, `fetch_usage`)  
**ISSUE:** All non–`token_revoked` 401s become `error="token expired …"`, which `Usage.refreshable()` treats as switch-eligible.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Revoked/invalid session with a different 401 body is ranked as “idle expired”; daemon switches to it, hits `active-usage-unknown` or flaps, skips real credits/quota candidates (behavior you already guarded against for other unreadable errors).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Only mark refreshable on explicit signals (e.g. `WWW-Authenticate`, known JSON codes); default 401 to non-refreshable unless live Codex refresh proves otherwise.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (~556–558 vs ~248–252, `_credits_account` / `on_credits_ok`)  
**ISSUE:** Credits pin reads `account` from JSON without normalizing case; emails elsewhere use `.lower()` (`email_of`, `stored_path`, `_sync_live`).  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Pin file or manual edit with mixed case → `pinned == current` false while on credits; extra switches, wrong “on-credits” branch, credit pin cleared/re-set each tick → session restarts.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** `return email.lower() if isinstance(email, str) else None` in `_credits_account`; always lower in `_set_credits_account`.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (~291–299, `parse_usage`)  
**ISSUE:** Classifying windows loops `primary_window` / `secondary_window` into one `short` and one `weekly` by overwriting—two sub-24h windows leave only the last as `short`; `weekly_pct` can stay `None`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** API exposes two short limits; `over()` / `life()` ignore the omitted window → stay on exhausted account or rank using wrong headroom vs Codex `/status`.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Collect all windows; set `short_pct = max(used_percent)` among short windows and `weekly_pct` among long (or take max used across each class), same binding-window rule as Claude `account_headroom`.

---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/macos_keychain.py` (~58–66)  
**ISSUE:** `_TIMEOUT = _timeout_from_env()` runs at import time; env changes after import are ignored.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Wrapper sets `CSWAP_KEYCHAIN_TIMEOUT_S` after importing the module (or in a child that already imported parent); daemon still uses 5s → spurious “keychain unavailable” under load despite the new knob.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Read timeout per operation (`_timeout_from_env()` at call site), or document that the variable must be set before process start / re-exec daemon after change.

No other high-confidence issues in this artifact chunk beyond the above. `oauth.py` `weekly_shift` / `weekly_life` align with documented “weekly vs 5h” semantics; no separate finding without seeing `relevant_windows` label guarantees in sibling chunks.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/codex.py:490](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/codex.py#L490)
ISSUE: Autoswitch deadlocks permanently on "active-usage-unknown" when encountering or switching to an expired token
ATTACK VECTOR: `rank()` intentionally selects accounts with expired access tokens (`usage.refreshable() == True`) under the assumption that Codex refreshes them on use. However, `_switch()` only writes the stored file into `live_auth_path()`. On the next tick, `fetch_usage()` calls `/wham/usage` with the still-expired token, receives HTTP 401, and sets `live_usage.error = "token expired..."`. `_auto_tick` hits `if live_usage.error is not None:` and immediately aborts with `reason: "active-usage-unknown"`. The daemon remains deadlocked on this account, never refreshing the token, never switching away, and never falling back to credits.
SUGGESTED FIX: In `_auto_tick`, if `live_usage.error` indicates an expired or invalid token on the active account, do not abort; treat it as needing rotation and proceed to rank remaining accounts or credits.

---

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/codex.py:289-300](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/codex.py#L289-L300)
ISSUE: Empty or non-short `secondary_window` unconditionally overwrites and clobbers valid `primary_window` weekly data
ATTACK VECTOR: In `parse_usage()`, the loop iterates over `("primary_window", "secondary_window")`. When `primary_window` contains the weekly window (e.g. `limit_window_seconds: 604800`) and `secondary_window` is present but empty or lacks `limit_window_seconds` (e.g. `{}` or `limit_window_seconds: 0`), `length` evaluates to 0. It falls into `else: weekly = window`, overwriting `weekly` with `{}`. This wipes out `weekly_pct` and `weekly_reset_at`, causing `_auto_tick` to miss over-threshold quota limits and causing `rank()` to discard the account as unreadable (`life() is None`).
SUGGESTED FIX: In `parse_usage()`, only assign `weekly = window` if `window.get("limit_window_seconds")` or `window.get("used_percent")` is actually present.

---

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/codex.py:431-439](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/codex.py#L431-L439)
ISSUE: `_switch()` and `login()` silently overwrite and destroy non-ChatGPT (API key) logins in `auth.json`
ATTACK VECTOR: `_switch()` guards against overwriting unstored accounts using `if live_email is not None and live_email != email and not stored_path(...).exists(): raise CodexError(...)`. If the live login is an API key (as recognized in `add()` when `email_of()` returns `None`), `live_email` is `None`. The guard evaluates to `False`, bypassing the check and overwriting `live_auth_path()` with target credentials. Similarly, `login()` checks `if email_of(read_auth(live_auth_path())) in (None, email)` and overwrites the live file. Any pre-existing API key login is permanently deleted without backup.
SUGGESTED FIX: Verify whether `read_auth(live_auth_path())` contains any existing non-empty credentials before overwriting, and raise `CodexError` if an unstored login or API key is present.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/codex.py:465-474](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/codex.py#L465-L474)
ISSUE: `auto_tick` holds exclusive file lock across unbounded sequential external network calls
ATTACK VECTOR: `auto_tick()` wraps `_auto_tick()` in `with store_lock(backup_root):`. Inside `_auto_tick()`, `usages_for()` sequentially executes blocking HTTPS `urllib.request.urlopen()` calls with a 20-second timeout for every stored account. Under network latency or packet loss across multiple accounts, the lock is held exclusively for tens of seconds to minutes. Any concurrent user interaction (`cswap codex switch`, `login`, `add`) or concurrent script hangs waiting on `fcntl.flock`.
SUGGESTED FIX: Fetch usages outside the lock; acquire `store_lock` only during local disk operations (`_sync_live`, reading target auth, and `write_private`).

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/codex.py:249-254](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/codex.py#L249-L254)
ISSUE: Accounts with unlimited credits are rejected by `on_credits_ok()` due to missing balance
ATTACK VECTOR: `parse_usage()` explicitly checks `credits.get("unlimited")` when computing `credits_usable`. However, `Usage.on_credits_ok()` requires `and (self.credits_balance or 0.0) >= MIN_CREDITS` (50.0). Accounts with unlimited credits typically report `None` or `0.0` for `balance`, causing `on_credits_ok()` to return `False`. When all accounts hit their quota thresholds, unlimited credit accounts are rejected from `funded` and cannot be used.
SUGGESTED FIX: Allow `on_credits_ok()` to pass if the account has unlimited credits or if `(self.credits_balance or 0.0) >= MIN_CREDITS`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/oauth.py:515](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/oauth.py#L515)
ISSUE: Case-sensitive `"5h"` string checks fail to match window labels with alternate casing
ATTACK VECTOR: `account_headroom()` normalizes labels using `label.lower()` on line 547, but line 552 checks `elif label == "5h"`, and `weekly_life()` filters via `if label != "5h"`. If the backend returns window labels in uppercase (e.g. `"5H"`), `weekly_life()` fails to filter it out and computes weekly quota headroom based on the short 5-hour window. Meanwhile, `account_headroom()` treats the 5-hour window as a weekly window and erroneously applies `weekly_shift` to it.
SUGGESTED FIX: Use `label.lower() != "5h"` in `weekly_life()` and `label.lower() == "5h"` in `account_headroom()`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/macos_keychain.py:67](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/macos_keychain.py#L67)
ISSUE: `_TIMEOUT` is evaluated once at module load time, ignoring daemon runtime environment updates
ATTACK VECTOR: `_TIMEOUT = _timeout_from_env()` binds at module import time. If the daemon process imports `macos_keychain` (or an orchestrator that imports it) before setting `os.environ["CSWAP_KEYCHAIN_TIMEOUT_S"]`, the timeout remains fixed at the default 5.0s. Subsequent keychain calls under high concurrency continue timing out despite the environment variable having been set by the daemon.
SUGGESTED FIX: Replace the module constant with a dynamic call to `_timeout_from_env()` in `_run_security()`, or define `_TIMEOUT` as a property/callable.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:165  
ISSUE: `add()` can overwrite a stored account with an older live refresh token.  
ATTACK VECTOR: If Codex rotates the token between the live-file read and acquiring the store lock, `add()` replaces the newer stored copy with its stale snapshot. A later switch may restore an invalid token.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Read the live auth file inside the lock, immediately before writing the stored copy.

SEVERITY: CRITICAL  
CONFIDENCE: medium  
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:204  
ISSUE: `login()` can silently replace a stored account’s credentials with a login that revokes another stored account.  
ATTACK VECTOR: The docstring records that logging into one account revoked another account’s token. The new login is saved, but the affected stored copy remains listed and can be selected later as though it works.  
SUGGESTED FIX: After login, verify existing stored logins before treating them as candidates, or mark failed verification as revoked.

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:453  
ISSUE: A failed usage lookup for the active account prevents every automatic switch.  
ATTACK VECTOR: An expired or revoked active access token, a transient HTTP failure, or malformed response returns `active-usage-unknown` before other accounts are evaluated. The daemon can stay on an unusable login indefinitely.  
SUGGESTED FIX: On active usage errors, rank healthy stored accounts and switch to one when available.

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:492  
ISSUE: The credits pin is saved before the credential switch succeeds.  
ATTACK VECTOR: If `_switch()` raises after `_set_credits_account()`, the state names an account that never became live. A later tick can make decisions using a false pin.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Save the pin only after `_switch()` succeeds.

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: medium  
FILE: src/claude_swap/codex.py:301  
ISSUE: `fetch_usage()` treats every non-revoked HTTP 401 as an expired token.  
ATTACK VECTOR: A 401 caused by another authentication failure remains eligible in `rank()` as “refreshable,” so automatic rotation may switch onto an account that cannot fetch usage.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Classify as refreshable only when the response explicitly identifies token expiry; leave other 401s ineligible.

###   PROVIDER: OPENROUTER-4

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/oauth.py:71  
**ISSUE:** `account_headroom` signature was changed to add a `weekly_shift` keyword‑only argument, but existing call‑sites (outside this diff) still invoke it with the old two‑parameter signature, causing a `TypeError` at runtime.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Any command that relies on headroom calculations (e.g., auto‑switch logic) will crash the daemon, leaving the live Codex login unchanged and potentially causing prolonged quota exhaustion.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Keep the original signature (`def account_headroom(usage, models=())`) and add `weekly_shift` as an optional positional argument with a default, or update all call‑sites to pass `weekly_shift=` explicitly.

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:447  
**ISSUE:** `run_after_switch` executes the user‑supplied `command` with `shell=True` and a plain string, opening a command‑injection surface if the `after_switch` argument contains untrusted data.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** An attacker who can influence the `after_switch` CLI argument could execute arbitrary shell commands with the privileges of the `cswap` process (e.g., `; rm -rf /`).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Invoke the command without a shell: split the string with `shlex.split` and call `subprocess.Popen(..., shell=False)`. Validate or whitelist allowed commands before execution.

---

**SEVERITY:** HIGH  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:53  
**ISSUE:** `stored_path` validates the email string for “/”, “\\”, “..”, and leading “.” but does **not** strip surrounding whitespace. An email like `" user@example.com "` passes the checks and results in a filename containing spaces, which can be confusing and may be exploited to place files in unexpected locations.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A crafted email (e.g., with trailing spaces) could cause the tool to write auth files to a path that the user does not anticipate, potentially leading to duplicate accounts or accidental overwrites.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Normalise the email with `email = email.strip().lower()` before validation, and reject empty results after stripping.

---

**SEVERITY:** HIGH  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:215  
**ISSUE:** `parse_usage` decides whether a window is “short” or “weekly” solely on `limit_window_seconds`. If **both** `primary_window` and `secondary_window` are short (≤ 24 h), the second one overwrites the first, leaving `weekly` as `None`. Consequently the weekly quota is ignored and the ranking logic may treat the account as having unlimited weekly quota.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Accounts with two short windows would be mis‑ranked as having abundant weekly quota, causing the auto‑switcher to stay on a nearly‑exhausted account and hit rate limits unexpectedly.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Preserve both windows: collect all windows in a list, then pick the *longest* one as the weekly window (or explicitly handle the case where both are short).

---

**SEVERITY:** MEDIUM  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:115  
**ISSUE:** `login` creates a temporary `CODEX_HOME` directory, runs `codex login`, and deletes the directory in a `finally` block. If the `codex` binary spawns a background process that continues to use the temporary directory after the parent exits, the `shutil.rmtree` may remove files the child still expects, leading to undefined failures.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A background Codex process could crash or lose its credentials, breaking subsequent commands that rely on the live login.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** After `subprocess.call`, verify that the `codex` process has terminated (e.g., use `subprocess.run` with `check=True`) or delay removal until the child process exits, or create the temp directory in a location that is safe to delete (e.g., use `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory` with `delete=False` and clean up only after confirming no child processes remain).

---

**SEVERITY:** MEDIUM  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/macos_keychain.py:9‑13  
**ISSUE:** `_timeout_from_env` references `os.environ` but the module does not import `os` in the shown diff. If `os` is not imported elsewhere in the file, importing the module raises a `NameError`, preventing any key‑chain functionality.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The entire macOS key‑chain integration fails at import time, causing fallback to file‑based storage and potentially exposing credentials.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add `import os` at the top of `macos_keychain.py` (or ensure it is already imported).

---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:71  
**ISSUE:** `store_lock` uses `fcntl.flock` for exclusive locking, which is advisory and only works on Unix‑like systems. On platforms that do not support `fcntl` (e.g., Windows), this will raise an `AttributeError`, breaking concurrency control.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Concurrent executions could corrupt the stored auth copies if the lock is ineffective, leading to lost refresh tokens.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Guard the import with a platform check and provide a fallback (e.g., `portalocker` or a file‑based lock) on unsupported platforms, or document that the tool is Unix‑only.

###   PROVIDER: CLAUDE

**SEVERITY: WARNING · CONFIDENCE: high**
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py:229` (`login`) and `:431` (`_switch`)
**ISSUE:** An API-key live login has no email, so `email_of` returns `None`, and both guards treat that as "no live login". The login is silently replaced.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The user runs `cswap codex login` or `cswap codex switch` while `auth.json` holds an `OPENAI_API_KEY` login. `login` runs `email_of(...) in (None, email)`, which is true, so `write_private(live_auth_path(), auth)` overwrites the key. `_switch` checks `live_email is not None and …`, so it skips the "not stored, refuse" guard and overwrites the live file. The API key is gone, and nothing was stored. This also contradicts the b9ded10 commit message ("never replace an API-key login").
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Read the live file once and refuse unless it is absent or unparseable. Use `live is None or (email_of(live) is None and not live)`, or treat any live dict that is not a ChatGPT login (no `tokens`) as protected in both places.

**SEVERITY: WARNING · CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` `parse_usage` (~L287–327) and `_auto_tick` (~L491–501)
**ISSUE:** A payload with no usable windows (empty `{}`, changed schema, `used_percent` sent as a string) yields a `Usage` with `error=None` and every percentage `None`.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `over()` returns False, so `_auto_tick` returns `below-threshold` on every tick. The daemon never switches, even when the account is exhausted, and `rank` silently drops every candidate whose usage is unparseable. Nothing is logged as an error.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** In `parse_usage`, if neither `weekly` nor `short` was found and `limit_reached` is absent, return `Usage(..., error="no usage windows in response")`. Also accept numeric strings in `pct()`.

**SEVERITY: WARNING · CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` `parse_usage` (~L294–298)
**ISSUE:** A window with a missing or zero `limit_window_seconds` falls into the `else` branch, so both windows can land in `weekly`. The second overwrites the first.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** If the API omits the length field on both windows, the short (5h) window is dropped without a trace, and `over()` only checks the weekly percentage. The 5h threshold is never enforced, so the account can run past it and the switch never triggers on that window.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Do not let a second window overwrite an already-set slot silently. Set `error` when a slot is taken twice, or assign by key (`primary_window` vs `secondary_window`) when the length is unknown.

**SEVERITY: WARNING · CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` `auto_tick` (L463) → `_auto_tick` → `usages_for` (~L503)
**ISSUE:** `store_lock` is held across `usages_for`, which makes up to N sequential HTTP calls with a 20 s timeout each.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A slow or hung `wham/usage` endpoint keeps the lock for minutes. Every interactive `cswap codex switch/add/login` (and `sync_live`) blocks behind the daemon's tick, with no message, so the user's command looks hung.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Fetch `usages_for(others)` before taking the lock. Under the lock, re-read the live login and confirm `current` is unchanged before calling `_switch`.

**SEVERITY: WARNING · CONFIDENCE: medium**
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/macos_keychain.py:56–61` (`_timeout_from_env`)
**ISSUE:** The comment says the override is for the daemon only, but the value is read at import time in every process. There is no upper bound, and `float("inf")` passes `value > 0`.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Any `cswap` invocation that inherits `CSWAP_KEYCHAIN_TIMEOUT_S=inf` (or a large value such as `1e6`) gets a timeout that effectively never fires. A hung `security` spawn then blocks the CLI indefinitely, which is the failure the "deliberately short" budget exists to prevent. The comment is also now above a `def` instead of the constant it describes.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Clamp the value (for example `min(value, 30.0)`), reject non-finite values with `math.isfinite`, and apply the override only in the daemon entry point.

**SEVERITY: INFO · CONFIDENCE: low**
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` `_switch` (~L435–438)
**ISSUE:** `_sync_live` reads the live file and `write_private` replaces it, but Codex itself is not under `store_lock`.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** If the running Codex refreshes the outgoing account's tokens between the sync read and the replace, that rotated refresh token is discarded. The stored copy keeps the pre-rotation token, which may already be invalid, and the account gets logged out. The window is small, so the impact is rare.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Before the replace, re-read the live file and compare it with the bytes that were synced. If they differ, sync again or abort the switch.

**SEVERITY: INFO · CONFIDENCE: low**
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` `run_after_switch` (end of file)
**ISSUE:** The docstring says "never raises", but only `OSError` is caught. `hasattr(proc, "wait")` is always true for a `Popen`, so that branch is dead.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A hook command containing a NUL byte raises `ValueError` from `Popen`. That escapes into `_auto_tick` while the store lock is held and aborts the tick, so the daemon's loop has to handle the exception.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Catch `(OSError, ValueError)` and drop the `hasattr` check.

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/settings.py:136-137` (+ reserve usage in `switcher.py` `_in_switch_order`)  
**ISSUE:** `reserve_min_life_pct` default and meaning changed (headroom → weekly life) with no migration for persisted values.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Upgrades keep e.g. `80.0` in config; code now treats that as “need ≥80% of the week remaining,” so the reserve is effectively never eligible and list notes show “held” while operators assume the old headroom semantics—wrong account burned or reserve never used in production.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** One-time migration (rename key or rescale stored values), or bump a settings schema version and map old headroom values to equivalent weekly-life thresholds on load.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5410-5414` (`by_reset` vs comment at 5409-5411)  
**ISSUE:** List ordering uses reset-based ranking whenever `home` is set, but the comment states the engine uses soonest weekly reset only for prefer + home **and** consume-first; plain `best` uses headroom.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `home_mode == "prefer"` with `strategy == "best"` (or any non–consume-first strategy): CLI/JSON `(next)` / `order` disagree with the daemon’s next pick; operators pre-position on the wrong slot during quota pressure.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Align `by_reset` with the same predicate the autoswitch engine uses (e.g. require `consume-first` or mirror the engine’s branch), and add a test matrix for prefer+best vs prefer+consume-first.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/switcher.py:503-509`  
**ISSUE:** `_read_credentials_settled` retries only on empty `""`, not on `None`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** If Keychain/transient errors return `None` on first read and a valid string on retry (same class of flake as `""`), switch rollback captures `None` immediately, raises `CredentialReadError`, or proceeds without a settled baseline—failed switches or rollback that cannot restore during heavy Keychain load (~70 sessions).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Retry when `creds is None` the same as empty string, or treat `None` and `""` as “unsettled” until delays exhaust, then fail explicitly.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5438-5446`  
**ISSUE:** Reserve row when `oauth.weekly_life(...)` returns `None` falls into the “held: 0% of week” branch but stays in `reserve_rows`, not `unusable`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Missing/stale weekly usage on the reserve slot shows a misleading held message and keeps the reserve in the “last resort” band; users think the reserve is policy-blocked when the engine may lack data and behave differently (skip vs attempt).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** If `week is None`, use a distinct note (e.g. “no weekly data”) and classify consistently with engine eligibility (likely `unusable` or same bucket as `life is None`).

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/settings.py:158-164` + `SETTING_SPECS` `weeklyThreshold` / `preFreshenThreshold`  
**ISSUE:** `weekly_shift` is zero when `weekly_threshold <= threshold`, with no guard if operators set weekly **below** threshold expecting earlier weekly switches.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Misconfiguration (`weekly_threshold` < `threshold`) silently disables weekly discounting (`weekly_shift == 0`); weekly windows keep switching at `threshold`, leaving large weekly quota stranded (the problem the feature comment describes).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** On load/validate, warn or clamp (e.g. require `weekly_threshold == 0` or `weekly_threshold >= threshold`) and document in `SettingSpec` help.

**SEVERITY:** INFO

**ISSUE:** `codex_after_switch` is an unvalidated shell string executed detached after Codex switch (per field comment).  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Any party who can write autoswitch settings (shared backup dir, synced dotfiles, compromised editor) gets arbitrary command execution on switch—outside the normal OAuth/token trust boundary.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Restrict to an allowlist of commands/paths, require explicit opt-in flag, or run via a fixed wrapper script path configured separately from arbitrary strings.

###   PROVIDER: AGY
###############################################################

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/switcher.py:503-510](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/switcher.py#L503-L510)
ISSUE: `_read_credentials_settled` retry loop is completely neutralized by `CredentialStore`'s 60-second Keychain cooldown.
ATTACK VECTOR: When a macOS Keychain `security` timeout occurs during a credential read, `CredentialStore._kc_call` marks `self._keychain_usable_cache = False` and sets a 60-second cooldown (`KEYCHAIN_RECHECK_COOLDOWN_S = 60.0`). In `_read_credentials_settled()`, the backoff loop sleeps for 0.5s, 1.0s, 2.0s, and 4.0s (total ~7.5s). On every retry invocation of `_read_credentials()`, `self._use_keychain()` returns `False` because the 60-second cooldown has not elapsed. Consequently, the retries never query the Keychain at all, immediately return `""`, and fail. The retry loop is entirely ineffective against the exact timeout condition it was designed to resolve.
SUGGESTED FIX: Reset `self._store._keychain_usable_cache = None` and `self._store._keychain_disabled_until = 0.0` before each retry in `_read_credentials_settled()`, or add a method on `CredentialStore` to force-probe the Keychain during settled reads.

---

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5438-5450](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/switcher.py#L5438-L5450)
ISSUE: Reserve accounts with exhausted 5-hour quota (`life <= 0`) are misclassified as usable "(reserve — last resort)" and ranked ahead of unusable slots.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_in_switch_order`, the branch `elif num == reserve:` precedes `elif life <= 0:`. If the reserve account has exhausted its 5-hour window (`life <= 0`), but retains weekly quota (`week >= settings.reserve_min_life_pct`), it enters the reserve branch, is annotated `"(reserve — last resort)"`, and is appended to `reserve_rows`. In both CLI text and `--json` ordering, `reserve_rows` is placed ahead of `unusable` slots. In production, switching to an account with `life <= 0` immediately causes 429 rate-limit errors across active sessions. Furthermore, when `week < settings.reserve_min_life_pct`, the account is protected/held and unusable, but is still appended to `reserve_rows` rather than `unusable`.
SUGGESTED FIX: Check `elif life <= 0:` before checking `elif num == reserve:`, and if `week < settings.reserve_min_life_pct`, route the held reserve to `unusable.append(row)` instead of `reserve_rows.append(row)`.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5298-5301](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/switcher.py#L5298-L5301)
ISSUE: `_in_switch_order` call in human CLI text mode lacks exception handling, causing `cswap list` to crash on ranking failures.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `list_accounts`, the JSON mode wraps `_in_switch_order` in `try...except Exception:` to ensure the listing degrades gracefully if ranking or settings resolution fails. However, the text mode call at line 5299 has no exception handling. If `_in_switch_order` encounters any unexpected error (e.g., corrupted sequence/settings, unexpected dict shapes, or math exceptions), `cswap list` terminates with an unhandled exception and crash traceback instead of displaying account information.
SUGGESTED FIX: Wrap the text-mode call to `self._in_switch_order` in a `try...except Exception:` block that logs the exception at debug level and falls back to `(accounts_info, {})`.
