SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/settings.py:165-167](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/settings.py#L165-L167)
ISSUE: `weekly_shift` silently discards user configuration when `weekly_threshold < threshold`.
ATTACK VECTOR: `AutoSwitchSettings.weekly_shift` computes `max(0.0, self.weekly_threshold - self.threshold)`. If a user configures `weekly_threshold` to switch off weekly quota earlier than 5-hour quota (e.g. `threshold = 95.0`, `weekly_threshold = 85.0`), `self.weekly_threshold - self.threshold` is negative, yielding `0.0`. `SettingSpec` permits any float between `0.0` and `99.9` without checking against `threshold`. As a result, the user's weekly threshold configuration is silently ignored, and the system continues switching weekly windows at `threshold` (95%) instead of the configured 85%.
SUGGESTED FIX: Enforce validation in `SettingSpec` / `parse_setting_value` requiring `weekly_threshold >= threshold` (or `weekly_threshold == 0.0`), or support signed shifts in `oauth.account_headroom`.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: [src/claude_swap/switcher.py:506-507](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/switcher.py#L506-L507)
ISSUE: `_read_credentials_settled` returns immediately on `None`, bypassing retry delays on transient file read errors.
ATTACK VECTOR: `CredentialStore._read_active_credentials` returns `ActiveCredentials(None, False)` when reading the plaintext fallback file fails due to an OS/filesystem error (`cred_file.read_text()` raising `OSError`/`PermissionError` or transient lock contention). In `_read_credentials_settled`, the condition `if creds is None or creds:` matches on the first attempt when `creds is None` and returns immediately without waiting through `CREDENTIAL_READ_RETRY_DELAYS_S`. Transient file read contention before a switch aborts immediately rather than settling.
SUGGESTED FIX: Only return early when credentials are non-empty (`if creds:`), and allow `creds is None` to enter the retry sleep loop before giving up.

---

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5469-5470](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/switcher.py#L5469-L5470)
ISSUE: `notes.setdefault(..., "(next)")` fails to designate the reserve account as `(next)` when it is the sole qualifying candidate.
ATTACK VECTOR: When all regular rotation accounts are exhausted or disabled, but the reserve account has valid headroom and weekly quota, the autoswitch daemon selects the reserve account as its next landing. However, `_in_switch_order` only assigns `"(next)"` to `ranked[0]`. Because the reserve account is kept exclusively in `reserve_rows`, `ranked` is empty, and no account in the listing is marked with `"(next)"`, hiding the true upcoming switch target from the operator.
SUGGESTED FIX: If `ranked` is empty and `reserve_rows` contains a usable reserve account (`week >= settings.reserve_min_life_pct` and `life > 0`), assign `notes[str(reserve_rows[0][0])] = "(next)"`.

---

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/settings.py:306-314](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/settings.py#L306-L314)
ISSUE: Setting `pre_freshen_threshold >= threshold` silently creates an unreachable condition in the autoswitch loop.
ATTACK VECTOR: `SettingSpec` defines `preFreshenThreshold` as a float between `0.0` and `99.9` with no relational constraint against `threshold`. In `autoswitch.py`, `_pre_freshen` is evaluated inside `if utilization < settings.threshold:` followed by `if utilization >= settings.pre_freshen_threshold:`. If an operator configures `preFreshenThreshold >= threshold` (e.g., threshold `90.0` and preFreshenThreshold `92.0`), `utilization < 90.0 and utilization >= 92.0` is impossible. Pre-freshening silently never runs, with no diagnostic message or configuration error emitted.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add cross-field validation in settings validation or `parse_setting_value` ensuring `pre_freshen_threshold < threshold` whenever `pre_freshen_threshold > 0`.

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/settings.py:~140 (property `weekly_shift`)  
**ISSUE:** `weekly_shift` accesses `self.threshold`, which is not defined in `AutoSwitchSettings`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** When `_in_switch_order` (or any other code) calls `settings.weekly_shift`, an `AttributeError` is raised, crashing the switcher daemon and aborting the auto‑switch process.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add a `threshold: float` attribute to `AutoSwitchSettings` (or rename the reference to the correct existing field) and ensure it is populated from settings.  

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/switcher.py:~511 (`_read_credentials_settled`)  
**ISSUE:** The retry helper returns an empty string (`""`) after exhausting retries, which is then used as `original_creds` without validation.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** An empty credential string is treated as a successful read; later code may attempt to roll back to or overwrite credentials with an empty value, breaking login for the user.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** After the retry loop, treat both `None` **and** empty string as a failure and raise `CredentialReadError` (or return `None`).  

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/settings.py:~150 (`codex_after_switch` field) & usage (not shown but implied)  
**ISSUE:** The `codex_after_switch` setting is a raw shell command string that is likely executed via `os.system`/`subprocess` with `shell=True` without sanitisation.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** An attacker who can modify the config could inject arbitrary commands, leading to remote code execution on the host running the switcher.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Validate the command against a whitelist of allowed patterns or, better, require the command to be supplied as a list of arguments and invoke it with `subprocess.run(..., shell=False)`.  

---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/switcher.py:~560 (`_in_switch_order` exception handling)  
**ISSUE:** All exceptions while building the ordering are caught and silently ignored, returning an empty `notes` dict.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Mis‑configuration or unexpected runtime errors (e.g., malformed settings) silently drop ordering information, confusing users and making debugging harder.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Catch only expected exceptions (e.g., `IOError`, `ValueError`) and re‑raise or log unexpected ones at warning/error level; ensure the caller can detect that ordering failed.  

---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/switcher.py:~492 (`rotation_account_numbers`)  
**ISSUE:** If `_get_sequence_data()` returns `None`, the method falls back to `{}` and may treat disabled slots as enabled because the disabled‑check relies on the missing data.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A disabled account could be selected for switching, violating the intended “disabled” contract.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Explicitly handle `None` from `_get_sequence_data()` and treat it as an empty sequence, or raise an error if the data cannot be retrieved.  

---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/switcher.py:~511 (`_read_credentials_settled`)  
**ISSUE:** The retry loop uses `time.sleep`, which blocks the thread. If the switcher is ever used in an async context, this will stall the event loop.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In an async environment the whole application could become unresponsive during the retry period.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Detect if the surrounding context is async and use `await asyncio.sleep(...)` instead, or document that the method is blocking and should only be called from synchronous code.  

---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/switcher.py:~5250 (JSON payload augmentation)  
**ISSUE:** The JSON payload returned by `list --json` is mutated to include UI‑specific fields (`order`, `note`). This mixes presentation concerns with the data layer.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Consumers that expect a pure data contract may break when unexpected keys appear, or future changes to UI logic could unintentionally affect API stability.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Separate the UI augmentation step from the core payload generation—e.g., return the raw payload and let the CLI layer add `order`/`note` only for human‑readable output.

## Findings

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5299` (text-output call site of `_in_switch_order`)
**ISSUE:** The text `list` path calls the new ordering helper with **no** error handling, while the JSON path wraps the identical call in `try/except`.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `_in_switch_order` reaches into engine internals that are not guarded by its own internal `try` (only `load_settings` is): `from claude_swap.autoswitch import MIN_USEFUL_LIFE_PCT, _home_return_margin, _seven_day_reset_ts` (an `ImportError`/rename now breaks `cswap list`, and importing `_`-prefixed symbols is exactly the kind of thing that breaks silently), `parse_model_names(settings.model)`, `oauth.account_headroom(...)`, `entry.token_dead()`, `oauth.weekly_life(...)`, `_seven_day_reset_ts(...)`. Any raise (a row whose `last_good` shape changed, an empty/None `settings.model`, a tuple arity change in `accounts_info`) propagates out of a command that previously printed fine with no ordering at all. The author's own defensive `try` in the JSON branch is proof they expected it to throw — they just forgot the human path, which is the one owners run during an incident.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Wrap the text-path call the same way (`try: accounts_info, order_notes = self._in_switch_order(...) except Exception: log + keep slot order`), or move the guard inside `_in_switch_order` so both callers get it once.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5400-5440` (`_in_switch_order` life/`exhausted` bucketing)
**ISSUE:** Threshold-discounted headroom (`weekly_shift`) is reused for *absolute* decisions — `life <= 0` → `"(exhausted)"`, `life < MIN_USEFUL_LIFE_PCT`, and the `(not usable now)` bucket.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `weekly_shift` is documented as "how many points a weekly window is **discounted** in decision headroom, so every comparison against `threshold` lands at `weekly_threshold`" — it is a comparison offset, not a physical quantity. With `threshold=10, weekly_threshold=30` (shift=20), an account with 15% real remaining quota computes `life = -5`, so it is tagged `"(exhausted)"` and dumped into the trailing "cannot be used" bucket on the live status page, and can never be `"(next)"`, even though it has a week's worth of usable quota left. The same value is then compared to `MIN_USEFUL_LIFE_PCT` (a *physical* life constant) and to `home_margin`, mixing two unit systems in one key tuple. Compounding the inconsistency, the reserve branch uses the **unshifted** `oauth.weekly_life(...)` against `reserve_min_life_pct` — one function judging the same account in two different scales, so the reserve can look healthy while its siblings are marked exhausted.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Compute two values: `abs_life = oauth.account_headroom(entry.last_good, models)` for the `(exhausted)`/`(not usable now)`/`MIN_USEFUL_LIFE_PCT` tests, and `score = abs_life - settings.weekly_shift` only for ranking vs. `threshold`.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/settings.py:160-168` (`weekly_shift`)
**ISSUE:** `max(0.0, weekly_threshold - threshold)` makes the feature unable to move the switch point in the direction its own comment justifies, and silently ignores valid input.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The motivation comment is "leaving it at the 5h margin strands a large slice of every account each week" — i.e. the owner wants to consume **more** of the weekly window. To consume more of a window you must relax the bar relative to `threshold`. But a shift is applied as a *discount* to headroom, so it can only ever make weekly windows look *less* healthy (switch earlier / strand more), and the `max(0.0, …)` clamp forces exactly the relaxing direction (`weekly_threshold < threshold`) to `0.0`. `SETTING_SPECS` accepts `0.0–99.9` for `weeklyThreshold`, so an operator who sets `weeklyThreshold=60` with `threshold=85` gets a documented-looking config, no warning, and zero behaviour change — the knob silently does nothing on a machine where it is supposed to reclaim weekly quota. Same shape of bug in the property's `<= 0` branch: it conflates "unset" with "explicitly 0".
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Allow the signed value (`return self.weekly_threshold - self.threshold`, no clamp) or, if the discount direction really is intentional, reject/validate `weekly_threshold >= threshold` at the spec layer and log when a configured value is discarded.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/settings.py:133-137` + `SETTING_SPECS["autoswitch.reserveMinLifePct"]`
**ISSUE:** `reserve_min_life_pct` was redefined (default `80.0` → `20.0`, measured against "the binding window" → against weekly life) under an unchanged, already-persisted key name.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Every install that ever wrote this key (or that materialises defaults into its config file) still holds `80`, but the value is now compared against `oauth.weekly_life(...)` (`switcher.py:5420`, `week >= settings.reserve_min_life_pct`). The reserve — the "has to stay usable" owner account, and the only landing option when nothing else qualifies — is then rejected unless 80% of its week remains, so late in the week the daemon has *no* last-resort account at all and does something worse (refuses the switch / burns a normal pool account). Conversely, a user who tuned `20` under the new semantics on one host and syncs config to a host running older code gets the opposite behaviour. Nothing in the diff bumps a config version, renames the key, or migrates the stored value, and the help text was rewritten in place so `cswap autoswitch help` no longer describes what existing configs mean.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use a new key (`reserveMinWeekLifePct`) and translate the old one on read, or add a settings version/migration step; at minimum warn on load when a persisted value is in the old range.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/switcher.py:502-509` (`_read_credentials_settled`)
**ISSUE:** The retry cannot distinguish "Keychain busy" from "there genuinely are no credentials", logs nothing about either, and blocks the switch critical path for up to 7.5 s.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Three production failure modes: (1) An empty/absent credentials file is a legitimate state (logged out, cleared by another tool, first-run). Every switch attempt then sleeps the full 0.5+1+2+4 s and raises the *same* `CredentialReadError` as before — turning an instant, diagnosable refusal into a 7.5 s stall per attempt in a daemon loop that runs on a timer, and (because there is no log line at all, not even `debug`) making it indistinguishable from the Keychain timeout it was written for. The post-mortem in the comment ("2026-10-23 … five at-limit switches failed on it") is unrepeatable: with no retry counter or warning emitted, the next occurrence of "Keychain busy" is again invisible. (2) The loop only retries the `""` result; if `security` *raises* under contention — the other documented macOS failure — there is no retry and no backoff. (3) The retries land inside the rollback transaction, widening the read-baseline→write window by 7.5 s on a host with ~70 concurrent Claude sessions, so a competing `cswap` can commit between the settled read and this process's write; on failure the rollback then restores a baseline that was never this process's own.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Log a warning on the first empty read (with attempt count and elapsed) so the condition is observable; distinguish empty-because-missing from empty-because-timeout (check the item exists / mtime unchanged) and bail immediately in the former case; also catch-and-retry the exception path with the same delay budget.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5368-5475` (`_in_switch_order`)
**ISSUE:** A second, approximate implementation of the engine's ranking now lives in the presentation layer, and the CLI claims it is authoritative.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The header is now `"(in the order they will be used)"` and the JSON adds `order`/`note` fields the status page keys on, but the ranking is a re-derivation of autoswitch policy: hardcoded `getattr(settings, "strategy", "best") == "consume-first"` instead of the engine's own constant/enum, `_home_return_margin` and `_seven_day_reset_ts` imported as private symbols, and its own bucket rules. Two concrete drifts: (a) if the real strategy values differ from the literal (or the attribute name is wrong — `getattr` with a default silently yields `"best"` and the "wrong rule" branch on a typo), `by_reset` is false and the list shows most-headroom-first while the daemon switches soonest-reset-first; the status page will confidently name a different `(next)` account than the one that gets switched, and the only trace is a `debug` line. (b) The docstring admits "the engine re-ranks on fresh data at every switch", yet the JSON now ships `order` as if it were a decision. The same call also drags credential/token machinery (`resolve_account`, `entry.token_dead()`, `load_settings`) into a read-only listing that the comment says is polled by a live status page — every poll now touches Keychain/token state and pays that cost at poll frequency, with retry sleeps on top.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Either export a public `rank_candidates(...)` from `autoswitch` and have both the engine and the list call it, or keep the display heuristic but label it as an estimate ("likely order, recomputed at switch time") and drop `order` from the JSON contract.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/switcher.py:5277-5293` (JSON `order`/`note` enrichment)
**ISSUE:** A failure partway through the enrichment leaves a half-applied payload, and the only signal is a `debug` log.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `rank`/`note` are assigned account-by-account inside the `try`, so an exception on the 20th of 40 slots yields a payload where the first 19 have integer `order` and the rest have `null` — a consumer that sorts or filters on `order` (the status page) will render 19 accounts in switch order and silently append the rest in slot order, with no `order_available`/degraded flag to tell it anything happened. The rest of the batch gets `note: null`, which is also a type change for a consumer that assumed a string. The inline comment explicitly says "a silent miss here hid every ordering bug from the status page" — and the fix keeps it silent: `logger.debug` is not emitted at normal levels, so the exact regression class they describe stays invisible in production. (Related, lower impact: `notes` strings embed `—` and interpolated floats, e.g. `"(reserve — held: 0% of week < 30%)"`, which conflates `week is None` with `week == 0` — "no data" and "0% left" print identically.)
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Build the `rank`/`note` maps fully, then assign in a second loop (all-or-nothing), log at `warning`, and set an explicit flag (e.g. `payload["switch_order_available"] = False`) so consumers can tell missing ordering from slot order.

Adversarial review of **chunk 4/6** (test-only diffs). Findings are limited to what the artifact shows; production `claude_swap.*` is not in this chunk.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `tests/test_home_near_limit.py` (~94–115)  
**ISSUE:** `h.switcher.switch_to` is monkeypatched but never restored in `finally` (only `mod.latest_session_activity_ts` is).  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Later tests in the same session that rely on the real `switch_to` can see wrong call order, double-wrapped behavior, or order-dependent flakes when pytest collection order changes.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** In the same `finally` block, save `original_switch = h.switcher.switch_to` before patching and assign it back after the assertion.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `tests/test_home_near_limit.py` (~121–141)  
**ISSUE:** The scan stub uses bare `except Exception` and only asserts `seen[-1] is not None`, not that `lastSwitchAt` changed or matches the switch under test.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A broken `state_path`, partial write, or stale state still yields `None` or an old timestamp; failures look like “lastSwitchAt missing” instead of the real bug, and a wrong non-`None` value could pass if something else set the field.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Drop the broad except (or re-raise after record). Assert `lastSwitchAt` increased vs a pre-tick snapshot and aligns with the switch event time/window.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `tests/test_codex.py` (~348–352)  
**ISSUE:** `test_usage_is_fetched_once_per_account_per_tick` checks `sorted(calls) == [...]` but not `len(calls) == 3` (or per-email counts).  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** An implementation that skips one stored account but never calls `fetch_usage` for it still produces the same sorted triple if the bug is “only fetch live + subset”; duplicate fetches for one email are caught, but **zero** fetches for a listed account are not distinguished from “exactly once each” without an explicit count invariant.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** `assert len(calls) == 3` and `assert len(set(calls)) == 3`, or `collections.Counter(calls) == Counter({...})`.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `tests/test_codex.py` (~248–261)  
**ISSUE:** `test_a_hook_that_cannot_start_is_reported_not_raised` only checks event fields, not post-conditions on live auth / stored tokens after `afterSwitch` failure.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** If production switches auth first and then runs the hook, a failed `Popen` leaves the process on the new account with no restart and no assertion that live/stored JSON still match the intended `to` account—regressions that corrupt or half-apply auth could stay green.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** After the tick, assert `email_of(read_auth(live_auth_path())) == event["to"]` and stored refresh token for both `from`/`to` accounts unchanged except the intended switch.

---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `tests/test_codex.py` (~403–411)  
**ISSUE:** `TestReviewPass2.test_an_expired_token_stays_a_candidate_after_readable_ones` locks in ranking `idle@x.com` (usage error: token expired) ahead of switching away from exhausted active usage.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In production, auto-switch may target an account whose usage API fails until Codex refreshes on use—extra failed switches, churn, or sitting on expired credentials if refresh on switch fails (network, revoked refresh).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** If that churn is unacceptable, add an integration test for “switch to expired-idle succeeds and usage readable after switch”; if intentional, document the accepted failure mode in the test name/docstring (already partly there).

---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `tests/test_codex.py` (~193–214)  
**ISSUE:** `test_login_runs_in_a_throwaway_home_and_leaves_the_live_login` asserts exact `stored_accounts == ["live@x.com", "new@x.com"]` (ordered list).  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Any implementation change to sort order, insertion order, or set semantics breaks CI without a behavioral regression; ordered equality is brittle for a non-contract detail.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use `assert set(codex.stored_accounts(env)) == {"live@x.com", "new@x.com"}` unless stable ordering is a guaranteed API.

**SEVERITY:** INFO

**FILE:** `tests/test_codex.py` (`_login` ~49–51, used widely)  
**ISSUE:** Tests seed live auth via `live_auth_path().write_text(...)` without asserting mode `0o600`, while `test_add_stores_a_private_copy_keyed_by_email` strictly checks stored file mode.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Production bugs that chmod only on `add`/`write_private` but not on live `auth.json` (or login copy-out) would not be caught by most codex tests that only use `_login`.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add one test that after `_login` or successful `login()`, `stat.S_IMODE` on `live_auth_path()` is `0o600` (if that is the product contract).

###   PROVIDER: AGY
###############################################################

SEVERITY: CRITICAL  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:160  
ISSUE: Daemon deadlocks in `active-usage-unknown` when the live account's token expires or is revoked  
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_unknown_active_usage_never_switches`, if `fetch_usage` on the active account returns any error (`URLError`, expired access token, or revoked login), `auto_tick` short-circuits with `{"reason": "active-usage-unknown"}` and refuses to rotate. While this protects against transient network hiccups, permanent auth failures (e.g. `login revoked` or an idle account with an expired access token that cannot refresh) will permanently stall the rotation loop. The switcher sits on the dead account indefinitely instead of failing over to valid stored accounts.  
SUGGESTED FIX: In `auto_tick`, distinguish transient network errors from permanent authentication/revocation errors. If the active account returns an unauthorized/revoked/expired error, treat the active account's life as zero and allow failover to healthy stored accounts.

---

SEVERITY: CRITICAL  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: tests/test_home_near_limit.py:73  
ISSUE: Assuming a 2-hour-old near-threshold reading predates a weekly reset re-creates the 100% quota overrun bug  
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_an_old_reading_does_not_push_the_home_off`, when the macOS Keychain is unavailable and `last_good` was 94%, an age of `7200.0`s (> `TRUST_MAX_AGE_S`) causes `tick_with_entries` to return `NO_ACTION` and hold the active pin on the home account. Claude's weekly quota reset occurs on a 7-day (604,800s) cycle. A reading that is merely 2 hours old has a >98% probability of *not* having reset. Whenever a user locks their screen or Keychain becomes locked for over 2 hours while the home account was at 94%, the daemon assumes a reset occurred, holds the pin, and burns the unreadable home account past 100%—reintroducing Fault 1 of the 2026-09-23 outage.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Do not assume an old near-limit reading has reset without verifying that `now > last_known_reset_ts`. If `sentinel=USAGE_KEYCHAIN_UNAVAILABLE` and the last known reading was in the danger band (>=91%), trigger failover regardless of reading age unless a reset is proven.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:105  
ISSUE: `TestRank` fails to test `reset=None` despite the test name, risking runtime `TypeError`  
ATTACK VECTOR: `test_soonest_reset_with_real_room_first_then_low_then_unknown` purports to test that accounts with unknown resets sort after accounts with known resets. However, every account in `usages` (`active`, `late`, `soon`, `thin`, `dead`, `blind`) has an explicit float reset or the default `reset=1000.0`. Not a single account has `reset=None`. In Python 3, directly comparing `None` with `float` in sort keys raises `TypeError: '<' not supported between instances of 'NoneType' and 'float'`. The test suite fails to validate its stated contract, masking sorting crashes when OpenAI rate limit responses omit `reset_at`.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Add an account with `reset=None` to `usages` in `test_soonest_reset_with_real_room_first_then_low_then_unknown` and ensure `codex.rank` uses a sort key that safely handles `None` (e.g. `(u.weekly_reset_at is None, u.weekly_reset_at)`).

---

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: high  
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:237  
ISSUE: `after_switch` hook process leaks zombies and fails on multi-word command strings  
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_a_real_switch_starts_the_hook_with_both_accounts`, `subprocess.Popen` is invoked asynchronously (`event["afterSwitch"] == "started"`) and the process handle is discarded. In a long-running daemon, un-reaped child processes accumulate as defunct zombie processes on Unix systems. Furthermore, passing a command string with flags (e.g. `"restart-codex --force"`) to `subprocess.Popen` without `shell=True` causes `os.execvp` to search for an executable literally named `"restart-codex --force"`, raising `FileNotFoundError`.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Parse `after_switch` using `shlex.split(cmd)` if passing a command sequence, and either set `signal.signal(signal.SIGCHLD, signal.SIG_IGN)` or reap background processes to prevent zombie accumulation.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: medium  
FILE: tests/test_home_near_limit.py:94  
ISSUE: Synchronous transcript scan inside `tick_with_usage` blocks daemon execution for up to 65 seconds  
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_at_limit_switch_lands_before_the_transcript_walk`, moving `latest_session_activity_ts` after `switch_to` only solved the switch delay; the transcript walk still executes synchronously within `h.tick_with_usage()` solely to label the switch event (`event.gate == "quiet"`). In production, transcript walks take 47-65s on large projects. This completely blocks the daemon loop, delaying monitoring and switches across all other managed accounts.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Offload `latest_session_activity_ts` labeling to a background thread/task or label the emergency switch immediately as `gate="urgent"` without a synchronous transcript walk.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: medium  
FILE: tests/test_expiring_quota_first.py:45  
ISSUE: Unknown resets sort strictly after far-future resets, starving healthy secondary accounts  
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_unknown_resets_sort_after_known_ones`, Account 2 (90% life remaining, `reset=None`) is ranked behind Account 3 (50% life remaining, `_R_LATEST`). When secondary accounts are newly added or idle, rate limit headers have not yet populated `reset_at`. The scheduler will prioritize draining an account that resets 6.9 days from now over an account with 90% available quota, defeating load distribution whenever an account's reset timestamp is unknown.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Only prioritize soonest reset when the reset timestamp is imminent (e.g. within 24-48 hours). If a known reset is in the distant future (`_R_LATEST`), sort by remaining headroom so accounts with unknown resets and high remaining life are not starved.

SEVERITY: WARNING  
CONFIDENCE: medium  
FILE: tests/test_codex.py:269  
ISSUE: Unhandled empty sequence when all accounts hit 100% quota and no accounts have credits  
ATTACK VECTOR: `TestCredits` tests assume that when all accounts hit 100% quota, at least one account has a credits balance (`30000`, `34000`, etc.). For standard users who only maintain ChatGPT Pro subscriptions without API credits (`credits_usable=False` or `credits_balance=None`), the candidate pool for credits fallback is empty. Finding the highest balance via `max()` over an empty list raises an unhandled `ValueError: max() arg is an empty sequence`, crashing the daemon tick.  
SUGGESTED FIX: Guard the credits fallback selection with an emptiness check before calling `max()`. If no accounts have usable credits, emit an `exhausted-all-accounts` event rather than raising `ValueError`.

###   PROVIDER: OPENROUTER-4
###############################################################

**Finding 1**  
**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** tests/conftest.py (the `env` fixture in `test_codex.py`)  
**ISSUE:** The `env` fixture mutates the global environment variable `CODEX_HOME` for the entire Python process. When the test suite is executed with parallel workers (e.g., `pytest -n auto`), multiple tests will share the same process‑wide environment and therefore the same `CODEX_HOME` value. This creates a race condition where one test can overwrite the temporary directory of another, leading to flaky failures, data‑corruption, or false positives.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In production‑like CI runs that enable parallelism, tests that rely on isolated `CODEX_HOME` will interfere with each other, causing nondeterministic behavior and potentially masking real bugs in the `claude_swap.codex` module.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Scope the environment change to the subprocess that needs it instead of the whole process, or use `monkeypatch.setenv` inside each test that requires it and restore the original value after the test. A safer pattern is:

```python
@pytest.fixture
def env(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    home = tmp_path / "codex-home"
    home.mkdir()
    old = os.getenv("CODEX_HOME")
    monkeypatch.setenv("CODEX_HOME", str(home))
    yield home
    if old is None:
        monkeypatch.delenv("CODEX_HOME", raising=False)
    else:
        monkeypatch.setenv("CODEX_HOME", old)
```

---

**Finding 2**  
**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py → `TestLogin.test_an_aborted_login_stores_nothing`  
**ISSUE:** The test forces `subprocess.call` to return a non‑zero exit code, simulating a failed `codex login`. The `codex.login` implementation creates a temporary “throwaway” home directory before invoking the external command, but the test never verifies that this temporary directory is removed when the login aborts. If the implementation forgets to clean up on error, the directory (and any partially written files) will remain on disk.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Accumulating stray temporary directories on a CI runner or developer machine can exhaust disk space over time and may interfere with subsequent test runs that assume a clean state.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Extend the test to assert that the temporary directory does not exist after a failed login, and ensure the production code deletes the directory in a `finally` block:

```python
def test_an_aborted_login_stores_nothing(...):
    ...
    with pytest.raises(codex.CodexError):
        codex.login(env)
    assert not os.path.isdir(seen["home"])   # new assertion
```

**Finding 3**  
**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py → `TestAfterSwitch.test_a_real_switch_starts_the_hook_with_both_accounts`  
**ISSUE:** The test monkey‑patches `subprocess.Popen` with a lambda that returns `None`. The real `codex.auto_tick` code may later access attributes of the returned `Popen` object (e.g., `.pid`, `.wait()`). By returning `None`, the test masks potential `AttributeError`s that would surface in production.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** If the production path expects a proper `Popen` instance, a regression that inadvertently returns `None` would go unnoticed because the test suite would still pass. This creates a hidden reliability risk.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Replace the lambda with a lightweight mock object that mimics the required interface:

```python
class DummyPopen:
    def __init__(self, *_, **__):
        self.pid = 12345
    def wait(self, timeout=None):
        return 0

monkeypatch.setattr(codex.subprocess, "Popen", lambda *a, **kw: DummyPopen())
```

**Finding 4**  
**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py → `TestLogin.test_login_runs_in_a_throwaway_home_and_leaves_the_live_login` (the `fake_call` implementation)  
**ISSUE:** `fake_call` writes the new `auth.json` file directly with `json.dump(..., fh)`. In a real environment the `codex login` command would likely write atomically (write‑to‑temp then rename). The test’s direct write could leave a partially written file if an exception occurs inside `json.dump`, which would corrupt the live auth file for subsequent test steps.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A corrupted `auth.json` could cause later tests to fail in obscure ways, making debugging harder.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use an atomic write pattern in the fake implementation, mirroring production behavior:

```python
def fake_call(argv, env):
    tmp_path = os.path.join(env["CODEX_HOME"], "auth.json.tmp")
    with open(tmp_path, "w") as fh:
        json.dump(_auth("new@x.com"), fh)
    os.replace(tmp_path, os.path.join(env["CODEX_HOME"], "auth.json"))
    return 0
```

**Finding 5**  
**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py → `TestReviewPass2.test_an_account_name_cannot_leave_the_store`  
**ISSUE:** The parametrized invalid names include `"../x"`, `"a/b@x.com"`, `".hidden"`, and `""`. However, the test does **not** cover the case of a name that resolves to a path outside the store via symbolic links or Unicode normalization tricks (e.g., `"\\u202e"`). If `codex.stored_path` later uses `os.path.join` without sanitizing such inputs, an attacker could escape the store directory.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A malicious user could craft an account identifier that writes files outside the intended storage area, potentially overwriting arbitrary files on the host.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add a test case for a Unicode‑trick name and ensure `stored_path` normalizes and validates the final resolved path against the store root:

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("name", ["\u202eevil", "subdir/../evil"])
def test_path_traversal_is_blocked(env, name):
    with pytest.raises(codex.CodexError):
        codex.stored_path(env, name)
```

SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_keychain_busy.py:27
ISSUE: Unswitchable slot with stale usage permanently blocks `AllExhaustedEvent`, trapping the daemon in an indefinite 15s spin-loop.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_a_slot_whose_backup_is_unreadable_blocks_the_exhausted_verdict`, the test manually injects `{"3": _usage(40)}`. In production, when Keychain is busy or corrupted, slot 3's backup cannot be read, meaning fresh usage credentials cannot be retrieved for slot 3. If slot 3 has stale usage in the usage store showing remaining life, the engine considers slot 3 "not exhausted" and suppresses `AllExhaustedEvent`. However, because slot 3 is omitted from `switchable_account_numbers`, the engine cannot switch to it either. When active slots 1 and 2 hit 100% usage, the fleet is paralyzed: instead of declaring exhaustion and entering a 600s sleep, the engine repeatedly returns `TickOutcome.BLOCKED` every 15 seconds, hammering Keychain and spinning indefinitely while live sessions fail on 429 rate limits.
SUGGESTED FIX: Base the exhaustion check strictly on slots that are present in `switchable_account_numbers` (or verify slot credentials are currently readable before allowing that slot's quota to veto the fleet-exhausted verdict).

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_keychain_busy.py:64
ISSUE: `switch_to(json_output=True)` raises an unhandled `Exception` on empty live credentials instead of returning structured JSON.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_a_read_that_never_settles_still_refuses`, calling `switch_to("2", json_output=True)` raises `Exception("refusing to overwrite its backup")` (requiring a `# noqa: BLE001` catch-all in the test). When invoked from the CLI or headless scripts (e.g., `cswap switch 2 --json`), callers expect a structured JSON response (`{"switched": False, "reason": "..."}`). Raising an unhandled exception causes the process to crash with an unformatted Python traceback on stderr rather than valid JSON on stdout.
SUGGESTED FIX: Catch the credential read failure within `switch_to` and return `{"switched": False, "error": str(e)}` when `json_output=True`.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py:49
ISSUE: Early-exit scan optimization fails to early-exit on the quiet path, guaranteeing full 9,500+ file stat scans on every switch.
ATTACK VECTOR: `test_all_old_transcripts_need_the_full_scan_and_report_the_newest` asserts that when all transcripts are older than `stop_at` (the quiet window), `latest_session_activity_ts` stats every file to find `max(mtime)`. The module docstring notes the projects directory contains 9,530 files (12.5 seconds per scan). Because switches only trigger when the user is quiet (all transcripts older than `stop_at`), the early-exit optimization will never trigger during actual switches, forcing the daemon to pay the full 12.5s disk scan delay on every switch.
SUGGESTED FIX: Return `None` immediately if no file exceeds `stop_at`, rather than performing a full scan to identify the absolute newest among inactive files.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py:31
ISSUE: Early-exit returns an arbitrary transcript timestamp rather than the newest active one, skewing quiet wait calculations.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_it_stops_at_the_first_transcript_inside_the_window`, `latest_session_activity_ts` returns the first transcript encountered where `mtime >= stop_at`. Directory iteration order in `os.scandir` is non-deterministic (filesystem/inode order). If file A was modified 59s ago and active file B was modified 1s ago, the function can return file A. When `_session_quiet()` computes remaining wait time (`QUIET_WINDOW_S - (now - ts)`), it calculates `60 - 59 = 1s` instead of `60 - 1 = 59s`. When cached via `SESSION_SCAN_CACHE_S`, the engine can judge the session quiet prematurely and execute a switch while the user is actively writing in session B.
SUGGESTED FIX: Track the maximum `mtime` among active files or return a boolean flag indicating activity rather than returning an arbitrary timestamp from an early-exit match.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py:58
ISSUE: `test_an_unreadable_entry_never_raises` tests file extension filtering rather than filesystem read errors.
ATTACK VECTOR: `test_an_unreadable_entry_never_raises` creates a readable `not-a-transcript.txt` file to test that non-jsonl files are skipped. It never tests actual unreadable entries (e.g. `PermissionError`, dangling symlinks, or `FileNotFoundError` from transcripts unlinked/rotated by Claude Code while `os.scandir` is running). If `latest_session_activity_ts` lacks an `except OSError` guard around `os.stat`, concurrent transcript rotation by active sessions will crash the daemon during its directory sweep.
SUGGESTED FIX: Wrap `os.stat()` in `try ... except OSError:` inside `latest_session_activity_ts`, and update the unit test to verify unreadable files (e.g., `chmod 000`) and missing files do not raise.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_pre_freshen.py:164
ISSUE: `_switch_timing` state cleanup is bypassed if `switch_to` raises an exception instead of returning a failure dict.
ATTACK VECTOR: `test_an_aborted_switch_leaves_no_timing_behind` verifies that when `switch_to` returns `{"switched": False}`, `_switch_timing` is reset to `None`. However, `switch_to` can raise unhandled exceptions (e.g. Keychain busy, backup refusals, network socket errors). If `_perform` only resets `_switch_timing` on explicit return values and lacks a `try ... finally: self._switch_timing = None` block, any raised exception leaves `_switch_timing` set on the engine instance. The subsequent switch then attributes the prior failed attempt's freshen duration and attempt count to itself, corrupting metrics.
SUGGESTED FIX: Wrap the switch execution block in `try ... finally: self._switch_timing = None` to ensure timing state cannot leak across switch attempts.

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_reserve_account.py:99
ISSUE: Reserve floor assumes 7-day usage metrics are always present, risking errors on accounts reporting only 5-hour quotas.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `test_a_busy_5h_window_does_not_hold_the_reserve`, test fixtures were changed from `_usage` to `_usage7` to test the rule "usable until 80% of its WEEK is spent". If an account tier does not report a weekly quota window or if the API returns a usage object with `seven_day = None`, reading weekly usage attributes directly will raise `AttributeError`/`TypeError`, or evaluate `None` as 0% life, permanently holding the reserve account even when healthy.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add a defensive check verifying `entry.last_good.seven_day` is not `None` before checking weekly usage; fall back to the 5-hour window or default floor if weekly usage is unavailable.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py (`test_an_unreadable_entry_never_raises`)
ISSUE: The test name promises an unreadable entry, but the test only writes a non-transcript `.txt` file, which the glob never matches.
ATTACK VECTOR: A dangling symlink named `*.jsonl`, or a transcript deleted between `scandir` and `stat` (a common race in an active projects dir), can make `os.stat` raise. Nothing in this file exercises that path, so an uncaught `OSError` in the early-exit loop would stop the daemon's tick and the test would still pass.
SUGGESTED FIX: Create a dangling symlink `broken.jsonl` (or `chmod 000` where supported) next to a readable transcript, then assert the call returns a timestamp and does not raise.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_pre_freshen.py (`spy` helper and the `TestPreFreshen` class)
ISSUE: Every pre-freshen test replaces `_freshen_target` wholesale, so no test checks that a refreshed credential is written back to the candidate's backup.
ATTACK VECTOR: Refresh tokens rotate. If the pre-band refresh succeeds at the provider but the new token is not persisted (write failure, lock contention, or a concurrent switch reading the old backup), the candidate's refresh token is invalidated and the slot later fails with `invalid_grant`. Because the quarantine tests fire on that same error, the slot could be quarantined for a problem the engine caused. The suite gives false confidence here.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add one test that calls the real `_freshen_target` against a fake token endpoint and asserts the backup file holds the rotated refresh token afterward, plus one that a write failure leaves the old backup intact.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_keychain_busy.py (`test_a_read_that_never_settles_still_refuses`)
ISSUE: The `else` branch fails the test whenever `switch_to` returns normally, regardless of the returned `{"switched": False, ...}` payload.
ATTACK VECTOR: A correct implementation that refuses by returning a non-switched result (which the `json_output=True` path suggests is a supported shape) fails this test with the misleading message "switch went through". The test then couples to the exception-raising contract rather than to the actual outcome, so a valid refactor breaks it.
SUGGESTED FIX: Accept either outcome: if it returns, assert `not result.get("switched")`; if it raises, assert the message. Fail only when the switch actually happened.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_reserve_account.py (`reserved()` fixture default, and the `reserve_min_life_pct` comments)
ISSUE: The file's fixture default floor encodes the opposite of the owner rule quoted in `test_a_busy_5h_window_does_not_hold_the_reserve` ("usable until 80% of its WEEK is spent").
ATTACK VECTOR: The refusal and "lands at exactly its life threshold" tests run with the helper's default, where a reserve at 79.9% life is refused. Production (`AutoSwitchSettings().reserve_min_life_pct`), which the default-floor test implies is 20, would switch at 21% life. The suite's main reserve tests therefore check a floor that production does not use, and the two docstrings disagree about what "80" means. A regression in the real default is caught only by the single default-floor test.
SUGGESTED FIX: Make the helper default equal `AutoSwitchSettings().reserve_min_life_pct`, and rewrite the refusal and boundary cases with explicit floors plus comments that use one unit ("life left" or "spent").

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_preferred_home.py (`test_with_equal_resets_the_most_life_wins_not_the_sequence`)
ISSUE: The old assertion (slot 2 wins) was flipped to slot 3, and the sequence-order escape now has no test.
ATTACK VECTOR: The rewritten test documents a production behavior change, but the regression guard for the fallback sequence is removed rather than replaced. A future change that reintroduces sequence-based escape would not be caught, and the test now passes for either ranking rule under other fixtures.
SUGGESTED FIX: Keep this test, and add a separate case where the sequence and the most-life slot differ and the reset data is absent, asserting the chosen rule explicitly.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: low
FILE: tests/test_pre_freshen.py (`test_timing_counts_every_candidate_it_had_to_try`)
ISSUE: A shared `iter(["transient", "ok"])` answers every `_freshen_target` call, including any pre-band call made in the same tick.
ATTACK VECTOR: If the engine calls the freshen helper one extra time (pre-band plus switch-time in one tick), the iterator yields the wrong answer or raises `StopIteration`. The test then asserts `freshenAttempts == 2` for the wrong reason, or fails without a clear cause.
SUGGESTED FIX: Key the fake by buffer size (`PREFRESHEN_BUFFER_MS` versus `FRESHEN_BUFFER_MS`), so the answers are tied to the path under test.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: low
FILE: tests/test_session_scan.py (`latest_session_activity_ts` early-exit contract)
ISSUE: With `stop_at`, the function returns the first in-window timestamp, not the newest, but the public name and return type imply the latest activity.
ATTACK VECTOR: Any caller that reads the returned value as "last activity" (a status display, a log line, or a timing field) gets a stale value without an error. The tests only lock in the quiet verdict, so this drift would not be caught.
SUGGESTED FIX: Document the return as "some in-window timestamp when `stop_at` is given" in the docstring, or return `(ts, exact: bool)`.

Reviewed the six test files as artifacts. The dominant defect class here is **non-discriminating regression tests** — tests whose docstrings claim to pin a specific production fix but whose chosen inputs produce the same outcome under both the fixed and the buggy implementation.

---

**1. SEVERITY: CRITICAL**
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** tests/test_reserve_account.py:99
**ISSUE:** `test_a_busy_5h_window_does_not_hold_the_reserve` passes identically under the pre-fix ("binding window") implementation it claims to guard — it is a false regression guard for the 2026-09-29 22:49Z outage.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The reserve floor reads `min(5h_life, 7d_life)`, the exact bug described in the docstring. With `_usage7(85.0, 57.0)` the 5h window gives 15% life and the weekly window gives 43%; the floor is set to `reserve_min_life_pct=20.0`. Under the buggy code life = min(15, 43) = 15 < 20 → refuse; under the fixed code life = 43 ≥ 20 → switch. Wait — that ordering only discriminates if `life` means *remaining*, and here 57.0 is *used*: remaining 43 vs 15. Either way the test never checks which input is binding, and `test_the_weekly_window_is_what_the_floor_reads` (line 90, `_usage7(0.0, 50.0)`) is the *only* case that actually separates the two implementations, because there the 5h window is the empty one. The incident case therefore has zero coverage: production can regress to binding-window semantics and the suite stays green while every session stops for hours.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Make the incident case self-evidently discriminating — pick numbers where the 5h window is *worse* than the weekly one by more than the floor (e.g. `reserve_min_life_pct=20.0` with `_usage7(95.0, 10.0)`: 5% life on 5h vs 90% on the week), and assert `SWITCHED`.

**2. SEVERITY: CRITICAL**
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** tests/test_session_scan.py:55
**ISSUE:** `test_an_unreadable_entry_never_raises` never creates an unreadable entry; it only adds a non-`.jsonl` file.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A scan over 9,530 files / 10.7 GB will hit dangling symlinks, rotated `.jsonl` files that vanish between `os.walk` and `os.stat`, per-directory permission denials, and sockets/fifos named `*.jsonl` — none of which this test produces. A `not-a-transcript.txt` is filtered by extension (or is simply `stat`-able), so the test passes even if the real path has no `try/except OSError` around `stat`. The "never raises" contract is untested and a `FileNotFoundError` from one transient entry aborts the whole quiet gate, which is on the switch critical path.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Create an actual unreadable entry — `(root / "dead.jsonl").symlink_to(root / "missing")` and/or `os.chmod` a `.jsonl` to `0o000` — and assert the scan still returns the newest readable mtime.

**3. SEVERITY: WARNING**
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** tests/test_session_scan.py:32
**ISSUE:** `test_it_stops_at_the_first_transcript_inside_the_window` cannot detect the removal of the early exit.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Files are `a=10s, b=5s, c=99_999s`. The full-scan result (`b`, 5 s old) and the early-exit result both satisfy `got is not None and got >= stop_at`, so deleting the early-exit branch reintroduces the 12.5 s scan on the switch path (`quietScanMs=12546` in this file's own docstring) with no test failure. The neighbouring `test_all_old_transcripts_need_the_full_scan_and_report_the_newest` (line 47) uses ages 9,000 s / 8,000 s — both outside the window — so the early exit is never actually exercised anywhere in the file.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Put the *newest* file outside the window and an older-but-inside file that `os.walk` reaches first (assert `got == old_inside_st_mtime`, not `>= stop_at`), or count `stat` calls with a wrapped `os.scandir`.

**4. SEVERITY: WARNING**
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** tests/test_pre_freshen.py:104
**ISSUE:** The quarantine tests replace the real `_quarantine` with a 3-positional-arg lambda, so they assert an exact calling convention instead of the behaviour they name.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `test_a_dead_candidate_is_quarantined_at_the_pre_band` claims the engine "learns a lineage is dead here", but stubbing `h.engine._quarantine = lambda num, email, why: ...` discards every side effect (persistence, event emission, candidate exclusion). Production renaming `why` to a keyword, adding a fourth argument, or passing `(email, num)` makes the test error out rather than reveal the contract; conversely a `_quarantine` that records nothing keeps the test green. Same pattern at line 122 (`lambda *a`). `test_a_disabled_slot_is_not_prefreshened` (line 181) shows the suite *can* assert effects via real methods.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Keep the real `_quarantine`, spy on it with `wraps=`, and add the behavioural assertion: a second `tick_with_usage(IN_BAND)` no longer offers slot 2/3 as candidates (or the slot is absent from `switchable_account_numbers`).

**5. SEVERITY: WARNING**
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** tests/test_switcher.py:8458
**ISSUE:** `test_json_list_carries_switch_order_without_resorting` asserts `order` is a permutation of `[0, 1]` with no position coherence, and stubs both credential readers to `""` — the exact "Keychain unreadable" state this chunk exists to handle.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `orders == [0, 1]` passes for `[1, 0]` and `[0, 1]`, so the test does not check that `order` reflects list position, which is the entire contract named in the docstring ("shows the text list's order"). Separately, `_read_account_credentials` returning `""` feeds the backup-unreadable path into `list_accounts`; if that path marks a slot unusable or drops it from the ranked sequence, the assertion holds for a fleet that is silently mis-ranked in production. Note `test_list_order.py:34` treats an unreadable backup as `sentinel="re-login needed"` in *ranked* position 5, so `order` for a sentinel row is undefined by this suite.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Assert the relation, not the set: `assert [r["order"] for r in payload["accounts"]] == list(range(len(payload["accounts"])))`, and stub the readers with a plausible non-empty credential (or assert the `note` for the unreadable case explicitly).

**6. SEVERITY: INFO**
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** tests/test_preferred_home.py:53
**ISSUE:** The "exact headroom margin" test was moved from `85` to `80` without a paired just-below case, so it now tests a value strictly inside the allowed region while its name claims the boundary.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `threshold=95`, `HOME_RETURN_MIN_HEADROOM_PCT=20` → allowed home usage is `≤ 80`. The pre-image value `85` fails under the new margin, so the edit was made to restore green. If the implemented margin were `19` (an off-by-one against the constant, or `hysteresis` applied on one side only), `80` still passes and nothing in this file notices — the boundary the docstring names is only probed from one side.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add the adjacent refusal: same harness with `"1": _usage(81)` asserting `is not TickOutcome.SWITCHED` (or `home stays active`).

**7. SEVERITY: INFO**
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** tests/test_list_order.py:40
**ISSUE:** The same `autoswitch.reserveAccount` setting is expressed as an email in this file and as the slot number `"3"` in test_pre_freshen.py:113, and the ordering assertions are made against hard-coded human copy (`"(next)"`, `"(reserve — held"` with a literal em dash, `"(not usable now)"`).
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Two accepted identity formats for one setting means an unmatched value can degrade silently: `test_the_reserve_is_prefreshened_last` asserts only the *order* of refresh calls (`["2", "3"]`), which is the naive-headroom order it claims to exclude — a reserve that fails to match because `"3"` isn't the stored key keeps the test green while the real reserve is freshened first. The copy assertions couple three test files to display punctuation; rewording or localising a note breaks ordering tests that have nothing to do with wording, and the em dash is easy to mistype into a hyphen during any edit.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use one identity form consistently (email, as `test_list_order` does) and add one assertion that the hold actually applied; assert on a stable field (`kind`/`rank` reason) rather than exact note text, reserving copy assertions for a single presentation test.

###   PROVIDER: AGY

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:47
ISSUE: Setting `weekly_threshold` below `threshold` silently disables weekly threshold enforcement
ATTACK VECTOR: If an operator sets `autoswitch.weeklyThreshold = 90.0` and `autoswitch.threshold = 95.0` to be conservative with weekly quota, `weekly_shift` is clamped to `0.0`. The weekly threshold setting is silently neutralized without error or warning, and the engine continues burning weekly quota up to 95.0%.
SUGGESTED FIX: Reject `weekly_threshold < threshold` during validation in `AutoSwitchSettings` / `SettingSpec` with a descriptive `ValueError`, or support negative `weekly_shift` so weekly thresholds tighter than the 5h threshold trigger earlier as expected.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:28-36
ISSUE: Shifting weekly usage inside `account_headroom` inflates near-exhausted accounts above healthier ones during failover
ATTACK VECTOR: With `weekly_shift=4.0`, an account at 99.9% weekly utilization reports `4.1%` headroom (`100.0 - (99.9 - 4.0)`). During at-limit failover or emergency escapes where multiple accounts are above threshold, candidate ranking sorts by `-headroom`. An account with only 0.1% weekly quota remaining outranks a healthy candidate sitting at 96.0% 5h usage (4.0% headroom), causing the engine to switch to an account that immediately hits the hard 100% weekly limit.
SUGGESTED FIX: Cap discounted headroom so it cannot exceed raw remaining quota (`min(100.0 - pct, 100.0 - (pct - weekly_shift))`), or compare against `weekly_threshold` at the trigger evaluation point instead of distorting raw `account_headroom`.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:34-36
ISSUE: Discontinuous 4.0-point step function at the 100.0% boundary disrupts hysteresis and ranking
ATTACK VECTOR: At 99.99% weekly usage, calculated headroom is ~4.01%, but crossing to 100.0% disables the shift and drops headroom abruptly to 0.0%. This creates a sudden jump in candidate ranking and hysteresis evaluations rather than monotonic degradation as quota depletes.
SUGGESTED FIX: Scale the discount proportionally as usage approaches 100% instead of applying a flat subtraction with an abrupt cutoff at `pct >= 100.0`.

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:1-6
ISSUE: Model-scoped weekly windows specified in the module docstring are completely untested
ATTACK VECTOR: The specification explicitly states that "the 7d and model-scoped weekly windows switch at 99", but `TestHeadroomShift` only passes `five_hour` and `seven_day` payloads, omitting the `models` parameter and `scoped` usage payloads. If Anthropic usage payloads provide model-scoped limits under different key formats or if model matching fails, model-scoped limits will not switch at 99 unnoticed by the test suite.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add unit tests in `TestHeadroomShift` and `TestEngine` passing `models=["claude-3-5-sonnet"]` and usage dictionaries containing `scoped` windows with `pct=97.0` and `pct=99.0` to verify that model-scoped weekly limits actually trigger switches.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:60-64
ISSUE: `test_weekly_at_99_switches` omits assertions on event emission and trigger reason
ATTACK VECTOR: Unlike `test_5h_still_switches_at_95` (which asserts `any(isinstance(e, SwitchEvent) for e in h.events)`), `test_weekly_at_99_switches` only checks `TickOutcome.SWITCHED` and `active_number() == 2`. If a switch occurs for an unintended reason (e.g. unmanaged account or failover escape), the test will pass despite the weekly threshold trigger failing.
SUGGESTED FIX: Assert `assert any(isinstance(e, SwitchEvent) and e.trigger == "proactive" for e in h.events)` in `test_weekly_at_99_switches`.

SEVERITY: INFO
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:30-32
ISSUE: Exact float equality assertions risk test flakiness with real-world usage percentages
ATTACK VECTOR: `assert oauth.account_headroom(usage, weekly_shift=4.0) == 7.0` uses exact `==` comparison. While passing with integer values like 97.0, real API usage metrics often report fractional values (e.g., 97.35%), where floating-point subtraction (`100.0 - (97.35 - 4.0)`) produces precision artifacts that break exact assertions.
SUGGESTED FIX: Use `pytest.approx(7.0)` and `pytest.approx(4.0)` for all floating-point headroom assertions.


###############################################################
###   PROVIDER: OPENROUTER-4
###############################################################

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:31  
**ISSUE:** `AutoSwitchSettings.weekly_shift` can become negative when `weekly_threshold` is lower than `threshold`. The property is used to discount weekly headroom, so a negative shift would *increase* the reported headroom instead of capping it at zero.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In production, a mis‑configured `weekly_threshold` (e.g., set lower than the main `threshold`) would cause the system to think there is extra headroom, allowing switches that should be blocked and potentially exhausting quota.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Clamp the computed shift to a minimum of 0.0, e.g.:

```python
@property
def weekly_shift(self) -> float:
    return max(0.0, (self.weekly_threshold or 0) - self.threshold)
```

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:55  
**ISSUE:** `TestSettings.test_round_trips_through_config` mutates the global configuration via `set_setting` but never restores the original value. Subsequent tests that rely on default settings may see the altered value, causing flaky, order‑dependent failures.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Hidden state leakage between tests can mask bugs or cause false negatives, especially in CI where test ordering is nondeterministic.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Save the original setting before mutating and restore it in a `finally` block or use a fixture that automatically reverts changes:

```python
original = load_settings(tmp_path).weekly_threshold
try:
    set_setting(tmp_path, "autoswitch.weeklyThreshold", "99")
    assert load_settings(tmp_path).weekly_threshold == 99.0
finally:
    set_setting(tmp_path, "autoswitch.weeklyThreshold", str(original))
```

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:46‑48  
**ISSUE:** `EngineHarness` instances created in the tests are never explicitly torn down. If `EngineHarness` opens files, sockets, or spawns background threads, those resources remain alive after the test finishes, leading to file‑descriptor leaks or hanging threads in long test runs.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Accumulated leaks can exhaust system resources in CI pipelines, causing unrelated tests to fail or the test runner to hang.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add a `teardown` method to `EngineHarness` (if not present) and invoke it via a pytest fixture or `try/finally` in each test, e.g.:

```python
@pytest.fixture
def harness(temp_home):
    h = _fleet(temp_home)
    yield h
    h.close()   # or h.cleanup()
```

**1. WARNING, high confidence.** `tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:28`
ISSUE: The test named "weekly window is discounted" shows the weekly headroom rising from 3.0 to 7.0, which is the opposite of a discount.
ATTACK VECTOR: The test name and the asserted behavior disagree. A later reader, or a refactor that flips the sign of `weekly_shift`, will be judged against the wrong intent. The headroom also no longer matches the switch rule: at `seven_day=99` the switch fires, yet `account_headroom` still reports 5.0 (1 + 4) for that account.
SUGGESTED FIX: Rename the test to match what it asserts (e.g. `test_weekly_headroom_gains_the_shift`), and add a comment stating whether headroom is meant to track the switch threshold (`99 - pct`) or the 100% ceiling (`100 - pct + shift`). Then assert which one is intended.

**2. WARNING, high confidence.** `tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:34-36`
ISSUE: `account_headroom` is non-monotonic at 100%. Using the formula the other tests pin, `seven_day=99.9` gives 4.1, but `seven_day=100` gives 0.0. The 0.0 comes from an ad-hoc special case that the test enshrines.
ATTACK VECTOR: Ranking candidates by headroom picks an account at 99.9% weekly over one at 100% only by accident, and a tiny usage change flips the ordering. Any future caller that treats headroom as continuous gets a cliff of about 4 points at 100.
SUGGESTED FIX: Make the zero-clamp explicit and continuous, e.g. `max(0, min(...))` on the weekly term at the switch threshold (`weekly_threshold - pct`), and add a boundary test at 99.5 / 99.9 / 100 that asserts monotonic decrease.

**3. WARNING, medium confidence.** `tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:44-46`
ISSUE: `AutoSwitchSettings(threshold=95.0).weekly_shift == 0.0` pins the old behavior as the default. The owner's complaint was that at the default 95 the weekly quota was left unused, but the fix only takes effect after someone explicitly sets `weeklyThreshold`.
ATTACK VECTOR: Users who never set the new key keep the bug the owner reported. The docstring's "the 7d windows switch at 99" is then false for every default-configured install.
SUGGESTED FIX: Confirm the shipped default for `weekly_threshold` (outside this chunk). If it should be 99 by default, change the default and assert `AutoSwitchSettings(threshold=95.0).weekly_threshold == 99.0`. If opt-in is intended, say so in the docstring and the settings docs.

**4. WARNING, medium confidence.** `tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:49-51`
ISSUE: Only the happy path is tested. `weeklyThreshold` set below `threshold` (e.g. 90 under 95) silently yields `weekly_shift == 0.0` (tested on line 46). Out-of-range values (150, -5, "abc") are not tested at all.
ATTACK VECTOR: A user types 90 expecting weekly to switch earlier. Nothing happens and nothing is reported, so the setting looks applied while being ignored.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add a validation test: reject or warn when `weekly_threshold` is not in `(threshold, 100]`, and assert the error or warning. Add an invalid-input case for `set_setting`.

**5. INFO, medium confidence.** `tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:1-6` (docstring), `:55-66` (engine tests)
ISSUE: The docstring says model-scoped weekly windows also switch at 99, but no test covers a model-scoped key. `account_headroom` is only exercised with `five_hour` and `seven_day`.
ATTACK VECTOR: If the model-scoped path still uses the 95 threshold or is ignored in the shift, the suite stays green.
SUGGESTED FIX: Add one `account_headroom` case with a model-scoped window (whatever key the code reads) and one engine case that switches at 99 and not at 97 for that window.

**6. INFO, low confidence.** `tests/test_weekly_threshold.py:55-66`

ATTACK VECTOR: An off-by-one (`>` instead of `>=`) in either threshold check would pass, since 95 and 99 are exact hits on `>=` and would fail on `>`. Only the exact-equality case is covered for each.

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py` (auto_tick / `_credits_account` block in diff)  
**ISSUE:** Treating revoked live usage as “proceed” skips the under-threshold branch that clears the credits pin, a path that did not exist when any `error` aborted the tick.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Live account becomes revoked while `_credits_account` still points at it. `auto_tick` no longer returns on `error`, but also does not enter `if not live_usage.revoked() and not live_usage.over(...)`, so the pin is never cleared. Later ticks can keep preferring or blocking rotation around a dead account until manual intervention.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** When `live_usage.revoked()` (or before switch selection), clear the credits pin if `_credits_account(backup_root) == current`, mirroring the existing under-threshold pin clear.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `scripts/hub-status-push.py` (~122–137)  
**ISSUE:** `prev_known` is true whenever the hub read **succeeds**, including an empty `{}` map, which is indistinguishable from “we know there is no prior status” vs “we failed to see existing docs.”  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Hub returns 200 with no matching status rows (filter mismatch, empty `body`, transient indexing), but the hub still has a full document. One failed half this round merges against `{}` and `put` can still send `null` for that half and wipe the good half on the hub—the scenario the `None` return fix addresses only for **transport/read failures**, not empty successful reads.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Treat `{}` after a successful read as safe only if the response is authoritative (e.g. explicit empty set for `col=status`); otherwise use the same “partial + previous unknown → do not send” rule, or require both halves non-null unless `old.get(half)` was explicitly loaded.

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py` (~3314–3318)  
**ISSUE:** Swallowing `OSError` on gate-label `atomic_write_json` leaves in-memory `state["lastSwitchGate"]` updated only in the successful-write path—the comment says the switch landed on disk, but persisted gate state can diverge.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Disk full, permissions, or transient FS errors after a successful switch: next cycle reads old gate state from disk, may re-run quiet/gate logic or user-visible “error” paths even though credentials already switched, causing duplicate work, confusing telemetry, or a second switch attempt if downstream logic is not strictly idempotent.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** On failed label write, either avoid mutating in-memory gate before write, or set a “dirty” flag and retry label-only persistence on the next tick; log at error with host/path.

**ATTACK VECTOR:** API or proxy returns `"used_percent": "85"` for the binding weekly window; parser keeps an older window at `10`, autoswitch thinks usage is low, delays Codex switch until hard limit or user impact.

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py` (~4801–4805)  
**ISSUE:** Cache tuple now uses `self.clock()` at scan **end** (second call) while `latest` was computed with an earlier `self.clock()` in `max(latest, self.clock())`; effective cache lifetime lengthens by whole scan duration.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Very slow scans (large transcript trees, slow disks): session activity after scan started but before it finished may be reflected in `latest`, yet callers may still treat the result as fresh until TTL from end-time; quiet window can be overstated briefly vs the pre-fix “TTL from scan start” behavior.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Single timestamp at end: `end = self.clock(); latest = max(latest, end); self._session_scan_cache = (end, latest)`; document TTL as “from scan completion.”

I have launched the test suite in the background to verify the existing tests. While it runs, I am continuing the adversarial review of the diff.
SEVERITY: CRITICAL
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/codex.py:540](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/codex.py#L540)
ISSUE: Unlimited credit accounts (`credits_unlimited=True`) sort behind finite credit accounts in `funded` candidate selection.
ATTACK VECTOR: `Usage.on_credits_ok()` allows accounts with `credits_unlimited=True` and `credits_balance=None` to qualify for the credits pool. However, `_auto_tick` sorts funded accounts with `key=lambda e: -(usages[e].credits_balance or 0.0)`. For an unlimited account, `credits_balance` is `None`, evaluating to `0.0`. In ascending sort order, accounts with any positive balance (e.g. $5.00 -> `-5.0`) sort *ahead* of the unlimited account (`0.0`). In production, this causes the daemon to burn paid finite credits on secondary accounts first instead of preferring the unlimited pool.
SUGGESTED FIX: Prioritize unlimited credits over finite balances:
```python
key=lambda e: (
    not usages[e].credits_unlimited,
    -(usages[e].credits_balance or 0.0),
)
```

---

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/codex.py:301-306](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/codex.py#L301-L306)
ISSUE: Falsy `0` in `or -1` causes zero-percent windows to be miscompared and overwritten, and lacks type guarding for existing windows.
ATTACK VECTOR: In Python, `0 or -1` evaluates to `-1`. If `primary_window` has `used_percent: 0`, `(weekly.get("used_percent") or -1)` evaluates to `-1`. If `secondary_window` also has `used_percent: 0`, the check `used > -1` (`0 > -1`) evaluates to `True`, causing the second window to overwrite the first even though usage was identical. Furthermore, while `used` is guarded with `isinstance(..., (int, float))`, `short.get("used_percent")` is not: if the first window contained a non-numeric string (e.g. `"50"`), evaluating `used > (short.get("used_percent") or -1)` raises `TypeError: '>' not supported between instances of 'int' and 'str'`.
SUGGESTED FIX: Extract the percentage using a numeric helper:
```python
def _window_used(w: dict | None) -> float:
    v = (w or {}).get("used_percent")
    return float(v) if isinstance(v, (int, float)) else -1.0

if length and length <= SHORT_WINDOW_MAX_S:
    if short is None or used > _window_used(short):
        short = window
elif weekly is None or used > _window_used(weekly):
    weekly = window
```

SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: high
FILE: [src/claude_swap/codex.py:363-365](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/codex.py#L363-L365)
ISSUE: `fetch_usage` treats any HTTP 401 without `"token_revoked"` as an expired, refreshable token.
ATTACK VECTOR: If OpenAI returns HTTP 401 with codes such as `account_deactivated`, `invalid_api_key`, `session_invalidated`, or Cloudflare 401 HTML, `"token_revoked"` is absent. The code falls into `elif e.code == 401:` and sets `kind = "token expired — refreshes on the next switch to it"`. This causes `usage.refreshable()` to return `True`. `rank()` then treats the dead account as a valid candidate and switches onto it, parking the switcher on a broken login.
SUGGESTED FIX: Only treat 401 responses explicitly containing `"token_expired"` as refreshable:
```python
if e.code == 401 and "token_revoked" in body:
    kind = "login revoked — log in again: cswap codex login"
elif e.code == 401 and "token_expired" in body:
    kind = "token expired — refreshes on the next switch to it"
else:
    kind = f"http {e.code}"
```

SEVERITY: WARNING

ATTACK VECTOR: In Python, `X or {}` evaluates to `X` whenever `X` is truthy. If an existing or legacy document on the hub contains `stale` stored as a timestamp (float/int) or boolean `True`, `(old.get("stale") or {})` evaluates to that primitive instead of a dictionary. SEVERITY: WARNING
CONFIDENCE: medium
FILE: [src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3315-3330](file:///Users/greglas/DEV/claude-account-switcher/src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py#L3315-L3330)
ISSUE: Event `gate` parameter diverges from persisted disk state when the label write fails.
ATTACK VECTOR: In `_perform`, `state["lastSwitchGate"]` is initially written as `"forced"` (line 3294). If `quiet` is subsequently determined to be `True`, the code attempts to update the state file with `state["lastSwitchGate"] = "quiet"`. If `atomic_write_json` raises `OSError`, the exception is swallowed by design to avoid failing a landed switch. However, local variable `quiet` remains `True`, causing `self._emit(SwitchEvent(..., gate="quiet"))` to emit `gate="quiet"`. This produces an inconsistency where external event listeners observe `quiet`, but the on-disk state file reads `forced`.
SUGGESTED FIX: Reset `quiet = False` if persisting the updated gate label fails:
```python
try:
    atomic_write_json(self.state_path, state)
except OSError as e:
    quiet = False
    _logger.warning("switch landed; gate label not saved: %r", e)
```

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3315  
**ISSUE:** Swallowing `OSError` when writing the switch‑gate label discards state on disk.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** If the filesystem is read‑only, out of space, or the write fails, the warning is logged but the state file isn’t updated. The engine will think the switch never landed, causing repeated switches or leaving the system in an inconsistent “quiet” state.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Propagate the exception (or abort the operation) after logging, or retry the write. At minimum, fail the command so the user knows the state wasn’t persisted.

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:4804  
**ISSUE:** `_session_scan_cache` now stores `(self.clock(), latest)` instead of `(now, latest)`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The cache’s timestamp is taken after the scan finishes. Any logic that treats the first element as the *start* time for expiration will consider the cache newer than it really is, so a long‑running scan can make the cache appear fresh for the full cache‑lifetime. Subsequent calls will skip a new scan and miss recent session activity, breaking the “quiet” detection.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Store the start time (`now`) as the first element, e.g. `self._session_scan_cache = (now, latest)`. If the intention is to cache the *end* time, adjust the expiration check accordingly.
