**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:298‑306  
**ISSUE:** `parse_usage` assumes `used_percent` is numeric; if the API returns it as a string the comparison `used > (short.get("used_percent") or -1)` raises `TypeError`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A change in the OpenAI API (or a malformed response) would crash the whole usage‑parsing path, causing the autoswitch engine to abort and potentially leave the system without a usable login.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Coerce `used_percent` to `float` when possible, e.g.:

```python
def _as_float(v):
    try:
        return float(v)
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return -1.0
used = _as_float(window.get("used_percent"))
```

and use the same helper for the stored values.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** scripts/hub-status-push.py:124‑126  
**ISSUE:** The stale‑time fallback `or old.get("at")` treats a legitimate timestamp of `0` (epoch) as falsy and replaces it with `None` or a later value.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** For very old documents the UI may show an incorrect “stale” age, misleading operators.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Distinguish between `None` and falsy timestamps, e.g.:

```python
stale_ts = (old.get("stale") or {}).get(half)
if stale_ts is None:
    stale_ts = old.get("at")
d.setdefault("stale", {})[half] = stale_ts
```

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** scripts/hub-status-push.py:115‑119  
**ISSUE:** When `previous_docs` fails (returns `None`), hosts that have *both* halves present are still sent, but the `stale` field is never populated because `old` is empty.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The hub UI will display missing “stale” information for those hosts, reducing observability.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** After a successful fetch, keep the original `stale` dict even if the document body is missing, or explicitly copy it when `prev_known` is `False` and both halves are present.

---

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** tests/test_session_scan.py:166‑176  
**ISSUE:** The test monkey‑patches `os.walk` but does not restore it, so later tests that rely on the real `os.walk` may see the patched version.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Inter‑test contamination can cause flaky failures unrelated to the code under test.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use `monkeypatch.setattr` with `raising=False` and ensure the original `os.walk` is restored (e.g., via a fixture or `try/finally`).

**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** low  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:226‑229  
**ISSUE:** In `login`, the condition `if live is None or email_of(live) == email:` will overwrite a live auth file that contains a *different* email but lacks an email field (`email_of(live) is None`).  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A valid token without an email (unlikely but possible) could be silently replaced, losing the original login.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Refine the guard to also reject files where `email_of(live)` is `None` but `live` is not empty, e.g.:

```python
if live is None or (email_of(live) is not None and email_of(live) == email):
    atomic_write_json(live_auth_path(), auth)
else:
    _logger.warning("Live auth file contains unknown format; refusing to overwrite")
```

NO ISSUES FOUND is not applicable — 7 findings below. The `hub-status-push` stale-chain fix itself checks out (carried-over halves keep their original time through repeated failures, and the both-halves-failed path correctly no longer resets `at`), but several of the other "fixes" trade one failure mode for a worse one.

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py:435-441` (and `:225-230`)
**ISSUE:** The API-key guard tests `email_of(live) is None` instead of the presence of an API key — "we couldn't read an email" is used as a proxy for "this is an API-key login", and the predicate is inverted from the actual threat.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Three distinct breaks, all provable from the artifact. (a) The test fixture itself (`{"OPENAI_API_KEY": "sk-test", "tokens": None}`) shows the live auth file can hold *both* an API key and ChatGPT tokens; when it does, `email_of` returns the email, the guard passes, and `write_private(live_auth_path(), auth)` replaces the whole file — the user's API key is destroyed, which is exactly what the fix claims to prevent. (b) In `_switch`, a live file that exists but whose email can't be decoded (legacy token set, missing/undecodable `id_token` claim, partially-written file) now raises `CodexError` *before* the pre-existing "would lose that login for good" checks, so automated autoswitch fails to move at all — the old code treated `None` as "no live login to protect" and switched safely. (c) In `login()`, the same unreadable-email case silently *skips* the live write and still returns `(email, plan)` as success — re-enabling the "next sync copies the old live tokens over the fresh stored copy" loss the inline comment says the write exists to prevent, with no message to the user.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Detect on the actual field: `if live.get("OPENAI_API_KEY")` (preserve/merge the key rather than blocking), and gate the `login()` write on `live is None or email_of(live) in (None, email)` only after ruling out an API key; print (or return a flag) when the live write is skipped so success isn't reported for a no-op.

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py:496-505`
**ISSUE:** A revoked live login is now pushed *into* the switch path, but nothing in the diff excludes revoked accounts from being chosen as the switch *destination* — and the new `list --json` test asserts the opposite (`not rows["dead @x.com"]["exhausted"]`).
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `revoked()` implies usage is unknown (`limit_reached`/pct all None), so `over()` returns False and the `not live_usage.revoked()` short-circuit makes the "back under the limit, don't switch" escape unreachable: every tick with a revoked live login falls through to candidate selection. If the picker ranks/filters on `exhausted`/`limit_reached` (the field the new JSON test proves is False for a revoked account), a revoked account is a fully eligible target — the engine switches from one dead login to another, writes auth files, and since the new live login is *also* revoked the gate opens again on the next tick: permanent ping-pong churn with no working account, and the hub/list page reports those accounts as "not exhausted" (healthy). The test `test_a_revoked_live_login_is_left_not_waited_on` only covers the single-good-candidate case, so this is untested in either direction.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Filter `revoked()` (and any `error is not None`) out of the candidate set before choosing the target, and add a `if target == current: return no-switch`/no-candidates branch that surfaces a `login revoked` reason instead of switching; mark revoked accounts distinctly in `list --json` (not just `error`).

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/autoswitch.py:3313-3319`
**ISSUE:** The fix's stated goal ("a failed label write must not turn a landed switch into an error") is not achieved: it catches only `OSError`, while the sibling state write in the *same* block catches broad `Exception`.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `atomic_write_json` fails for two reasons, and only one is an `OSError`. Serialization (`json.dumps` → `TypeError` on a `datetime`/`Path`/lock object newly added to `state`) and any temp-file/cleanup helper error escape the `try`, propagate out of the landing path, and turn a successfully landed switch into a reported error — the exact outcome the comment claims to have eliminated. The asymmetry is visible in the diff itself: the marker write above uses `except Exception as e: _logger.debug(...)`, so a reviewer of only this hunk would assume both writes are protected. Also, `state` keeps `state["lastSwitchGate"] = "quiet"` in memory after the failed write, so the in-process verdict and the on-disk label diverge until the next successful write.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Match the neighbouring call: `except Exception as e` (keep the warning log), and roll back the in-memory label (`state.pop("lastSwitchGate", None)`) when the write fails so memory and disk agree.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py:255-256` (`revoked`), confirmed by `tests/test_codex.py:423-446`
**ISSUE:** Error strings are load-bearing control flow (`error.startswith("login revoked")`, `refreshable()`, and per the test docstring "rank() keys on these strings"), while the network path reduces exceptions to `type(e).__name__` and drops the message.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Two failures from one root cause. (1) Fragility: revocation classification depends on a server-supplied string surviving both a copy edit by the author and a change upstream (OpenAI returns several codes — `token_revoked`, `token_invalidated`, 403 variants). Any mismatch silently degrades to `active-usage-unknown` → `no-switch` forever, which is the pre-fix outage the change was meant to end; nothing raises, because the same literal is duplicated in `_auto_tick`, `rank()` and the tests, so the tests pass in lockstep with a wrong assumption (tautological coverage). (2) Operability: `error == "OSError"` (asserted in the new test) discards `"no route"`, so a hub page / cron log shows an exception class name with zero detail, and a DNS failure is indistinguishable from a proxy reset or an auth problem.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Give `Usage` a structured discriminator (e.g. `error_kind: Literal["revoked","expired","http","network"]` set once in `fetch_usage` from the HTTP code + `error.code`) and keep `str(e)` in the human-readable `error`; have `revoked()`/`refreshable()`/`rank()` read `error_kind`.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py:327-330` (`credits_unlimited`) with `:249-253`
**ISSUE:** `credits_unlimited=bool(credits.get("unlimited"))` coerces an unvalidated API field, and the bypass is honored only inside `on_credits_ok()`.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The same function type-checks its other network field (`isinstance(window.get("used_percent"), (int, float))`) but not this one — so a string from the API (`"unlimited": "false"`, `"0"`, or `""`-vs-`"0"` variants) coerces to `True`, and with `credits_balance=None` the account passes `on_credits_ok()` permanently: the `MIN_CREDITS` floor is silently skipped, that account is never a failover candidate, and the whole point of the balance check is disabled by one truthy key in a JSON response. Conversely, because `unlimited` only exists in `on_credits_ok()`, every other balance-derived decision still sees `None` as zero — notably `reserve_spent(live_usage, ...)` in `_auto_tick` and the `list --json` balance column — so a genuinely unlimited account with no numeric balance reads as an exhausted reserve and gets abandoned. One flag, two inconsistent readings.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Validate strictly (`credits.get("unlimited") is True`) and apply the same predicate wherever a balance floor is evaluated (centralize "credits effectively available" in one method instead of only `on_credits_ok`).

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** `scripts/hub-status-push.py:127-137`
**ISSUE:** `d[half] is None` conflates "this half failed to collect" with "this host has no such half", so the new `prev_known` guard withholds correct data on hub-read failure and emits a nonzero exit for a normal condition.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A machine that legitimately runs only Claude (codex not installed → `d["codex"]` is always None) now hits the new `not prev_known and ...partial` branch on *every* transient `/api/db` failure: its fresh, complete-for-that-host document is not sent and `rc = 1`, so cron/monitoring pages on a data-loss alarm whenever the hub read blips — even though no data was lost and nothing about the host is stale. Worse, when the read succeeds those hosts publish normally, so the failure mode appears only during outages and is invisible in the tests (the diff adds no test for the `prev_known` path). The same conflation leaks into the carry-over loop: if `old.get(half)` is a *falsy* valid value (`{}`, `0`) it is skipped and the good value is overwritten with null — `or {}` on `prev.get(...)` shows the author already assumes falsy-means-absent here.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Distinguish per-half collection failure from per-half absence (use `d["errors"]`/a per-half marker rather than `None`) and only suppress the push for a half that actually failed; keep the good half. Downgrade the "previous unknown" case to a warning with a distinct exit code if it shouldn't page.

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** `tests/test_session_scan.py:158-173`
**ISSUE:** The new test replacing the "passed for a full scan too" one still cannot fail for the reason it claims — it proves early exit only if the implementation happens to catch exceptions narrowly.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `AssertionError` is used as the oracle, but the only assertion is `is not None`. If `latest_session_activity_ts` wraps its walk in `except Exception` (a normal defensive pattern for scanning another process's transcript directory — a *concurrent* scan target whose dirs are deleted mid-walk is precisely why such a handler exists), the raised `AssertionError` is swallowed, the value found in the first directory is returned, and the test passes with the early exit removed — the exact blind spot the docstring says it fixes. It also never asserts the returned value equals the expected mtime, so a wrong-timestamp return also passes, and `monkeypatch.setattr(mod.os, "walk", ...)` mutates the process-global `os` module (likewise `codex.urllib.request.urlopen` in `tests/test_codex.py:423-446`), hijacking every other in-process caller during the test.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Record instead of raise: have the fake `walk` append visited dirs to a list and assert `visited == [str(first)]`, plus assert the returned value equals the expected mtime — that fails on a full scan regardless of the implementation's exception handling.

## Validation summary

Reviewed only the provided diff. The four fixes behave as intended:

| Fix | Verdict |
|-----|--------|
| **Hub `stale` guard** | Non-dict `old["stale"]` no longer blows up on `.get(half)`; dict path unchanged. |
| **Window `used()` helper** | `used_percent == 0` is no longer treated as missing (`0 or -1`); comparisons use the same `-1.0` sentinel on both sides. |
| **401 + JWT `exp`** | Revoked checked first; “expired / refreshable” only when `exp <= time.time()`; other 401s get a distinct non-refreshable message (matches new tests). |
| **Unlimited credits sort** | `key = -inf` for unlimited sorts before any finite balance under ascending `sorted()`. |

**NO ISSUES FOUND.**

---

### Deliberate tradeoffs (not defects in this pass)

These follow from the comment that only JWT `exp` defines “expired,” not the 401 body:

- **Clock skew** — Server rejects a token while local `exp` is still in the future → classified as “account refused,” not refreshable.
- **No `exp` claim** — Same: any 401 stays non-refreshable even if the body says `token_expired`.

Those are consistency choices for ranking, not logic errors in the diff.

### Weak edges (below reporting bar)

- `was.get(half) or old.get("at")` would treat timestamp `0` as missing (unlikely for real timestamps).
- `def used` redefined every loop iteration (noise only).
- Tie-breaking on equal `used_percent` still keeps the first window (unchanged behavior).

Nothing here contradicts the stated goals of this validation pass.

###   PROVIDER: AGY

### Adversarial Code Review

---

### Finding 1
**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py:365`  
**ISSUE:** Expired access token detection relies solely on local client clock comparison instead of the authoritative server response body, causing minor clock skew to falsely classify active accounts as refused/banned.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:**  
When an access token expires on the auth server, the API returns HTTP 401 with `{"error": {"code": "token_expired"}}`. If the client machine's clock is even slightly behind the auth server (e.g. 5–30 seconds of NTP drift), `exp <= time.time()` evaluates to `False`. Line 370 then triggers: `kind = "http 401 (token still valid — account refused)"`. Because `usage.refreshable()` checks `error.startswith("token expired")`, it returns `False`. In `rank()`, any account where `life is None and not usage.refreshable()` is dropped from candidates (`continue`), permanently locking out healthy accounts with expired access tokens from being refreshed or rotated.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:**  
Check `if e.code == 401 and ("token_expired" in body or _access_token_expired(tokens.get("access_token"))):` or allow a clock-skew grace window in `_access_token_expired` (`exp <= time.time() + 60`).

---

### Finding 2
**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py:365`  
**ISSUE:** Deactivated or banned accounts whose tokens have passed expiration are misclassified as healthy refreshable accounts.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:**  
When an account is deactivated or banned (`account_deactivated`), its cached access token will naturally expire after sitting idle (`exp <= time.time()` becomes `True`). When queried, OpenAI answers with HTTP 401 and `{"error": {"code": "account_deactivated"}}`. Because `fetch_usage` tests `_access_token_expired` without inspecting whether `body` indicates account deactivation, line 365 matches and labels it `"token expired — refreshes on the next switch to it"`. As a result, `usage.refreshable()` returns `True`, and `rank()` retains the deactivated account as a switch target, causing the daemon to repeatedly switch to and stall on a deactivated account.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:**  
Check for account deactivation in `body` before checking token expiration:
```python
if e.code == 401 and "account_deactivated" in body:
    kind = "http 401 (account deactivated)"
elif e.code == 401 and ("token_expired" in body or _access_token_expired(tokens.get("access_token"))):
    kind = "token expired — refreshes on the next switch to it"
```

---

### Finding 3
**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py:302-308`  
**ISSUE:** Window comparison in `parse_usage` classifies malformed or zero-length windows as weekly windows.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:**  
If a window in the API payload has `limit_window_seconds: 0`, `None`, or missing, `length = window.get("limit_window_seconds") or 0` evaluates to `0`. Line 302 (`if length and length <= SHORT_WINDOW_MAX_S:`) evaluates to `False`. The code then falls into `elif weekly is None or used(window) > used(weekly):`. If a genuine weekly window exists (e.g. 7 days with 20% usage) and a secondary malformed/zero-length window has 50% usage, `weekly` is overwritten with the invalid window. This corrupts `weekly_pct` and `weekly_reset_at`.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:**  
Explicitly require `length > SHORT_WINDOW_MAX_S` before treating a window as weekly:
```python
if length and length <= SHORT_WINDOW_MAX_S:
    if short is None or used(window) > used(short):
        short = window
elif length > SHORT_WINDOW_MAX_S:
    if weekly is None or used(window) > used(weekly):
        weekly = window
```

---

### Finding 4
**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** `scripts/hub-status-push.py:126-138`  
**ISSUE:** Unchecked document body structure causes `AttributeError` when remote DB contains non-dict status documents.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:**  
`previous_docs(token)` returns `{d["id"]: d["body"] for ... if d.get("body")}`. If any document body in the hub DB was stored as a non-dict (e.g. string `"offline"`, list, or primitive), line 126 sets `old = prev.get(d["host"]) or {}` (which retains the non-dict truthy value). While line 142 guards `old.get("stale")`, line 138 unconditionally calls `old.get(half)`. This raises `AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'get'`, crashing `main()` and halting status updates for all remaining targets in the loop.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:**  
Ensure `old` is guarded as a dictionary on retrieval:
```python
old = prev.get(d["host"]) if isinstance(prev.get(d["host"]), dict) else {}
```

---

### Finding 5
**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py:550-557`  
**ISSUE:** Tying unlimited accounts with `-inf` in credits mode causes unnecessary switches away from an already active unlimited account.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:**  
When entering credits mode without an existing pin (`pinned is None`), if multiple accounts have `credits_unlimited = True`, each receives key `-float("inf")`. Python's `sorted()` is stable. In `_auto_tick`, `usages` is built by fetching `others` first and inserting `usages[current] = live_usage` last. Consequently, another unlimited account precedes `current` in `funded`, choosing `target = funded[0] != current`. The switcher executes an unnecessary account switch away from an already unlimited active account, killing running sessions and restarting the daemon contrary to the stated goal of minimizing flap.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:**  
Prioritize keeping `current` when it is funded with unlimited credits:
```python
if live_usage.on_credits_ok() and live_usage.credits_unlimited:
    target, mode = current, "credits"
else:
    target, mode = funded[0], "credits"
```

---

### Finding 6
**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** `src/claude_swap/codex.py:299-301`  
**ISSUE:** `isinstance(v, (int, float))` treats booleans as numeric floats due to Python's `bool` subclassing `int`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:**  
In Python, `isinstance(False, (int, float))` is `True` (`float(False) == 0.0`) and `isinstance(True, (int, float))` is `True` (`float(True) == 1.0`). If an upstream API response contains a boolean value for `"used_percent"` (e.g. `false`), `used(w)` returns `0.0` (interpreted as 0% usage) rather than `-1.0` (unspecified/invalid).  
**SUGGESTED FIX:**  
Add a boolean guard:
```python
def used(w: dict) -> float:
    v = w.get("used_percent")
    return float(v) if isinstance(v, (int, float)) and not isinstance(v, bool) else -1.0
```

###############################################################
###   PROVIDER: OPENROUTER-4
###############################################################

**1**  
**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:line ≈ 380 (function `_access_token_expired`)  
**ISSUE:** `_access_token_expired` can raise an exception when the JWT is malformed, `None`, or missing the `exp` claim, which propagates out of the `fetch_usage` 401‑handling block and aborts the whole call.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A corrupted or tampered access token (e.g., supplied by a compromised client) will crash the usage‑fetching service, causing denial‑of‑service for the whole CLI session.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Guard the claim extraction:

```python
def _access_token_expired(token: str | None) -> bool:
    try:
        exp = _jwt_claims(token or "").get("exp")
    except Exception:          # any decoding error
        return False            # treat as “not expired”
    return isinstance(exp, (int, float)) and exp <= time.time()
```

---

**2**  
**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL  
**CONFIDENCE:** high  
**FILE:** scripts/hub-status-push.py:line ≈ 145 (the `except Exception as e` block)  
**ISSUE:** The blanket `except Exception` swallows *all* exceptions, including `KeyboardInterrupt`, `SystemExit`, and unexpected runtime errors, silently skipping the remaining machines.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** An operator pressing Ctrl‑C or a bug raising `MemoryError` will be hidden, leaving the script appearing to succeed while half the hubs remain uninformed.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Catch only anticipated errors (e.g., `IOError`, `requests.RequestException`) and re‑raise or log critical ones:

```python
except (IOError, requests.RequestException) as e:
    log.error("Failed to push status for %s: %s", token, e)
    # continue with other machines
except Exception:
    raise  # let unexpected exceptions surface
```

---

**3**  
**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:line ≈ 549 (sorting key in `_auto_tick`)  
**ISSUE:** The key `-(float("inf") if usages[e].credits_unlimited else ...)` yields `-inf` for every unlimited account. When **multiple** unlimited accounts exist, their relative order is undefined, leading to nondeterministic selection of the “to” account.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** In environments with several unlimited‑credit users, the auto‑tick may flip between them across runs, causing flaky behavior and unpredictable credit consumption.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add a secondary deterministic tie‑breaker (e.g., email) to the key:

```python
key=lambda e: (
    -float("inf") if usages[e].credits_unlimited else -(usages[e].credits_balance or 0.0),
    e  # alphabetical tie‑break
)
```

**4**  
**SEVERITY:** WARNING  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:line ≈ 384 (401 handling in `fetch_usage`)  
**ISSUE:** The logic treats any 401 where the JWT’s `exp` claim is in the past as “token expired” and assumes the token is still a valid candidate for ranking. It ignores other 401 reasons (e.g., account disabled) that may have a non‑expired token.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** A disabled or revoked account with a still‑valid JWT will be mistakenly considered “refreshable”, causing the ranker to schedule switches to a dead account.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Distinguish the two cases explicitly:

```python
elif e.code == 401:
    if _access_token_expired(tokens.get("access_token")):
        kind = "token expired — refreshes on the next switch to it"
    else:
        kind = "http 401 (token still valid — account refused)"
```

(Already present, but ensure the `elif e.code == 401` branch does **not** fall through to the generic case.)

**5**  
**SEVERITY:** INFO  
**CONFIDENCE:** medium  
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:line ≈ 298 (function `parse_usage`)  
**ISSUE:** The inner helper `used` converts only `int`/`float` values to `float`; string representations of numbers (e.g., `"45.2"`) are treated as missing and yield `-1.0`.  
**ATTACK VECTOR:** If the upstream API returns numeric strings, windows will be mis‑ranked, possibly selecting a less‑used window and causing sub‑optimal throttling.  
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Accept numeric strings:

```python
def used(w: dict) -> float:
    v = w.get("used_percent")
    try:
        return float(v)
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return -1.0
```

###############################################################
###   PROVIDER: QWEN
###############################################################

Read-only adversarial pass over the four fixes. Verdict: the `used()` window comparison and the hub `stale` guard's *intent* are correct, but the JWT-exp change introduces a new crash-on-error-path and swaps one misclassification for a worse one.

---

**SEVERITY:** CRITICAL
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:365 (and the helper at :378)
**ISSUE:** `_access_token_expired()` is called *inside* the `except urllib.error.HTTPError` handler with no error handling of its own — a `_jwt_claims`/`tokens` failure there aborts the entire error path.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Python does not let a sibling `except Exception` clause (the one that returns `Usage(..., error=type(e).__name__)`) catch an exception raised *inside* the `HTTPError` handler. So an access token that is opaque / truncated / hand-edited in the auth file / not a JWT, or `tokens` not being a dict, turns a recoverable "this account 401'd" report into an exception that propagates out of `fetch_usage` → `rank`/`_auto_tick`. One malformed auth file then kills the whole tick for *every* account, so nobody gets switched and the user rides 100% quota. The old `elif e.code == 401:` branch could not fail; the fix added a new failure mode to the failure path. Note also the annotation `token: str | None` is a promise that `None` reaches `_jwt_claims`, and nothing in the artifact shows `_jwt_claims` tolerates it.
**SUGGESTED FIX:**
```python
def _access_token_expired(token: str | None) -> bool:
    try:
        exp = _jwt_claims(token).get("exp")
    except Exception:
        return False
    return isinstance(exp, (int, float)) and not isinstance(exp, bool) and exp <= time.time()
```

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:365-370
**ISSUE:** The expired/revoked split now keys *only* on the local JWT `exp` and throws away the server's error code, which is already in hand (`body`) — so the common non-expired-looking 401 lands in the *permanent* bucket.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Three production cases all mislabel as `"http 401 (token still valid — account refused)"`: (a) `exp` absent or a string, (b) a non-JWT/opaque access token, (c) the machine's clock running a few seconds behind the server's, so `exp <= time.time()` is False while the server already rejected it. `body` is right there in scope — the author's own test fixture at tests/test_codex.py:441 sends `{"error":{"code":"token_expired"}}` and the new code never reads it. The consequence is asymmetric and is exactly what the fix was trying to prevent, only inverted: `refreshable()` is False → rank treats a merely-expired account as refused/dead and drops it from rotation, and it stays dropped until the classification changes. The message itself also asserts something never verified ("token still valid").
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Use the body as the primary signal and `exp` as a fallback, and add skew grace:
`elif e.code == 401 and ("token_expired" in body or "invalid_token" in body or _access_token_expired(...)):` … and in the helper `exp <= time.time() + 60`. Reword the fallthrough to `f"http 401 ({body code or 'unknown'})"` instead of claiming validity.

---

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:552
**ISSUE:** `-inf` for every unlimited account makes the credits ordering a *clamp*, not a ranking — all unlimited candidates tie and the winner falls back to dict insertion order.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** "unlimited credits" does not mean unlimited *usage*; the 5-hour/weekly windows still bind (that's the invariant `_auto_tick` and `parse_usage` exist to enforce). With two or more unlimited accounts, `sorted` is stable over `usages` — which comes from an on-disk account enumeration — so the selected target is the enumeration order, not the least-loaded account. Any change in that order (or a tick where `current` is itself an unlimited, healthy account with room left) produces an unnecessary switch/logout-thrash, and the chosen unlimited account can be the one whose windows are already nearly full while funded accounts idle at 0%. Note this ordering ignores both `primary_pct`/`weekly_pct` and `current`, unlike the window path in the same function.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Keep unlimited first but give it a real tie-break and hysteresis: sort by `(not credits_unlimited, -balance_or_inf, most_used_window_pct)` and short-circuit before the sort when `current` is in `funded` and still below the tick's own threshold.

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** scripts/hub-status-push.py:142
**ISSUE:** The dict guard is applied to the read side (`old["stale"]`) but not the write side (`d["stale"]`), and the `or` chain still treats a timestamp as a truthiness test.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `d.setdefault("stale", {})[half] = ...` only inserts `{}` when the key is *missing*. If `d` is seeded from `old` anywhere earlier in `main()` (the `d[half] = old[half]` carry-over on the line above shows the two are conflated), a `d["stale"]` that is a list/str — i.e. a payload written by an older or different pusher — makes this line raise `TypeError`/`AttributeError` and abort the whole push loop. The guard also masks rather than fixes the shape mismatch: `was` becomes `{}` silently, so the half's stale clock is reset to `old["at"]` on every run and it looks "one minute old forever" — the precise bug the comment says the line prevents. Same for `was.get(half) or old.get("at")`: a `0`/`None` in either slot silently restarts the age clock instead of being flagged.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Guard both sides symmetrically: `st = d.get("stale") if isinstance(d.get("stale"), dict) else {}; st[half] = ...; d["stale"] = st`, and use an explicit sentinel (`v = was.get(half); ... if v is not None else old.get("at")`) rather than truthiness.

**SEVERITY:** WARNING
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py:519-534
**ISSUE:** The `used()` rewrite has no test for the case it was written to fix — `used_percent: 0`.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** The old code's defect was `short.get("used_percent") or -1`, which turned a legitimate `0%` window into `-1` and let a *missing* percentage (also `-1`) or any later window beat it. The new `used()` returns `0.0` correctly, but every test added in this pass exercises `reset_at` ordering, 401 classification, and unlimited credits. Nothing pins `0 > -1`. A refactor back to `or -1`, or a change of the invalid sentinel from `-1` to `0`, keeps all shipped tests green while reintroducing the bug that made a fresh window lose to an unknown one — a silent data-integrity regression in the number that drives switching.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Add the missing pair, e.g. `{"primary_window": {"used_percent": 0, "limit_window_seconds": 18000}, "secondary_window": {"limit_window_seconds": 604800}}` and assert `short_pct == 0`, plus one window with `used_percent` absent to prove it loses to the `0`.

**SEVERITY:** INFO
**CONFIDENCE:** high
**FILE:** tests/test_codex.py:530-534
**ISSUE:** The unlimited-credits test asserts only the target email; the actual switch is stubbed away and never verified, and the `fetch_usage` stub can raise `KeyError` instead of failing the assertion.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** `monkeypatch.setattr(codex.subprocess, "Popen", lambda *a, **kw: None)` makes the side effect free and unrecorded, so the test passes even if `auto_tick` selects `u @x.com` but never launches it, or launches it with the wrong env/email. Likewise `by[codex.email_of(auth)]` raises `KeyError` for any account present in `env` but absent from `by` (e.g. leftovers from a previous test's `_login`), producing an error that looks like a real failure rather than a fixture gap.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Capture the calls: `calls = []; monkeypatch.setattr(codex.subprocess, "Popen", lambda *a, **kw: calls.append(a))` and assert the chosen email appears in `calls`; make the stub total (`by.get(...)` with an explicit assertion, or `usage_by_email.get`).

**SEVERITY:** INFO
**CONFIDENCE:** medium
**FILE:** src/claude_swap/codex.py:299-302
**ISSUE:** `used()` is a nested `def` recompiled on every loop iteration and repurposes the name `used`, which the replaced code held as a number.
**ATTACK VECTOR:** Purely a maintenance trap: any later line in `parse_usage` that reads `used` expecting the current window's percentage now silently gets a function object (`used < 0` becomes a `TypeError`, `if used:` is always True). Also, `isinstance(v, (int, float))` admits `bool` and `NaN` — `used_percent: true` reads as `1.0`, and a `NaN` (Python's `json` accepts bare `NaN`) can never win a `>` comparison, so the whole window list collapses to "first one wins", reintroducing the overwrite behaviour the comment claims to have removed.
**SUGGESTED FIX:** Hoist it to a module-level `_used_pct(w)` and narrow the guard to `isinstance(v, (int, float)) and not isinstance(v, bool) and v == v`.
